# Spec: Data foundation (Phase 2)

**Status:** Draft  ·  **Issue:** #8  ·  **Related:** [ADR 0003](../decisions/0003-data-adapters-local-first.md), [ADR 0004](../decisions/0004-tooling-adopt-avoid.md), [ADR 0005](../decisions/0005-objective-benchmark-stop-criteria.md), [ADR 0006](../decisions/0006-universe-and-cadence.md), [ADR 0020](../decisions/0020-fred-risk-free-rate.md) (the `rates` table, amendment 2026-10-11), [roadmap Phase 2](../roadmap.md)

## Problem & why now

Nothing in Phases 3–6 can be trusted unless the data underneath answers one question correctly: *what was knowable at time T?* This spec builds that layer: a point-in-time store, a security master, a trading calendar, the adapters that feed them from free sources, a daily job, and the page that shows whether the data is healthy. It is the first code in the project.

## Users & usage

- **The owner**, once a day, glances at the data-health page. Once, at the start, the owner runs a recording script with their own keys to produce the scrubbed fixtures the tests use, and records the source-terms facts.
- **The scheduler**, once per trading day after the close, runs `tradepartner ingest`.
- **Phase 3 code** reads through the as-of API and never touches adapters directly.
- **Tests**, on every PR, run against the fixture universe with the network disabled.

## Definitions

- **T** is always a tz-aware UTC timestamp. A rebalance *date* maps to T = that session's close via the calendar. Never a bare date.
- **`known_at`** is when a fact became knowable to a market participant. **`ingested_at`** is when we stored it. `known_at ≤ ingested_at` always.
- **Revision**: a later value for the same key is a **new row** with its own `known_at`; rows are never updated in place. "Latest as of T" is the row with the greatest `known_at ≤ T`. One exception by design: `statement_facts` (amendment 2026-10-03, #660) holds the **first** vintage of each key only and never a later one, so it has no revisions to read; the amendment says why. `filing_events` (amendment 2026-10-09, #1358) is keyed by the filing's accession, which EDGAR never reuses, so it has no revisions either: an 8-K/A is another accession and another row. A **retraction** (amendment 2026-10-04, #859) is a revision of a `securities` or `listings` key with `retracted = TRUE`: from its `known_at` on, the key has no row; before it, the old row stands.
- **Provenance** on every row: `filing` (`known_at` = SEC acceptance timestamp), `filing_name` (amendment 2026-10-12, #1597: a `listings` ticker read from a filing's file names, its XBRL instance or a Form 25 exhibit, rather than a tagged fact; `known_at` = that filing's acceptance, as `filing`), `bar` (session close), `action` (rule below), `release` (amendment 2026-10-11, #1498: a published statistical release whose source gives a release date and no time; `known_at` = the close of the first XNYS session strictly after that date), `snapshot` (current-only endpoint; `known_at` = fetch time), or `snapshot_static` (a snapshot attribute the config explicitly allows to apply before its fetch time, e.g. a company name or a pre-2019 exchange; counted and shown on the health page). A `snapshot_static` value is stored only in a row whose `known_at` is that snapshot's fetch time; only the row's `valid_from` may predate it, and as-of reads still see the row only once `known_at <= T` (#35).
- **Raw payload**: the bytes a source returns (JSON, SGML header text, or a downloaded filing file). Fetch functions return raw payloads; parsers turn raw payloads into records. Only parsers are unit-tested; fixtures are recorded raw payloads.

## Requirements

1. **Store.** A single DuckDB file, path from config. Every fact table has `known_at`, `ingested_at`, `source`, `provenance`. Prices raw OHLCV; corporate actions separate; adjusted series computed at read time as of T. Ingest holds the file read-write only while committing a chunk and releases it between chunks; every other process uses **short-lived `read_only=True`** connections and shows a "store busy" state when locked.
2. **Calendar.** Wrapper over `exchange_calendars` XNYS: is-session, next/previous session, open and close timestamps, last session of month, last *completed* session as of a timestamp, sessions in a calendar-month window, half-day detection. No weekday logic. The calendar-month window is exact: for `end_session` and `months`, it is every session `s` with `end_session − relativedelta(months=months) < s ≤ end_session`.
3. **Security master.** A `securities` row is created from the **earliest issuer filing per CIK** in EDGAR's filing index (`known_at` = that filing's acceptance; issuer form types from `master.issuer_forms`, so reporting owners filing Forms 3/4/5 and 13D/G never create rows). The index is scanned over **full history regardless of `--since`**, so `securities.known_at` is never too late. Companies therefore exist at historical T and delisted names exist at all. Ticker ranges, exchange, SIC, type and delisting come from filings where filings supply them and from `snapshot_static` attributes where they do not (pre-~2019 ticker and exchange); those count only from their fetch time, see open question 1. *Amended 2026-10-12 (#1597): a 10-K's XBRL instance file name and a Nasdaq Form 25's exhibit file name supply pre-2019 ticker and exchange rows known at those filings' acceptance (`provenance = filing_name`, behind `edgar.filename_listings`); the amendment under Data / interfaces has the rules.* Keyed by `security_id`; adapters never resolve by bare ticker. Benchmarks (SPY, MTUM) are **seeded from config**, not derived from EDGAR (MTUM shares its trust's CIK with other funds). A stored EDGAR master row that the current rules no longer derive is withdrawn by a retraction known at the correcting run, never deleted (amendment 2026-10-04, #859).
4. **Delistings.** Forms **25 and 25-NSE** are both ingested as filing rows (`known_at` = acceptance) that name a class title and exchange. **Transfer status and listing end are never stored; they are derived at read time** from rows with `known_at ≤ T`: a listing's end is its last bar session known at T after the filing; the filing is a **transfer** at T only if a new listing row for the same security exists with `known_at ≤ T` and `valid_from` within `master.transfer_window_sessions` of the filing date. Between the filing and the new listing becoming known, the security is treated as delisted at T (conservative), and becomes a transfer once the new listing is known. `effective_on` is stored for reference (filing date + 10 days per Rule 12d2-2 unless stated) and is the anchor of req 11's *no bars after a listing's end* rule (amendment #822); it never sets a listing's end.
5. **Corporate actions.** `PriceSource.corporate_actions()` supplies splits and dividends. **First-seen** row: `known_at` = source announcement time if present, else the close of the session before ex-date. The announcement time is stored as `announced_at` (NULL when the source gives none), so the rule is checkable: `known_at == min(announced_at, proxy)` if set, else exactly the proxy; an earlier stamp without an announcement is look-ahead and refused (#83). A later announcement is capped at the proxy: raw bars from the ex-date on already reflect the action, and it is public before its ex-date. Revisions carry `announced_at` forward unchanged; it is never a filter for T. A source that gives only an announcement **date** is stamped at the close of the first session strictly after that date (the announcement may follow that day's close). **Any later row that differs** for the same action: `known_at = ingested_at`. Never back-dated on revision. An action's identity is (security, source action id) when the source gives a stable id, else (security, type, ex-date) (#108). So a **re-dated** action (same id, new ex-date) is a revision: the old ex-date applies until its `known_at`, the new one after, never both. A **cancelled** revision (`cancelled = TRUE`, stamped at `ingested_at`) withdraws the event from its `known_at` on. It also retires the old key of a re-date from a source with no id: ingest writes a cancel for the old key plus a **replacement** row for the new one, and the replacement is stamped `known_at = ingested_at` (it revises a known event, so it never takes a first-seen proxy stamp). The same applies when an event first stored without an id later arrives with one: cancel the id-less key, write the id row as a replacement. An id row may not share (security, type, ex-date) with a live id-less row. The store holds one row per identity per `known_at`. As-of reads take the latest revision per identity first, then apply the ex-date and cancel filters.
6. **`PriceSource`** (`bars`, `corporate_actions`) with **fixture** and **Alpaca** adapters. **`FilingSource`** (filing index, company facts, filing headers, cover pages, Form 25/25-NSE, companies snapshot) with **fixture** and **EDGAR** adapters. Real adapters = thin raw-fetch client (`httpx` for EDGAR JSON/SGML/files, `alpaca-py` for Alpaca) + pure parsers; `edgartools` (pinned, cache dir from config) is used only to parse downloaded cover-page iXBRL.
7. **`Broker` interface** and in-memory **fake broker** (submit, cancel, positions, fills; duplicate client order IDs rejected). No risk logic (Phase 4).
8. **As-of read API.** `prices_as_of`, `adjusted_prices_as_of(T, include_dividends=False)` (splits only by default; dividends for Phase 3 total return), `facts_as_of`, `securities_as_of`, `listings_as_of`, `universe_as_of`, `survivorship_gap`, `statement_facts_as_of` (#660), and `filing_events_as_of(T, forms, items)` (#1358; its contract is under "Amendment 2026-10-09 (#1358)"). All take tz-aware T and return only rows with `known_at ≤ T`, latest revision as of T (`statement_facts_as_of` has one row per key by construction, so "latest" and "first" coincide there). **Level rules** (ADR 0006 rules 4 and 8) use the **raw close at T** and adjust shares only for splits with **ex-date ≤ T**, regardless of when the split became known.
9. **Ingest.** `tradepartner ingest [--source alpaca|edgar|all] [--backfill --since DATE] [--dry-run]`. Per-source atomic chunks (one calendar month per source); a failure commits nothing from that chunk, earlier chunks stand; `--backfill` resumes from the last committed chunk. Idempotent for a completed session. Halts non-zero on stale or failed source, with only the `ingestion_runs` row written for that source. Retries on lock for `store.lock_retry_seconds`, then exits non-zero. Amendment #831 (holes): `--backfill --since DATE --source alpaca --fill-holes [--dry-run]` refetches, in the months the backfill for that `--since` has committed, each (security, month) whose security the code at the run counts as fetched in the month and that has no bar known at the run (a corrected listing end or a new listing row makes one; resume never revisits a committed month). The dry run lists them, fetches nothing and needs no Alpaca secret. The real run fetches only those names and the reference symbol, one month per chunk with req 10's staleness over the stored and fetched bars together and the existing write rules (a first-seen bar keeps the Provenance timing `known_at`, its session's close, and `ingested_at` is the fetch; absence withdraws nothing), and writes one run row per month fetched: mode `holes`, status `filled` (never `ok`, so never a fresh ingest), a cursor `holes;since=…;through=…` that resume ignores. Amendment #876: a hole is listed or fetched only when the store's price resolver at the run assigns the security some session of the month under a ticker that is an Alpaca symbol (otherwise no fetched bar can land on it); the others are counted per reason and not fetched. `--security ID[,ID…]` (repeatable) limits the holes to the named securities; a named id with no hole fetched is listed with the reason, not an error. Amendment #891 (rename gaps): a ticker that leads to the security (the #843 rename lead, or the #974 first-span lead) counts as assigning it for #876, and a (security, month) with bars known at the run is also a hole when the lead assigns the security a session of the month, under an Alpaca symbol, with no bar known at the run and none later on a session the lead assigns it (a rename gap, from the old symbol's last bar to the new span, that starts or ends inside a month: FB→META's June and July 2022; a halt the old symbol traded after is none; the partial month at a first-span lead's end, #974, is one too). The whole month is refetched by the rules above; the lead takes a session only where the security has no row of its own, so no session is stored twice and every bar keeps its session-close `known_at`. A partial month with no lead is no hole. *Amended 2026-10-12 (#1550, #1572): a daily Alpaca run also fetches every XNYS session the store lacks before its expected one, up to `alpaca.max_catchup_sessions`, or is `stale` naming them and the backfill command; the amendment of 2026-10-12 (#1550) under Data / interfaces has the rule.*
10. **Staleness** is source-level: expected latest session = calendar's last completed session at run time minus `ingest.settle_delay_minutes`; stale if the reference symbol has no bar for it, or more than `ingest.max_missing_share` of listed names are missing it. Amendment #784 (owner decision, 2026-10-04): listed names are the benchmarks and the common names on one of `universe.exchanges` (OTC is not one; the SIP feed has no OTC bars). A listed common name whose every listing span live in the chunk window (the month in backfill, the expected session in a daily run, where only the current listing is read) is `snapshot_static`, and that has no bar at all in that window, is reported (the run message gives their count and up to 10 names) and left out of both sides of the `max_missing_share` share; benchmarks, and names with any filing-based span in the window, count as before. Then (dark names, same amendment): any other listed name with no bar counts only if it had a bar in the previous chunk window (the previous calendar month in backfill, the previous session in a daily run; bars ingested before this chunk began, nothing later; #796) or its first listing starts after that window. A first listing is the security's first listing on one of `universe.exchanges` (owner, #796 ii a): a move between those exchanges is a transfer, not a new listing, while an up-listing from OTC is a first listing (daily run; the backfill still dates a first listing from any exchange until #912). In backfill a new listing counts only when it starts no later than the window's first calendar day (on it, when that day is a session), since a name listed later in the month is not in the denominator; otherwise it is reported by cause the same way and not counted, so a name that goes dark counts once, in the chunk it disappears. Benchmarks always count, and when the store holds no bar at all in the previous window (the first chunk, or a daily run whose previous session was never ingested) every miss counts. *Dark share* (#796, owner option i a): a chunk is also stale when the names reported rather than counted (dark plus snapshot-only) are more than `ingest.max_dark_share` of its listed names, so a fault that drops a few names per chunk, each going dark under `max_missing_share`, still ends in a stale run. The default 1.0 is off until it is set from the backfill's measured baseline after T45b. What this gives up: before ~2019 almost every common name is `snapshot_static` (req 3), so in those backfill months the share check rests on the reference symbol, the benchmarks and the names with a bar or a filing-based span; a partial outage that returns no rows for static-only names, or for names already dark the chunk before, shows only in the message, and a month with no counted names has share 0. On an IEX-only feed (set by the owner in T3), `max_missing_share` and rule 6 thresholds are what the owner recorded, never silently relaxed. Amendment #1234 (owner decision, 2026-10-08): in a daily run, a listed name with no bar that is a *stale listing* by the survivorship gap's rule (ADR 0003 amendment #1199: its last traded bar known at the run is more than `gap.stale_listing_sessions` sessions before the reference symbol's last traded bar so read, on or before the expected session, so a lapse in ingests that leaves the whole store behind makes no name stale; a name with no bar ever is never one) is reported (count and up to 10 names) and left out of both sides of the `max_missing_share` share and of the `max_dark_share` share, before the snapshot-only and dark tests; benchmarks and the reference symbol never are. The rule is a read: a name that trades again counts again. The backfill's month check is unchanged (open question on #1234's PR).
11. **Health as code.** `tradepartner health [--check]` uses a pure `health.py` shared with the page: last successful ingest per source; coverage; gaps; survivorship gap and its three side categories; unclassifiable count; `snapshot_static` reliance count at the latest T; **delisted names count and list**; liquidity-rule and fill-price settings; integrity rules (non-null `known_at`; `known_at ≤ ingested_at`; bars only on sessions; no duplicate `(security_id, session, known_at)`; non-overlapping listings per security; no bars after a listing's end for delistings; guarded SIC default = charter, *dropped by amendment 2026-10-11 (#1540) with plan task T176*). `--check` exits non-zero on any failure. Amendment #822 (owner decision, 2026-10-04): a delisted listing's end is its last bar (req 4), so *no bars after a listing's end* fails only a bar after `effective_on` that comes back after more than `master.transfer_window_sessions` missing sessions (XNYS sessions strictly between two bars, the first gap counted from the last bar on or before `effective_on`, or from `effective_on` when there is none), and every later bar up to the security's next listing on an exchange (a NONE or OTC row does not end the check); a line that keeps trading past `effective_on` without such a gap passes. A delisting that took effect more than `master.transfer_window_sessions` sessions before the store's first bar therefore fails its line's bars on purpose: the store cannot show the line kept trading (a first bar inside that window still passes, per the gap rule above). *Non-overlapping listings* tolerates rows of the same ticker with the same `valid_from`, whatever their exchange tag or class-title wording (filer noise, one line), and, when testing a late Form 25, does not count a next row on exchange NONE or OTC as a second line (suspension, OTC quote, then a late Form 25, the normal Nasdaq sequence): the filing is tested against the next row on an exchange instead. Same-start rows of different tickers still fail. *Amendment #855 (owner decision, 2026-10-05):* unless the owner, after reviewing the pair, lists it in `universe.accepted_same_day_pairs` as `"<security_id>@<valid_from>"` (default empty; HACAR/HCACR, a security's first listing, is the case). An accepted pair is one line: a late Form 25 on its rows is still tested, against the next exchange line after that day, and `health` lists every accepted pair. Every other same-start pair still fails.
12. **Page.** Streamlit, rendering `health.py` output, read-only, with the busy state.
13. **Fixture universe** as CSV, seeded generator, content-equal regeneration. Required cases: delisting with truncated history (> N sessions before the Form 25 filing) and one within N; a **clean merger delisting** (last bar the session before filing; expected *not* missing); a **25-NSE**; a Form 25 on a **non-common class** (common survives); an **exchange transfer**, plus a probe T **between the Form 25 and the new listing's `known_at`** (expected: delisted at that T, transfer later); a **same-company ticker change**; a **ticker reused** by a different company; a **dual-class** company; a split; a split between a shares filing and T; a **split known before T with ex-date after T**; a **revised dividend**; a **split re-dated by source id**, a **split re-dated to an earlier ex-date with no source id** and a **cancelled dividend** (#108); a restated shares fact; a stale shares fact; an unclassifiable name; a `snapshot_static`-only pre-2019 listing; *(amendment 2026-10-12, #1597)* a pre-2019 survivor listed by its 10-K's instance prefix with no snapshot row, a Nasdaq delister whose prefix its Form 25 exhibit confirms with a successor on the same ticker, an NYSE delister with a single reading, a refused abbreviation prefix, a prefix rename, and a dual-class issuer whose prefix names one class; a holiday; a half day; SPY and MTUM as benchmarks with dividends. Generated CSVs are reviewed through the generator and excluded from the 400-line budget.
14. **Config** (pydantic settings). Every ADR 0006 threshold is a key with its default; `universe.exclude_sic_ranges` is guarded (*amended 2026-10-11, #1540: no longer guarded; default `[]` from plan task T176; see the amendment under Data / interfaces*). `universe.liquidity_rule_enabled` defaulted to `false` and `execution.fill_price` to `close` until the owner's T3 research set them; T3 (#84, 2026-09-25) set `liquidity_rule_enabled=true` (SIP history gives consolidated volume) and kept `fill_price=close` ([research](../research/2026-09-25-free-data-terms.md)). Secrets only from `.env`.
15. **`data-validator` agent**: read-only, runs `health --check` and the suite on the owner's real store, reports; computes nothing itself.
16. **As-filed statement facts** (amendment 2026-10-03, #660). Revenue, cost of revenue, gross profit, total assets and operating cash flow as each issuer **first filed** them, keyed by CIK and the fact's own period, `known_at` = the filing's acceptance, one vintage per period for ever. The rules are under "Data / interfaces" > "Amendment 2026-10-03 (#660)".
17. **Filing events** (amendment 2026-10-09, #1358). One row per 8-K (and 8-K/A) in the submissions record of every issuer CIK, with the filing's verbatim `items` string and its acceptance; `known_at` = the stamped acceptance, never the index's filing date; rebuildable from the submissions record; read as of T by `filing_events_as_of`. The rules, the coverage count and the clock caveat are under "Data / interfaces" > "Amendment 2026-10-09 (#1358)".
18. **Rates** (amendment 2026-10-11, #1498, [ADR 0020](../decisions/0020-fred-risk-free-rate.md)). One row per (series, observation date, value) of a published interest-rate series, the 3-month T-bill rate `DTB3` first, from FRED/ALFRED through a `RateSource` adapter; `known_at` = the close of the first XNYS session strictly after the row's vintage date (req 5's date-only rule); a later vintage with a different value is a revision row; read as of T by `rates_as_of`. The rules, the ingest, the health block and the terms are under "Data / interfaces" > "Amendment 2026-10-11 (#1498)".
19. **Borrow status** (amendment 2026-10-11, #1506, [ADR 0021](../decisions/0021-alpaca-assets-borrow-status-record.md)). One row per Alpaca `us_equity` asset per *change* of its tradability and borrow flags (`status`, `tradable`, `marginable`, `shortable`, `easy_to_borrow`, `borrow_status`, `fractionable`), from one unfiltered `GET /v2/assets` a day; `known_at` = the fetch time, `provenance = snapshot`; each snapshot's observation recorded on its `ingestion_runs` row; the recorder's files from 2026-10-11 imported first; read as of T by `borrow_status_as_of`. Data only: nothing on the order path, in the risk rules, the backtest or the paper books reads it, and the data vintage leaves it out. The rules are under "Data / interfaces" > "Amendment 2026-10-11 (#1506)".
20. **Spin-offs and name changes** (amendment 2026-10-11, #1506). `PriceSource.corporate_actions()` also supplies Alpaca's `spin_offs`, stored in `corporate_actions` as `action_type = spinoff` in the T64 convention (child, child shares per parent share, parent in `source_action_id`), and `PriceSource.symbol_changes()` its `name_changes`, stored in a new `symbol_changes` table; both stamped by the date-only rule of req 5 (the close of the first session strictly after the source's `process_date`), never earlier; the actions backfill starts at 2020-06-01 and coverage before it is unknown, not absent. Every existing reader of `corporate_actions` is byte-identical with the new rows present and pinned by test, except the one intended reader, `window.start`, which gains real rows to explain a residue on the paper path (the owner confirms the convention by open question 19); the data vintage leaves `spinoff` rows and the new table out. The rules are under the same amendment.

## Acceptance criteria (testable)

Timing and store
- [ ] Every fact table row: tz-aware UTC `known_at`, `ingested_at`, `known_at ≤ ingested_at`, non-null `source`, `provenance` in the allowed set (schema test; `health --check` rule).
- [ ] A bar for session S has `known_at` = XNYS close of S (normal day and half day).
- [ ] A bar re-fetched with a different close becomes a second row with `known_at = ingested_at`; `prices_as_of` returns each in its interval.
- [ ] First-seen corporate action without an announcement time: `known_at` = close before ex-date; a **revised** dividend becomes a new row with `known_at = ingested_at`, and `adjusted_prices_as_of(T, include_dividends=True)` uses the old amount before that and the new after.
- [ ] Backfilled 2018 split in 2026: `adjusted_prices_as_of(2019-01-31 close)` adjusts 2017 prices.
- [ ] Split known before T with ex-date after T: `universe_as_of(T)` cap uses unadjusted shares and raw close; rule 4 uses the raw close.
- [ ] Restated shares fact: `facts_as_of(T)` returns the earlier value between the two `known_at`, the later after; facts carry `as_of_date` and `class_member`.

Look-ahead
- [ ] **Truncation invariance** for every as-of function including `universe_as_of` and `survivorship_gap`: f(T) on the full fixture store equals f(T) on a store truncated to `known_at ≤ T`, for probe timestamps placed **just before and just after every distinct `known_at`** in the fixture.
- [ ] The broken adapter's violations (early `known_at`; pre-adjusted prices; ticker-based resolution; `known_at > ingested_at`; back-dated revision) are each caught by a named check.
- [ ] A bare date passed as T raises.
- [ ] An autouse test fixture makes `socket.connect` raise; every test passes with it on.

Security master and universe
- [ ] Reused ticker: prices by `security_id` return only that company's rows. Same-company ticker change: one `security_id`, two listing ranges.
- [ ] Span lag (#1314 item 2, rule 8), one fixture case each: (a) S's last bar on its T-span is more than `master.transfer_window_sessions` sessions before another company's span of T starts (D): S's span ends on the session after it, the ticker resolves to nobody there, and when T is the new issuer's first span its #974 lead fills from that day to D; (b) a gap of at most that many sessions, a later span of S's own company (`<cik>:<class>`, `<cik>@<date>`), or S's bars running on to D (the fail-safe before item 3): unchanged; (c) a span already ended by rule 7 before the hand-over day: its end is unchanged; (d) D's span a co-registrant or disputed claim against S, with a gap straddling D: unchanged; (e) a store hole before D: `--fill-holes` lists S's months from the hand-over day to D, a refetched bar of S's `asof` request is stored under S (never under the new issuer's lead) and moves the end, and an empty refetch stores nothing and is counted; (f) the run row counts the spans ended, and `repair-resolution --dry-run` lists S's bars on or after the hand-over day.
- [ ] First-span lead (#974): a security whose first assigned equity span starts after its first session has bars under that ticker from the later of its first session and the backfill start, each `known_at` its session's close; `resolve` is the same with and without the lead; a ticker another security's span covers inside the window leads to nothing, the whole window, and is counted; a split with an ex-date inside the window is stored for the security, one inside a #843 rename gap is not; `universe_as_of(T)` at a T before the span's listing is known excludes the name (`not_listed`) with the lead bars stored, and at a T after it passes rule 6 on them; `alpaca.first_span_lead=false` leaves every mapping as before.
- [ ] Earliest-filing rule: a fixture company whose only filings are historical has a `securities` row with `known_at` = its first filing's acceptance; `securities_as_of` at a T before any filing returns nothing for it.
- [ ] `snapshot_static` attributes apply before fetch time only for the columns config allows; `health` reports the reliance count. `snapshot_static` values are never written into a row with an earlier `known_at`; as-of reads and the truncation harness give such rows no exemption, so before its `known_at` the row is invisible (#35).
- [ ] Delistings: 25 and 25-NSE both end the named listing; a Form 25 on a preferred class leaves the common listed; a transfer within the window keeps the security in `universe_as_of` on the new exchange once the new listing is known, and shows it delisted at a T before that; the delisted fixture name is absent from `universe_as_of` after its last session and present in `securities_as_of` with dates.
- [ ] `universe_as_of(T)`: rules 1–8 in ADR order; dual-class cap summed over classes, both listed classes admitted; split-adjusted cap; stale shares excluded; output records enabled rules and the fill-price setting.
- [ ] `universe.liquidity_rule_enabled=false` (when set; the default is `true` since T3): rule 5 skipped and recorded; `health` shows it.
- [ ] Each `universe.*` key overridden one at a time changes membership as expected; AST check: `universe.py` and `gap.py` contain no numeric literals other than 0, 1, -1.
- [ ] `survivorship_gap(T)` matches hand-computed count and size share per the formula; clean merger delisting is *not* missing; the three side categories are reported separately.
- [x] #1199 (step 2, after the ADR 0003 amendment): a live name whose last traded bar is before W and more than `gap.stale_listing_sessions` sessions before T's session is in `SurvivorshipGap.stale_listings` (columns `security_id`, `last_bar`, `dark_sessions`) with that bar and the sessions dark, in neither M nor L, and in none of `unclassifiable`, `truncated_history` or `stale_shares`; one dark exactly that many sessions is `no_bar_at_t`; with the key below the sessions in W, a name dark more than that many sessions whose last bar is inside W is `no_bar_at_t`, not stale (no death becomes a survivor); the count is in XNYS sessions (a holiday week moves the verdict by the sessions it holds, not the days); a listing with no bar at all stays `no_bar_at_t`; a `delisted` listing takes the `truncated_tail` path unchanged, and a security whose current listing is stale but whose Form 25 row is filed inside W is in L and judged `truncated_tail`; a stale name with a new traded bar known at a later T is live again at that T (in M as `no_bar_at_t` when the bar is not T's session, else counted as priced); no look-ahead: a bar first known after T, or on a session after T's session, does not make the name live at T, and truncation invariance holds for `survivorship_gap` with the rule on; `gap.stale_listing_sessions` is frozen with `gap.*` and `survivorship_gap(T, settings)` reads only the settings it is given; health reports the category's count and list; `gap.py` still holds no numeric literal other than 0, 1 and -1.

Adapters and fixtures
- [ ] EDGAR parsers on recorded payloads: filing-derived records have `known_at` = acceptance and `provenance = filing`; snapshot records `provenance = snapshot` with recorded fetch time; SGML header yields SIC; cover page yields title, symbol, exchange; 25 and 25-NSE yield class title and exchange.
- [ ] Alpaca parsers on recorded payloads: raw closes, `known_at` per timing rule, actions with the proxy rule, resolution through the master.
- [ ] Scrub test: no fixture contains a key-shaped string, an email pattern, or a `User-Agent`-shaped string (pattern-based; no `.env` needed).
- [ ] No `.env`: package imports and all tests pass.

Ingest
- [ ] Idempotent re-run for a completed session: no fact rows change; `rows_added = 0`.
- [ ] Staleness on holiday, weekend, pre-close, half day: expected latest session is the last completed one (four tests).
- [ ] Stale reference symbol: non-zero exit, `status = stale`, no fact rows changed for that source.
- [ ] #784: an OTC common name with no bar is not counted, and a NYSE or NASDAQ common name with no bar still is (daily and backfill).
- [ ] #784: a `snapshot_static`-only common name with no bar in the window gives `ok`, named and counted in the run message; the same gap on a name with a filing-based span in the window, or on a benchmark, is `stale`; a static-only name with some bars follows the existing rule.
- [ ] #784: a name with no bar in this chunk or the previous one is reported, not counted; a name with a bar in the previous chunk and none now counts; a name first listed in this chunk with no bar counts (daily and backfill).
- [ ] #796: dark plus snapshot-only names above `ingest.max_dark_share` of the listed names make the chunk stale (daily and backfill); a bar of the previous window ingested after the chunk began does not make a name countable; a benchmark always counts; an up-listing from OTC this session with no bar counts (daily; backfill is #912).
- [ ] Mid-chunk failure: that chunk not committed, earlier chunks committed, `--backfill` resumes from `chunk_cursor`.
- [ ] Lock: with a short-lived reader that closes within `lock_retry_seconds`, ingest completes; with a writer that never closes, ingest exits non-zero after the retry window. The page shows "store busy" while a chunk commits.

Health and page
- [ ] `health --check` passes on the fixture store; fails naming the rule on an injected duplicate bar, overlapping listing, bar after a delisting end, or changed SIC default.
- [ ] `health --check` fails naming `session_holes` on an XNYS session in the `universe.min_history_months` window (from the calendar) whose `prices_daily` rows known at `t` are below `health.min_session_rows_share` of the previous session's count -- an empty session always -- with the session and its row count named; a store with every session's bars passes, and a session after the latest stored one is staleness, not a hole (#1575, #1550 part c).
- [ ] Headless render shows every `health.py` metric, including delisted names and `snapshot_static` reliance.

Real-store evidence (close-out PR, not unit tests)
- [ ] `universe_as_of` at a month-end **inside the backfill window** on the owner's real store is non-empty and includes at least one name later delisted.
- [ ] Five consecutive scheduled `ingestion_runs` rows with `status = ok`.

Statement facts (amendment 2026-10-03, #660)
- [ ] Schema: `statement_facts` has the four common columns, `provenance = filing` only and UNIQUE `(cik, fact_name, period_end, period_days)`; a second row for a stored key raises; `period_start` is NULL exactly when `period_days = 0` (`health --check` rule).
- [ ] Switch: with `edgar.statement_facts_enabled = false` (the default) an ingest run calls no `statement_facts`, makes no request for it, writes no cache for it and adds no row; `backfill` is byte-for-byte the same code path as before on that setting.
- [ ] Parser on a constructed companyfacts payload: a 10-K carrying the fiscal year and two comparative years of `Revenues` yields three keys on their own `(period_start, period_end)`, the two comparatives flagged; an entry with a misleading `fy` and no `frame` still lands on its dates; a form outside `edgar.statement_forms` (taken from the submissions record, not the entry) is ignored; a non-`USD` unit is skipped and counted; a 10-K/A that is the first carrier of a key is its vintage with `form = 10-K/A`; an entry naming a dimension raises.
- [ ] Precedence and conflict: a filing carrying `Revenues` and `RevenueFromContractWithCustomerExcludingAssessedTax` with different values stores the first with its `xbrl_tag`; the same tag twice for one period with two values stores nothing for that key from that filing, counts one conflict and lists it, and the next filing's clean value is the vintage with `comparative` set by the Periods rule (TRUE from a prior-period column, FALSE from a 10-K/A of the same period); more than `edgar.min_failed_filings` conflicting accessions of one form do **not** fail the chunk and write nothing to `failed_filings.json`.
- [ ] Co-registrant filters: an accession FSN extracted under another CIK, and one whose cached cover page names another entity, contribute no statement rows to this CIK.
- [ ] Derived gross profit: no `GrossProfit`, stored `revenue` and `cost_of_revenue` vintages from one accession and period → one `derived` row equal to their difference with that filing's `known_at` and accession; components from two filings, or one withheld → no row; a filed `GrossProfit` that is the first carrier → `reported`, nothing derived; a `GrossProfit` filed after a derived vintage → counted restated, not stored, also when both filings arrive in one run (the derived row keeps the first filing's acceptance); an unstamped accession carrying `GrossProfit` for the key with `filed` on or before the components' filing's → nothing derived, `statement_held` counts the held entry once, and once stamped the earlier-accepted is the vintage; a conflict-withheld `GrossProfit` in the components' filing → nothing derived from it, and the key stays empty until a later filing carries it cleanly; a `GrossProfit` skipped as non-USD or malformed → the candidate stands; every `derived` row in the fixture store equals the difference of the two stored `revenue` and `cost_of_revenue` rows with its `cik`, `period_end`, `period_days` and `filing_accession`, both present (the `--check` rule, which also fails on a derived row with a component row missing).
- [ ] First vintage: a fixture issuer whose FY2018 revenue is restated in its FY2019 10-K: `statement_facts_as_of(T)` returns the FY2018 original at every T from the first acceptance on, including after the restating filing; the restated value is in no row; the run message counts `statement_restated = 1`; a re-run adds zero rows.
- [ ] `known_at` is the filing's acceptance from submissions: a fixture entry whose companyfacts `filed` date differs from its acceptance date (an after-hours filing) is visible from the acceptance instant and carries no trace of `filed`.
- [ ] Hold, unstampable and late: with the earlier carrier's accession absent from the stamps file, the key is not written and `statement_held` counts it, also when the two `filed` dates are equal; once stamped, that carrier is the vintage; an accession settled as unstampable holds nothing and its entries count on `statement_unstampable`. A record accepted before a stored vintage that arrives on a later run is not written, counted on `statement_vintage_late`, and `health` warns; `ingest --rebuild-statement-facts` then deletes and re-ingests the table in one transaction, after which the count is zero and the earlier carrier is the vintage, with the deleted count in the run row.
- [ ] Writes: a CIK's rows reach the store in one bulk insert per CIK (a test counts the statements), the tz validation runs once per frame, and a frame with a naive timestamp is refused before the insert.
- [ ] **No look-ahead**: truncation invariance for `statement_facts_as_of` with probes just before and just after every distinct `known_at` in the fixture (the T10 harness), including the join through `securities_as_of` (a CIK with no securities row at T returns nothing; a dual-class CIK's rows appear once per class); the broken adapter's statement fact stamped at its `period_end` close, and one stamped at midnight of `filed`, are each caught by a named check (the stamp precedes the filing's acceptance).
- [ ] `--bulk-from-cache`: with a zip present no request is made for the bulk file (the socket fixture proves it), the bulk path is taken whatever the stale-cache count, a CIK whose member holds its latest stamped cover-form accession makes no per-CIK request, and a file that does not open as a zip raises; without the flag the bulk file is requested as today, and stale statement caches count toward `edgar.bulk_stamp_threshold_ciks` as stale shares caches do, only while the switch is on (off, the count is unchanged).
- [ ] Real-store evidence (owner, plan T78; report-only, no threshold): the re-ingest's run-row counts and run time; zero bulk and per-CIK companyfacts requests for CIKs the zip holds; coverage at a month-end T inside the backfill window; one hand-checked issuer's FY revenue, cost of revenue, gross profit and total assets against its 10-K; `statement_vintage_late = 0`.
- [ ] Comparative by duration class (#1034): a leap-year FY column with its prior year (365 and 364 days) and a 53-week year with its 52-week prior (370 and 363) flag the prior, and a quarter and a nine-month year-to-date in one 10-Q stay separate groups, in all three places the flag is set: the parser, a statement-cache-hit read (the adapter's read-time recompute) and a derived `gross_profit` candidate whose prior year has a different length; `test_comparative_groups_on_exact_period_days_as_the_spec_reads` is inverted, not deleted.
- [ ] Parse-only counts on the health page (#740): the statement card labels exactly `statement_non_usd`, `statement_malformed` and `statement_none` "parsed CIKs only (a cache hit adds zero)" and leaves the other seven counts unlabelled.
- [ ] Price-quality gate (#787): `traded_only=True` drops a zero-volume bar that `traded_only=False` keeps, and from the revision's `known_at` only. `price_jumps_as_of` lists an unexplained 3× move and its return. A stored split or dividend in (previous bar, session] explains its move. A split first known after T leaves the jump listed at T. The list equals the one over a store truncated at T at every probe (truncation invariance).
- [ ] Universe rule 6: a zero-volume bar in the window is `missing_bars`. An unaccepted jump in the window is `price_jump`. Accepting every listed jump of the name, or a window that no longer holds the jump, readmits it. A `price_jump` exclusion is not in the gap's `truncated_history`. Each new key has a membership case.
- [ ] `health` lists pending and accepted jumps without failing `--check`, and `--jumps-before` hides later ones. The backtest provider's marking and raw frames have no zero-volume bar.
- [ ] Retraction (#859):
  - A master read at T before a retraction's `known_at` returns the row; at or after it, it does not; the frame has no `retracted` column.
  - A retraction is never back-dated, and is stamped after the lock.
  - A key derived again comes back live.
  - A `snapshot_static` row of a name gone from the snapshot is never judged, and neither is a CIK with a failed or quarantined filing this run.
  - `--apply` refuses another count or digest, and a traded name without `--allow-traded`, writing nothing.
  - The dry run refuses on a version-10 store, on an empty build and on a failure naming no CIK.
  - An EDGAR ingest's set fails `underived_master_rows`, and the apply clears it.
  - The v10 → v11 migration keeps every row.
  - A `master.keep_successors` id (#922) is never proposed, so the count and digest leave its rows out; the dry run lists them as kept; an ingest check records none of them, an earlier check reads as recorded, and a malformed id refuses the config.

Borrow status and the Alpaca spin-off and name-change actions (amendment 2026-10-11, #1506, ADR 0021)
- [ ] Borrow schema, record and parser (T171): the migration adds `borrow_status` with the four common columns, `provenance = snapshot` only, UNIQUE `(asset_id, known_at)`, and nothing else; a second row for a key raises; `BorrowStatus` refuses a naive datetime and a `known_at` before its `fetched_at`; the parser over a payload in the recorder's shape keeps `us_equity` assets only and counts the others; the pure diff over (the latest stored row per asset, a snapshot) yields a row for a new asset, a changed asset, a renamed asset, a vanished one (`present = FALSE`) and a reappeared one, and none for an unchanged one; the vanished-share guard refuses a snapshot over `max_vanished_share`; the fixture table holds the six cases.
- [ ] Borrow as-of read and the vintage rule (T171b): `borrow_status_as_of(T, symbols)` picks the latest row per `asset_id` with `known_at ≤ T` first, drops `present = FALSE`, then filters `symbols`, in the stated columns and order, with `observed_at` the latest `ok` `alpaca_assets` run cursor at or before T; the flipped asset reads ETB before its second snapshot and HTB after; the vanished asset reads after its first snapshot and not after its second; the renamed asset reads under its new symbol only; a symbol held by two assets returns two rows; truncation invariance holds with the table in the truncated set; `data_vintage` is unchanged at every cutoff by a `borrow_status` row while `store_max_ingested_at` still moves, and the dashboard header's freshness reads ignore the table and the source.
- [ ] Borrow ingest and import (T171c): with `borrow.enabled = false` (the default) `--source all` makes no assets request and writes nothing; enabled, `alpaca_assets` runs after every other source; `--source alpaca_assets` makes one unfiltered request, writes the diff rows and one `ok` run row whose cursor is the fetch time, with `ingested_at` read after the response under a strictly increasing fake clock; a dry run writes nothing; a failed request writes only a failed run row and exits non-zero; `--import-dir` applies the recorder's files in fetch order, skips a file at or before the latest run cursor (the quiet-day ordering: a changed 08:09 file imported after a no-change 22:00 run is skipped), a re-run adds zero rows, a file with a fetch time after the run's clock or with `fetch_finished_utc` before `fetch_started_utc` is refused and counted; the raw keep writes the file and an empty `raw_dir` writes none.
- [ ] Borrow health (T171e): the block's counts, the latest observation from the run cursor, the stale warning at `max_stale_sessions + 1` sessions and the switch warning; `--check` fails on a provenance other than `snapshot` or a duplicate `(asset_id, known_at)`.
- [ ] Borrow real-store evidence (T171d, owner): the import's counts per file, the first snapshot's `known_at` equal to the first file's `fetch_finished_utc`, the daily run's counts against the recorder's file of the same day, `health --check` exit 0, the default flipped and the job line updated in the same PR.
- [ ] Spin-off and name-change parsing (T172): on a constructed payload in Alpaca's shape, a `spin_offs` row becomes one `spinoff` `CorporateAction` with the child as `security_id`, `new_rate / source_rate` as the ratio, the parent's `security_id` as `source_action_id`, `announced_at` NULL and `known_at = announcement_date_known_at(process_date)`; a `name_changes` row becomes one `SymbolChange` stamped the same way with the holder rule (the plain case, the reused-ticker case taking the earlier holder, the conflict case dropped); the two kinds are kept by `process_date` in the request's range whatever the ex-date; a row whose parent, child or old symbol resolves to no security is reported, not returned; a parent equal to its child is counted, not returned; a non-positive rate raises; `ActionType("spinoff")` is valid and `CorporateAction` refuses a non-positive spin-off ratio; the fixture source's contract rule 2 accepts a `spinoff` row stamped at or after the ex-date proxy and refuses one before it.
- [ ] Symbol-change schema, reads, pins and the paper reader (T172b): the migration adds `symbol_changes` with the four common columns, `provenance = action` only and the identity index; `--check` fails on a `symbol_changes` row whose `known_at` is not `announcement_date_known_at(process_date)` and on a `spinoff` row stamped before the close of the session before its ex-date, and passes on the fixture; `symbol_changes_as_of(T)` returns the latest revision per identity with `known_at ≤ T`; the fixture's name change is invisible at the close of its `process_date` and visible at the next session's close; truncation invariance holds with both new rows in the truncated set; the broken adapter's early-stamped spin-off is refused; `data_vintage` is unchanged by a `spinoff` row and a `symbol_changes` row; **the consumer pins**: with the fixture's `spinoff` row present and removed, `adjusted_prices_as_of` (with and without dividends), `dropped_dividends_as_of`, `price_jumps_as_of`, `universe_as_of`, `late_dividends`, `compare_periods`, `check` and the action-reading helper of each named execution module return equal frames, and the H1 and TWIN fingerprint and paper-invariance tests are green; `window.start` explains a residue from the real-shaped row and refuses a child position below the expected quantity.
- [ ] Ingest, writers and repair (T172c): the daily fixture run stores one `spinoff` row and one `symbol_changes` row with the stated stamps and a re-run adds zero rows; a spin-off with ex-date in month M and `process_date` in month M+1 is stored by the first run on or after its stamp; the month backfill and `--fill-holes` store a spin-off from their payload and drop the name changes; a record whose stamp is after the run's clock is skipped and counted, never raised, on every writer; an unresolved record is counted and stored by a later run once the fixture resolver holds the child, same stamp; a backfill month re-returning a stored spin-off with an ex-date outside the month adds zero rows and raises nothing; a differing CUSIP for a stored name change writes a revision at `ingested_at`; `repair-resolution`'s dry run deletes a `spinoff` row whose child the resolver no longer holds on its ex-date and leaves one it holds, a child's misattributed same-day dividend no longer takes the spin-off with it, and `symbol_changes` rows are listed as unjudged.
- [ ] The actions backfill (T172e): `ingest --source alpaca --actions-backfill --since 2020-06-01` makes one symbol-less `spin_offs` and `name_changes` request per month and writes by the same rules, one run row per month in mode `actions` that resume ignores; the dry run lists per-month counts and writes nothing; `--since 2020-05-01` is refused.
- [ ] Actions real-store evidence (T172d, owner): the backfill's counts per month (stored, unresolved, reused, conflict, not yet known), the AAN/PROG 2020-12-01 name change on PROG Holdings and one known 2021-or-later spin-off (its ratio checked by hand against the parent's and child's closes around the ex-date), one month's daily-path rows equal to the scan's, the adjusted close of five H1 names on five sessions unchanged, `health --check` exit 0, no trial or sweep variant made stale by the run, and the H1 and TWIN fingerprint tests green on the store after it.

Filing events (amendment 2026-10-09, #1358)
- [ ] Schema (T164b): the migration adds `filing_events` with the four common columns, `provenance = filing`, UNIQUE `(cik, accession)`, and nothing else; a second row for a key raises; the `FilingEvent` record refuses a naive datetime; the fixture source answers `filing_events(cik)` with the three 8-K rows (the 16:00-17:30 `2.02,9.01` whose `filed` date equals its session, the 20:30 `2.02,9.01`, the `5.02`).
- [ ] Adapter (T164c): `reduce_submissions` keeps `items` verbatim and an `items`-less page parses with `items = None`; the stamps cache round-trips `items` under `STAMPS_LAYOUT`; **every pre-existing stamp (`form`, `accepted_at`, every `accepted_at = None` sentinel) is byte-identical after the layout migration** on the fixture cache, and a read-only diff of every CIK's old and new stamps file on the owner's cache is posted on the PR; the refresh writes `items` only, once per CIK, and never with the switch off (the socket fixture proves no request); an accession without the column after the refresh is terminal and counted.
- [ ] As-of read (T164d): `filing_events_as_of(T, forms, items)` joins through `securities_as_of(T)`, one row per `(security_id, accession)`, filters exactly on `form` and on whole item codes; an 8-K accepted after `close(T)` is invisible at T and visible at the next read; the `filed`-date trap with teeth (the 16:00-17:30 row is invisible at T's open and at `close(T)`, first visible at T + 1, and a `filed`-keyed read fails both); the 20:30 lag row; truncation invariance with the table in the truncated set.
- [ ] Ingest (T164e): with `edgar.filing_events_enabled = false` (the default) a run asks no `filing_events`, makes no request and no cache write for it, and adds no row; a dry run writes nothing; the fixture run stores the three rows with their acceptances, `known_at = accepted_at`; a re-run adds zero rows; a record accepted after the run's clock is skipped and counted `filing_events_not_yet_known`, never clamped, never fatal; `--rebuild-filing-events` deletes and re-inserts in one transaction and records the counts before and after; the run row carries `filing_events_unstamped`, `filing_events_items_missing`, `filing_events_not_yet_known` and `filing_events_clock_suspect`.
- [ ] Health (T164e): the row count and latest `accepted_at` per form, the last run's counts with the two warnings, the switch's state; `--check` fails on `known_at ≠ accepted_at`, a `form` outside `edgar.event_forms`, a NULL `items`, or a duplicate `(cik, accession)`, and passes on correct data; the clock detector flags a fixture stamp accepted before 17:30 on a session and dated the next day, and one accepted after its filing date, and flags nothing on the three fixture rows.
- [ ] Real-store evidence (T164e, report-only except the flip): the run-row counts and the quarterly 2.02 coverage from the store against TP-1's column, within one percentage point; the default is flipped only with that, `health --check` green, a zero-row re-run, and #1382 closed.

Rates (amendment 2026-10-11, #1498, ADR 0020)
- [ ] Schema and record (T169): the migration adds `rates` with the four common columns, `provenance = release` only, UNIQUE `(series, observation_date, known_at)`, and nothing else; a second row for a key raises; `RateObservation` refuses a naive datetime where one appears and a non-finite value; `rate_known_at(vintage_date)` equals `session_close(next_session(vintage_date))` for a session, a Friday, a Saturday and a holiday vintage date; the fixture source serves the six generated cases.
- [ ] As-of read (T169b): `rates_as_of(T)` returns the latest revision per `(series, observation_date)` with `known_at ≤ T` and the stated columns; the revised fixture observation reads as its first value between the two stamps and its second after; truncation invariance with `rates` in the truncated set at probes around every `known_at`; the broken adapter's rate stamped at close(`observation_date`) and one stamped at the vintage date's close are each caught by `early_rate_stamp`.
- [ ] Adapter (T169c): the parser on a constructed `output_type=1` payload yields one record per observation and value with `vintage_date = realtime_start`, counts `.` values and raises on a missing `observations` key, an unparseable date or a non-numeric value; the raw client builds the request with the key as `api_key`, `file_type=json`, `output_type=1`, `limit=100000` and pages on `count > limit`; no log line or exception text holds the query string: a test fails a request against a fake transport with a known dummy key, captures every logger at DEBUG (`httpx` and `httpcore` included) and the full formatted traceback (`traceback.format_exception`, chained causes included), and asserts the key appears in neither; `scrub_text` replaces an `api_key=` value; the socket fixture proves no request without a key and a refusal that names `FRED_API_KEY`.
- [ ] Ingest (T169d): the fixture backfill stores the six cases with their stamps and a re-run adds zero rows; a record stamped after the run's clock is skipped and counted `rates_not_yet_known`, never clamped, and the next run (clock moved) stores it with its vintage; a revision writes a second row stamped at its own vintage's next close, counted `rates_revisions`; a repeated value writes nothing; a `.` counts `rates_missing_values`; the daily request's `realtime_start` is `today − rates.refetch_days`; a latest returned observation more than `rates.max_stale_sessions` sessions before the expected session halts non-zero with only the run row written; `--dry-run` writes nothing; `--source all` includes `fred`; a daily run on a store whose latest observation is older than `rates.refetch_days` starts its window the day after it and fills the hole in one run, counting the rows `rates_late_rows`; a daily run on a store with no row for the series refuses and names the backfill.
- [ ] Health (T169e): the block's per-series fields and the attribution line; a warning on a revised key, on a last daily run with `rates_late_rows > 0`, on a gap between observation dates longer than `rates.max_stale_sessions` sessions, and on a value outside `rates.plausible_range_percent`; `--check` fails on `known_at ≠ rate_known_at(vintage_date)`, a non-`release` provenance, a duplicate key or a non-finite value, and passes on the fixture store.
- [ ] Real-store evidence (T170d, owner): `ingest --source fred --backfill --since 2016-01-04` run-row counts (rows, revisions expected 0, not-yet-known, missing), the latest observation and vintage, `health --check` exit 0, a hand check of five observation dates against the H.15 historical release (2016-01-04, 2020-03-16, 2022-03-11, 2024-01-02 and the latest observation stored), and `rates_as_of` at a 2022-03-15 close returning the 2022-03-11 observation as the latest (the probe's lag, one session more under the rule).

## Out of scope

Signals, backtester, trial registry, cost model (Phase 3). Risk-gated broker wrapper, Alpaca paper adapter, alerts (Phase 4). Paid vendor (Phase 3 ADR). Intraday data. Fundamentals beyond shares outstanding, SIC and the five statement facts of the 2026-10-03 amendment (#660); within that amendment, also out of scope: IFRS statements (20-F/40-F filers), dimensioned (segment) facts, restated values as revision rows, and every derived series (a fourth quarter as FY minus nine months, trailing twelve months, calendarised periods), which are read-time computations for Phase 3. Pre-2019 ticker history beyond `snapshot_static` *(amended 2026-10-12, #1597: rows read from a filing's own file names are in; a historical ticker file or any snapshot-like source stays out)*. Parquet export command is Phase 2 (`tradepartner export`) but automated export at tags is Phase 3 tooling.

## Data / interfaces

**Tables** (all with `known_at`, `ingested_at`, `source`, `provenance`):
- `securities(security_id, cik, name, benchmark, retracted)` (`retracted` from schema version 11, #859)
- `listings(security_id, ticker, exchange, class_title, valid_from, retracted)` — `valid_to` derived at read time
- `classifications(security_id, sic, security_type, rule)`
- `delistings(security_id, form, class_title, exchange, filed_at, effective_on)` — end session and transfer status derived at read time
- `prices_daily(security_id, session, open, high, low, close, volume)` raw
- `corporate_actions(security_id, action_type, ex_date, ratio_or_amount, announced_at)` (`announced_at` nullable, #83)
- `symbol_changes(security_id, old_symbol, new_symbol, old_cusip, new_cusip, process_date, source_action_id)` — amendment 2026-10-11 (#1506); UNIQUE on the identity (Alpaca's id when given, else `(old_symbol, new_symbol, process_date)`, with `security_id`) and `known_at`, `provenance = action`, `known_at` = the close of the first session strictly after `process_date` (req 5's date-only rule)
- `borrow_status(asset_id, symbol, exchange, status, tradable, marginable, shortable, easy_to_borrow, borrow_status, fractionable, present)` — amendment 2026-10-11 (#1506, ADR 0021); UNIQUE `(asset_id, known_at)`, `provenance = snapshot`, `known_at` = the fetch time; one row per change, not per day
- `facts(security_id, fact_name, as_of_date, class_member, value, filing_accession)`
- `statement_facts(cik, fact_name, xbrl_tag, period_start, period_end, period_days, value, unit, form, filing_accession, basis, comparative)` — amendment 2026-10-03 (#660); UNIQUE `(cik, fact_name, period_end, period_days)`, no `known_at` in the key
- `filing_events(cik, accession, form, items, accepted_at)` — amendment 2026-10-09 (#1358); UNIQUE `(cik, accession)`, `known_at = accepted_at`, no revisions by construction
- `rates(series, observation_date, value, vintage_date)` — amendment 2026-10-11 (#1498); UNIQUE `(series, observation_date, known_at)`, `known_at` = close of the first XNYS session strictly after `vintage_date`, `provenance = release`; a later vintage with a different value is a revision row; left out of the data vintage
- `ingestion_runs(run_id, started_at, finished_at, status, source, mode, rows_added, chunk_cursor, message)`
- `master_underived(run_id, recorded_at, table_name, security_id, ticker, exchange, valid_from, known_at)` — not a fact table (#859): a master check's finding, read only by health

**Instrument ids** ([ADR 0015](../decisions/0015-expansion-seams.md) seam 6; `master.py`, T138). Under that rule every `security_id` column in the journal and the backtest is read as an *instrument id*; for a share the instrument id equals the security id, so today's rows need no change (a class id's `:` follows a CIK, never a type; see `master.py`). By the same rule a future `instruments` table issues ids in the same namespace with a type prefix, as `BENCH:` already does for benchmarks, and `securities` stays the equity master.

**Master column sources and `known_at`**

| Column | Source | `known_at` | Provenance |
|---|---|---|---|
| securities row, cik | earliest **issuer-form** filing per CIK in the full-history EDGAR filing index | first acceptance | filing |
| name | companies snapshot | fetch time, allowed static | snapshot_static |
| ticker, exchange, class title (≥ ~2019) | cover-page `dei:TradingSymbol`, `dei:SecurityExchangeName`, `dei:Security12bTitle` | acceptance | filing |
| ticker, exchange (earlier) | Alpaca assets snapshot + companies snapshot | fetch time, allowed static | snapshot_static |
| SIC | filing SGML header | acceptance | filing |
| security type | rules per ADR 0006 "Verify" (SIC 6770; 20-F/40-F; F-6; N-CSR/N-PORT/485BPOS/N-2; title/suffix; asset class) | acceptance of the filing used, else fetch time | filing / snapshot_static |
| shares outstanding | XBRL `EntityCommonStockSharesOutstanding` with cover `as_of_date` and class dimension | acceptance | filing |
| delisting / transfer | Forms 25 and 25-NSE, per class and exchange | acceptance | filing |
| splits, dividends | `PriceSource.corporate_actions` | first seen: announcement time else close before ex-date; revisions: `ingested_at` | action |
| bars | `PriceSource.bars` | session close; revisions: `ingested_at` | bar |

**Amendment 2026-09-25 (#216).** For XBRL filings since `edgar.fsn_first_year` (default 2015) the SIC comes from the SEC Financial Statement and Notes data sets (`sub.sic`, as of the filing date) and the cover facts (`dei:Security12bTitle`, `dei:SecurityExchangeName`, `dei:TradingSymbol`, per-class `EntityCommonStockSharesOutstanding`) from the same files. The SGML header and the iXBRL document are the sources for filings the data sets do not yet hold: headers for registration forms in `edgar.header_forms` from `edgar.header_first_year`, for periodic forms and 8-K only from the first day of the newest cached data-set period, and for a data-set filing in that window whose SIC is blank; cover documents only from that same day; an older cover filing absent from the data sets is counted, not fetched. Both are stamped at the filing's acceptance from the submissions cache, never from the data set's own `accepted` or `filed` columns. See plan T11c to T11f and [research #174](../research/2026-09-25-cover-page-facts-at-scale.md).

**Amendment 2026-10-03 (#660): as-filed statement facts.** The store gains the statement values the Phase 3 profitability claim needs (research brief B3, "slow liquid profitability-aware tilt": gross profits over total assets, the only SUPPORTED selection claim, [brief](../research/2026-10-03-quantitative-investing-research-brief.md); the [claims pilot](../research/2026-10-03-claims-pilot.md) HS-4 and its data table), stored as each issuer **first filed** them, so a signal at T sees exactly what a reader of the filing saw at T and nothing a later restatement, reclassification or comparative column added. Merging this amendment records owner decisions (a) to (e) below; the open questions stay open.

*Facts.* Five canonical names, each an ordered list of `taxonomy:tag` fallbacks in `edgar.statement_tags` (the defaults below; the order is the precedence within one filing):
- `revenue`: `us-gaap:Revenues`, `us-gaap:RevenueFromContractWithCustomerExcludingAssessedTax`, `us-gaap:SalesRevenueNet`, `us-gaap:RevenueFromContractWithCustomerIncludingAssessedTax`, `us-gaap:SalesRevenueGoodsNet`, `us-gaap:SalesRevenueServicesNet`;
- `cost_of_revenue`: `us-gaap:CostOfRevenue`, `us-gaap:CostOfGoodsAndServicesSold`, `us-gaap:CostOfGoodsSold`, `us-gaap:CostOfServices`;
- `gross_profit`: `us-gaap:GrossProfit`, derived when not filed (below);
- `total_assets`: `us-gaap:Assets`;
- `operating_cash_flow`: `us-gaap:NetCashProvidedByUsedInOperatingActivities`, `us-gaap:NetCashProvidedByUsedInOperatingActivitiesContinuingOperations`.

Why these (decision (a)): gross profits over assets needs `gross_profit` and `total_assets`; `revenue` and `cost_of_revenue` are its components (the derivation, and E1's revenue target); `operating_cash_flow` is included because cash-based operating profitability (Ball, Gerakos, Linnainmaa and Nikolaev, JFE 2016; not graded in the brief) is the one refinement of the profitability claim the signal work is likely to ask for next, and it costs nothing structurally: it is a duration fact exactly like `revenue`, stored as filed (a 10-Q's cash-flow statement is year-to-date, so its quarterly value is a read-time difference, which this amendment does not store). `us-gaap:InventoryNet` is **not** included (decision (b)): no SUPPORTED claim uses it (Novy-Marx's gross profitability does not), and for an issuer without inventory the absence of the tag and a value of zero mean different things, so a consumer could not tell "no inventory" from "not tagged" and any ratio built on it would be silently biased. Adding a name later is a config change plus one re-ingest (below). The lists are deliberately short: every tag on them is the concept itself, never a near relative (`CostOfGoodsAndServiceExcludingDepreciationDepletionAndAmortization` is a different concept and stays off the list).

*Table.* `statement_facts(cik, fact_name, xbrl_tag, period_start, period_end, period_days, value, unit, form, filing_accession, basis, comparative)` plus the four common columns, `provenance = filing` only. Keyed by **CIK**, not `security_id` (decision (c)): a statement is the issuer's, not a share class's (a dual-class company has one revenue), so no class mapping, no "unmatched" fact and no duplicate per class. ADR 0003 rule 3 is kept: the key is the master's own CIK column, never a ticker, and the read resolves through the master. `statement_facts_as_of(T, security_ids=None)` joins through the `securities` rows known at T and returns **one row per `(security_id, fact_name, period_end, period_days)`**, so a dual-class issuer's rows appear once per class (the shape every other as-of function has) and a CIK with no securities row at T is invisible at T. `period_start` is NULL and `period_days = 0` for an instant (`total_assets`); for a duration, `period_days = period_end − period_start` in days. **UNIQUE `(cik, fact_name, period_end, period_days)`** with no `known_at`: the schema itself enforces one vintage per period. Schema version bump with an additive migration (the table is new; no existing row changes).

*First vintage (decision (d)).* For each key the stored row is the value in the **earliest-accepted** filing (acceptance from submissions; the form is the submissions record's, never the companyfacts entry's `form`, and must be in `edgar.statement_forms`, default `10-K, 10-Q, 10-K/A, 10-Q/A, 10-KT, 10-QT`) that carries it. `known_at` is that filing's acceptance (submissions UTC; never companyfacts `filed`, FSN `accepted` or an SGML time, pitfall P12 of [#572](../research/2026-10-02-edgar-data-pitfalls.md)). A later filing carrying the same key, with an identical value (next year's comparative column) or a different one (a restatement, a reclassification, a 10-K/A), is **never stored**: no revision row, no in-place update. Differing later values are counted per run (`statement_restated`) so the owner can see how much restatement the rule hides; the Definitions' revision rule ("a later value for the same key is a new row") does not apply to this table, and that is the point of it. ADR 0003 rule 1 (every record carries `known_at` and `ingested_at`) holds unchanged; rule 4's fixture case "one restated fundamental (two `known_at` values for the same period)" stays met by `facts` (the restated shares case), and `statement_facts` cannot hold a revision by construction; no ADR is needed, because the table is rebuildable from the cached zip and nothing stored elsewhere changes. `form` is kept verbatim (`10-K/A`), so an amendment that is the first carrier of a key is visible as such. Within a run, a CIK's records are taken in acceptance order and a stored key skips. Two guards keep the result independent of the order runs see filings in: (i) a key is **held** (not written this run, counted `statement_held`) while an entry for it sits under an accession that has **no stamp record yet** (not in the CIK's stamps file) and a companyfacts `filed` date **earlier than or equal to** the stamped carrier's (`filed` orders, it never stamps; a same-day tie holds); an accession the stamps file has settled as **unstampable** (`accepted_at` None, the adapter's cached-unstampable case) never holds anything: its entries are absent, counted on `statement_unstampable`; (ii) a record accepted earlier than a stored vintage that surfaces on a later run (a stamp that arrived late, or a tag added to the list after the key was stored from a later filing) is not written and is counted (`statement_vintage_late`); `health` warns while it is non-zero. The remedy is `tradepartner ingest --source edgar --rebuild-statement-facts` (with `--bulk-from-cache`): in the run's one transaction it deletes every `statement_facts` row and re-ingests the table from the cached payloads, and the run row records the row counts before and after; never a back-dated row, never a partial fix. A rebuild changes what a past as-of read of this table returned, so a trial logged before it is reproducible only from its own export (the Phase 3 registry's concern, not this table's); T78 states the count before the first rebuild, if one is needed.

*Within one filing.* Tag precedence: the first name in the fallback list that the filing carries for the period wins and is recorded in `xbrl_tag`; two listed tags with different values for one period are different concepts, not a conflict. **Conflict** (pitfall P13): the same tag, period and unit with two different values in one filing withholds that (accession, fact, period) key from that filing: counted (`statement_conflicts`) and listed by the adapter on `statement_conflict_keys`, a plain attribute (accession, fact, period, the values), for the run message and T78's report, **never** recorded in `failed_filings.json` and never counted by T11h's per-document share or its cross-day (error class, base form) pair rule, which would otherwise halt every EDGAR run once five 10-K accessions had conflicted (a conflict is a property of the filing, not a fetch failure, and no number of them is a reason to stop ingesting); identical duplicates collapse to one; a withheld key's vintage is then whichever later filing carries it cleanly, with `comparative` set by the Periods rule as for any row (TRUE when it came from a prior-period column, FALSE for a 10-K/A of the same period). Only the units in `edgar.statement_units` (default `USD`) are read; a filing whose only unit for a tag is another currency is counted (`statement_non_usd`) and skipped. Only undimensioned facts: the companyfacts API carries no segment facts by construction, so an entry that names one is schema drift (pitfall P9) and the parser raises, which fails the chunk on purpose, as every other drift does.

*Derived gross profit (decision (e)).* The parser never derives; the **ingest** does, inside the first-vintage pass: a filing whose `revenue` and `cost_of_revenue` for a period are the **stored** vintages of both keys (stored on an earlier run, or being stored from this filing), in one unit, and which carries no `GrossProfit` for that period in a read unit (an entry skipped as non-USD or malformed is no value for the key, exactly as a skipped component is, so it does not count as carried), contributes a **derived candidate** for the `gross_profit` key, `revenue − cost_of_revenue`, taken at that filing's turn in the CIK's acceptance order, after the filing's reported keys, as the filing's own entry: if the key is still unstored then, the row is stored with `basis = derived`, `xbrl_tag` = the two source tags joined by `-`, that accession, and its acceptance as `known_at` (no knowledge beyond the filing's own); a stored key skips the candidate as it skips any entry. Every filed row carries `basis = reported`. Ordering the candidate by its filing's acceptance is what makes the vintage the same whether the filings arrived in one run or several: the first load sees a 10-K without gross profit and next year's 10-K carrying it as a comparative in one pass, and still stores the derived row at the first 10-K's acceptance and counts the comparative as restated (a derivation run at the end of the CIK's pass would store the later filed value instead, which is why it runs at the filing's turn). When the two components' vintages come from different filings, or one is withheld, nothing is derived (a consumer may subtract at read time). A filed `gross_profit` whose filing is the earliest carrier always wins; a derived row, once stored, is the key's vintage like any other, so a `gross_profit` filed later is counted as restated, not stored (the first filing had no gross profit, and the derived value is what its reader could compute). Two cases withhold the candidate. **Held key:** guard (i) applies to the candidate as to any carrier: while an accession with no stamp record (its records reach the ingest with `accepted_at = None` and their `filed`) carries `GrossProfit` for the key with a `filed` date earlier than or equal to the components' filing's, the key is held and nothing is derived for it this run, however complete the components (`statement_held` counts the held entry once; the withheld candidate adds nothing), since a derived row would take the key and the held filed value, once stamped, would be counted as restated and lost; once the stamp arrives, the earlier-accepted of the two is the vintage, the filed value as `reported` or the derived row. **Conflict in the same filing:** a `GrossProfit` withheld by conflict in the components' own filing (on the adapter's `statement_conflict_keys` list, which the ingest reads by accession, fact and period; a source without the attribute has none) withholds the candidate too: that filing's gross profit is ambiguous, so nothing is derived from it; and because the components' vintages are that filing's and no other accession can share them, only a later filing's clean `GrossProfit` can fill the key (`reported`, `comparative` by the Periods rule), and if none ever comes the key stays empty, visible through the run's `statement_conflicts` count, and a consumer may subtract at read time. Every derived row therefore equals the difference of the two stored `revenue` and `cost_of_revenue` rows with its `cik`, `period_end`, `period_days` and `filing_accession`, both present, and `health --check` recomputes exactly that (a derived row with either component row missing fails it), which cannot fail on correct data. The flag is on the row because a consumer may want to exclude derived values (a filer that reports no gross profit is often one whose cost line is not a cost of goods).

**Amendment 2026-10-12 (#1556): a financial issuer's revenue.** The Facts list above gains `revenue_financial` (`us-gaap:RevenuesNetOfInterestExpense`) and two constituent names, `net_interest_income` (`us-gaap:InterestIncomeExpenseNet`) and `noninterest_income` (`us-gaap:NoninterestIncome`), each parsed and stored as filed (a config change plus one re-ingest, as the Facts paragraph allows). A financial issuer's revenue is one documented quantity from its own filing: `revenue_financial` holds `us-gaap:RevenuesNetOfInterestExpense` when the filing reports it, else the sum of net interest income plus noninterest income. The sum is computed by the **same derived-candidate rule as gross profit** (`ingest._derived_candidates`, the table `DERIVED_FACTS` of `(target, left, right, operator)` pairs): whenever both constituents are the stored vintages of one filing and period, in one unit, the filing carries no reported `revenue_financial` and `conflicts` lists none, whatever `revenue` tag that filing carries, the row is stored at that filing's turn with `basis = derived`, `known_at` its acceptance and `xbrl_tag` the two source tags joined by the operator (`+`). A reported `RevenuesNetOfInterestExpense` row is `basis = reported`. No target is a `revenue` fallback: `revenue` (and therefore `gross_profit`, which derives from `revenue` and `cost_of_revenue`, not from `revenue_financial`) is exactly as before, every row stays point-in-time, and a one-pass and a run-by-run build agree. Non-financial issuers are unchanged; gross profit stays N/A for financials. A consumer wanting one revenue series coalesces `revenue` and `revenue_financial` at read time and takes the later `known_at` of the rows it reads. IFRS issuers are out of scope. The Health rule `statement_derived_matches_components` checks derived `revenue_financial` by the same rule as derived gross profit.

*Periods.* The key is the fact's own `(period_start, period_end)` from the entry, never `fy`/`fp` (a comparative column in a 10-K carries the filing's `fy`/`fp`, not its own) and never `frame` (SEC assigns it to one entry per calendar frame, usually the latest filing's, so a first vintage often has none). Every as-filed duration is stored as filed: a quarter, a half-year or nine-month year-to-date, a fiscal year, a stub after a fiscal-year change; `period_days` tells them apart and consumers filter on it. **No fourth quarter (FY minus nine months), no trailing twelve months, no calendarisation** at ingest: each is a read-time computation whose `known_at` is its latest component's, Phase 3's to write against this table. `comparative = TRUE` when the row's `period_end` is earlier than the latest `period_end` the same filing carries for the same fact and `period_days` (the value came from a prior-period column; amended by #1034 below: the same fact and **duration class**, not the same `period_days`).
