*Borrow status: rows record change, and the observation record (open question 16).* A pure function `snapshot_rows(latest, assets, fetched_at)` over the latest stored row per `asset_id` and one parsed snapshot returns the rows to write: one per asset with no stored row, one per asset whose stored tuple (`symbol`, `exchange`, `status`, the five flags, `borrow_status`, **and `present`**) differs from the snapshot's (a payload row has `present = TRUE`, so a vanished asset that reappears with its old values gets its reappearance row rather than staying vanished for ever), one `present = FALSE` row per stored asset with `present = TRUE` that the snapshot lacks, and none for an unchanged asset. **The observation record is the run row:** every snapshot, changed or not, writes one `ingestion_runs` row (source `alpaca_assets`, status `ok`, cursor = the fetch time as ISO-8601 UTC, `rows_added` = the rows written, message = the counts: assets, `us_equity`, new, changed, vanished, other class). **The latest observation** is the newest `ok` `alpaca_assets` run's cursor, never the table's `MAX(known_at)`, which on a quiet day lags the last snapshot; the import's skip, the as-of read's `observed_at` and the health warning all read the run cursor. "Latest as of T" per `asset_id` is therefore the status at T, and `observed_at` says when it was last confirmed. **The truncation guard:** the request is `get_all_assets()` with no status, class or exchange filter, exactly the recorder's; a snapshot whose vanished share (stored `present` assets missing from the payload, over the stored `present` assets) exceeds `borrow.max_vanished_share` (default 0.05) is refused with a failed run row and no table write, because a partial or differently filtered list would write thousands of `present = FALSE` rows and their reappearances as permanent false history. The first snapshot writes one row per `us_equity` asset (about 33.6k).

*Borrow status: source, ingest and import (T171c).* `alpaca_raw.assets_all(settings)`: one `TradingClient.get_all_assets()` through the existing `_trading_client` (the data key pair, `paper=True`; the paper and live endpoints are assumed to serve one reference list, which T171d checks once by comparing the daily run's counts with the recorder's `main`-key file of the same day; open question 21), returning the raw list and the two clock readings around the call. `tradepartner ingest --source alpaca_assets`: one request, one chunk, one transaction under the run's lock, one run row; no staleness rule (the endpoint is current-only: the request succeeds or the source fails, with only the run row written, and the run exits non-zero as req 9 says for any failed source). Under `--source all` it runs **last**, after `alpaca` and `edgar` (and `fred`, #1498), and only when `borrow.enabled`, so a failure of the assets request never costs a day's bars: the earlier sources' chunks are committed before it starts. `--import-dir <dir>` (with `--source alpaca_assets`): every `assets-*.json.gz` in the directory in ascending `fetch_finished_utc`, each applied as one snapshot stamped at its own `fetch_finished_utc` through the same parser, guard and diff (`fetch_started_utc` and `endpoint` are checked, not stored), skipping a file whose fetch time is at or before the latest observation (the run cursor; so a file recorded at 08:09 is never applied after the daily run of 22:00 wrote a later observation, which would make an older state read as the latest), so the import is idempotent and the recorder may keep running until the first scheduled run; no request is made; a dry run lists the files and the counts and writes nothing. The daily raw payload is kept under `borrow.raw_dir` when set (open question 17), the same file shape the recorder writes, so the import and the keep are one format.

*Borrow status: as-of read (T171b).* `borrow_status_as_of(T, symbols=None)` (`store/asof.py`) returns the latest row per `asset_id` among rows with `known_at ≤ T` (chosen first), dropped when that row has `present = FALSE`, and only then filtered by `symbols` on the row's `symbol` exactly (the symbol on the fetch day; a renamed asset's old symbol therefore returns nothing, never its stale pre-rename row; a symbol held by several assets returns several rows), `None` meaning no filter; sorted by `(symbol, asset_id)`; columns `asset_id`, `symbol`, `exchange`, `status`, `tradable`, `marginable`, `shortable`, `easy_to_borrow`, `borrow_status`, `fractionable`, `known_at`, `observed_at` (the latest `ok` `alpaca_assets` run cursor at or before T, the same on every row, NULL when there is none). The look-ahead cases: the flipped fixture asset reads ETB at the close after its first snapshot and HTB after its second; the vanished asset reads after its first and not after its second; the renamed asset reads under its new symbol only; truncation invariance with the table in the truncated set (the run rows are not truncated by the harness, and `observed_at` is filtered by T itself).

*Borrow status: health and config (T171e).* `health.py` and the page gain a borrow block: row count, distinct assets, the latest observation (the run cursor) and the latest `known_at`, the last run's counts, and at the latest observation the counts of `shortable` and `easy_to_borrow` assets among `active` `us_equity` ones (the recorder's first file: 5,373 shortable, 5,374 ETB; one asset, `STRZ`, is ETB and not shortable, so a future ETB gate requires both flags); a warning when the latest observation is more than `borrow.max_stale_sessions` sessions before the run's last completed session, and when `borrow.enabled` is off; `--check`: the four common-column rules, `provenance = snapshot` on every row, `(asset_id, known_at)` unique. Config: `borrow.enabled=false` (T171d flips it once the import and the first daily run have passed, as T78 did), `borrow.max_stale_sessions=2`, `borrow.max_vanished_share=0.05`, `borrow.raw_dir=data/alpaca_assets` (empty turns the keep off). No new env var. The scheduling runbook's daily job line carries `--source all`, which includes the snapshot once enabled (T171d).

*Spin-offs (T172, T172b, T172c).* `parse_corporate_actions` reads the `spin_offs` category it drops today. Per row: the parent is `resolve(source_symbol, previous_session(ex_date))` and the child `resolve(new_symbol, ex_date)` (the child's own first session; both through the `lead` as splits are, #974); the ratio is `new_rate / source_rate`, child shares per parent share, positive and finite or the payload is malformed. The row is stored in `corporate_actions` in the **T64 convention** (#436, #473; `execution/window.py` reads it): `action_type = spinoff` (a new `ActionType` member), `security_id` = the child, `ratio_or_amount` = the ratio, `source_action_id` = the parent's `security_id`, `ex_date` = the payload's, `announced_at` NULL, `source = alpaca`, `provenance = action`. The row's identity is therefore (child, parent) and Alpaca's own id is not kept for a spin-off (open question 19); a later payload that differs for that identity is a revision stamped at `ingested_at` by the existing rule; a second distribution from the same parent to the same child is a revision of the first, and a record Alpaca withdraws stays until a repair (both known limits; no id, no absence-based cancel). A spin-off whose parent and child are the same security (a symbol kept across the ex-date) is counted `actions_self_spinoff`, not stored. **The stamp is the date-only rule the spec already has (req 5): `known_at = announcement_date_known_at(process_date)`, the close of the first session strictly after `process_date`**, never the split proxy and never the same day's close (open question 18): the source gives no announcement time (ADR 0009), a date carries no time to show the record was public by that day's close, and a spin-off is applied to no adjusted series, so the one extra session costs nothing. `--check` gains (T172b, `health.py`) a lower bound on every `spinoff` row, `known_at ≥ close(previous_session(ex_date))`, since the table stores no `process_date`; the fixture contract (`fixture_prices.py` rule 2) gets the same per-type bound, and the broken adapter a spin-off stamped before it, refused. **Every writer of `corporate_actions` stores spin-offs the same way:** the daily run, the month backfill and `--fill-holes` (`backfill.py`) all go through `prices.corporate_actions()` and `_add_actions`, so the two rules below live in `_add_actions` and apply to each. (1) A record whose stamp is after the run's clock is skipped and counted `actions_not_yet_known`, never clamped and never fatal (the split path raises on that case; the new types do not, since `process_date` is at or after the run on the day Alpaca lists a record). (2) A record whose parent or child resolves to no security is reported on `last_actions_report`, counted `actions_unresolved`, never stored. **The daily window is `process_date`, not `ex_date`:** the daily request already covers `process_date` up to `alpaca.actions_process_lag_days` past the ex-date window (`AlpacaPriceSource`), and a spin-off or name change is kept when its `process_date` lies in the request's process-date range **whatever its `ex_date`**, because a spin-off processed weeks after its ex-date, in the next month, would otherwise be outside every daily window and never stored (the ex-date filter stays for splits and dividends). So a record is first seen by the daily run on or after its `process_date` and stored by the first run whose clock passes its stamp; an unresolved one is retried by every daily run whose range still covers its `process_date` and by any actions backfill of its month. A backfill month that re-returns a spin-off the daily path already stored, with an ex-date outside the month, adds zero rows and raises nothing. **Consumers that stay byte-identical, by construction and pinned by test (T172b):** `adjusted_prices_as_of` and `dropped_dividends_as_of` (their latest-revision CTE filters `action_type IN (split, dividend)`), `price_jumps_as_of` and `universe_as_of` (rule 6's split factors), `late_dividends`, `execution/report.compare_periods` and `execution/check`, and every `live_actions_as_of` reader on the execution path (`ledger`, `plan`, `planning`, `outcomes`, `marks`, `risk`, `reserve`, `phases`, `wrapper`, `run`), each of which filters by type; the pin is one parametrised test over the action-reading helper of each named execution module, on a frame with and without the row (`live_actions_as_of` itself returns the row, so the pin must sit at the readers), plus the H1 and TWIN fingerprint and paper-invariance tests; `reconcile_run`'s `spinoffs` stays `{}` until T48b's recording (#473; its docstring is updated by T172c); `ingest`'s id-less re-date logic ignores a row that carries an id. **The paper-path reader (T172b, safety-reviewer):** `window.start` explains a residue from a real-shaped row as its tests already do, and a child position below the expected quantity (cash in lieu) is refused, not accepted. **`repair-resolution` and `repair-bars` (T172c):** `_Counted` and `_delete` take an `action_type` filter so a judgement touches only the types it judged (a child's misattributed dividend on the spin-off's ex-date no longer deletes the spin-off); a `spinoff` row is judged by its own rule, the parent held on `previous_session(ex_date)` and the child on `ex_date` (both through the lead, `actions=True`), so a row whose parent or child the resolver no longer assigns is deleted and the next fetch rewrites it under the right ids, which is also how a re-resolved identity retires its old one; `repair-bars` keeps deleting every action of the security on the sessions it repairs, and `--fill-holes` rewrites them, spin-offs included. `symbol_changes` rows are listed by the dry run as unjudged, never deleted.

*Name changes (T172, T172b, T172c).* A new table, not `corporate_actions` rows: a name change has no ratio and two symbols and two CUSIPs that the actions table cannot hold. `symbol_changes(security_id, old_symbol, new_symbol, old_cusip, new_cusip, process_date, source_action_id)` plus the four common columns; `source = alpaca`, `provenance = action` only; symbols and CUSIPs verbatim; `source_action_id` Alpaca's id when given, else the empty string. **Identity** (#108's pattern): `(security_id, source_action_id)` when the id is non-empty, else `(security_id, old_symbol, new_symbol, process_date)`; the UNIQUE index mirrors `corporate_actions_identity`; a later payload that differs for an identity (a CUSIP, a re-dated `process_date` under the same id) is a revision at `ingested_at`. `known_at = announcement_date_known_at(process_date)` as for spin-offs, and `--check` (T172b, `health.py`) recomputes it on every row. **The holder, two candidates in order.** (1) The security `new_symbol` resolves to on the first session on or after `process_date` (through the lead, which covers a first span before its cover page), provided that security held `old_symbol` on some session within the `alpaca.actions_process_lag_days` calendar days before `process_date`; this is the renamed company itself, and it is right whatever happened to the old symbol afterwards. (2) Only when `new_symbol` resolves to nothing yet: the old symbol's holder on `previous_session(process_date)`, with one correction for the reused-ticker shape #869 names (PROG→PRG on 2020-12-01, the new Aaron's taking `AAN` the same day, and Alpaca's `process_date` possibly a day later): when that holder's span of `old_symbol` began inside the lag window and another security held the symbol before it, the symbol changed hands inside the window and the rename is the departing holder's, so **the earlier holder is taken**, the row is counted `symbol_change_reused` and listed in the run message for the owner's check (a span that began inside the window for an ordinary reason, an earlier holder delisted weeks before, is the case this can get wrong, which is why it is listed and why candidate (1) comes first). When `new_symbol` resolves to a security that did not hold `old_symbol` in the window, the record is counted `symbol_change_conflict` and not stored; a symbol with no holder in the window is unresolved and counted. Unresolved and not-yet-known records are counted as spin-offs are, and the daily window is `process_date` as for spin-offs. `symbol_changes_as_of(T, security_ids=None)`: the latest revision per identity with `known_at ≤ T`, sorted by `(security_id, process_date, old_symbol)`, every table column. **Writers:** the daily run and the actions backfill only; the month backfill and `--fill-holes` write `corporate_actions` rows (spin-offs included) and drop the parsed name changes. **No consumer:** the price resolver, `repair-resolution`, `--fill-holes` and the master do not read it; #869's choice between 8-K cover pages (a) and these rows (c) for dating a ticker change stays the owner's, and whichever he picks is its own task with its own look-ahead tests. Schema version bump with an additive migration; the table joins the same lists as `borrow_status` and the vintage rule leaves it out.

*Interfaces.* `PriceSource` gains `symbol_changes(ids, start, end)`: the fixture adapter reads `symbol_changes.csv`; the Alpaca adapter makes **its own request** (the per-symbol actions request for `types = name_change` over the range's `process_date` span, parsed by the same parser) and keeps no state between calls, so a caller's order never changes an answer; `corporate_actions()` keeps returning `CorporateAction`s only, spin-offs now among them. `AlpacaPriceSource.last_actions_report` carries the new counts.

*The actions backfill (T172e, T172d).* **Coverage before mid-2020 is unknown, not absent:** the corpact probe (2026-10-11, symbol-less band scans around 150 Form 25 cases) found 2016 empty, 2017 to 2018 one record, 2019 stock mergers only and full coverage from mid-2020, while ADR 0009 (#101) records that the per-symbol request matched every ground-truth spin-off for seven large caps in every year 2016 to 2025; the two methods differ, so a consumer must never infer from an absent row before 2020-06 that no spin-off happened. The backfill therefore starts at **`alpaca.actions_backfill_floor = 2020-06-01`** and refuses an earlier `--since`; a spin-off any writer returns is stored whatever its date. `tradepartner ingest --source alpaca --actions-backfill --since DATE [--through DATE] [--dry-run]`: one calendar month per chunk; per month **one symbol-less request** for `types = spin_off, name_change` over the month's `process_date` range (open question 20; `alpaca_raw.corporate_actions_scan(types, start, end)`, paged to the end as `corporate_actions` is), resolved through the store resolver at the run, written by the rules above in one transaction per month, one run row per month (source `alpaca`, mode `actions`, status `filled`, a cursor resume ignores; never `ok`, so never a fresh ingest); the dry run lists the counts per month and writes nothing. The daily path's per-symbol request is assumed to return a spin-off when either its `source_symbol` or its `new_symbol` is requested; T172d checks it once by comparing one month's daily-path rows with the symbol-less scan's.

*Fixtures (generated, never recorded).* `scripts/make_fixture_universe.py` writes `tests/fixtures/universe/borrow_status.csv` (three snapshots a day apart over six assets: unchanged, ETB→HTB, vanished, new, renamed, vanished then reappeared with its old values) and `symbol_changes.csv` (one rename of a fixture security), and adds one `spinoff` row to `corporate_actions.csv` (a fixture child of a fixture parent, in the convention, stamped by the date-only rule). Parser tests use constructed payloads in Alpaca's shape (`spin_offs`: `source_symbol`, `source_cusip`, `source_rate`, `new_symbol`, `new_cusip`, `new_rate`, `process_date`, `ex_date`; `name_changes`: `old_symbol`, `old_cusip`, `new_symbol`, `new_cusip`, `process_date`); no re-record of `tests/fixtures/alpaca/corporate_actions.json` is needed.

*Validate before building.* Both inputs were validated read-only before this amendment: the recorder's first file (33,636 assets, 5,373 shortable, the field names above) and the corpact probe (coverage by year, the `name_change` bankruptcy hint, 11 requests). T172d's first step on the real store is the AAN/PROG 2020-12-01 name change and one hand-checked spin-off ratio before the full backfill proceeds.

**Amendment 2026-10-09 (#1358): filing events, the 8-K `items` field.** The store gains one row per 8-K in every issuer's submissions record, with the filing's `items` and its acceptance, because the earnings-reaction hypothesis ([b9-earnings-reaction-drift](../hypotheses/b9-earnings-reaction-drift.md), the [short-horizon report](../research/2026-10-09-short-horizon-candidates.md) SH-12) keys on the acceptance of the 8-K whose `items` names 2.02, the quarterly release: the submissions payload carries `items` per filing, and the adapter reads the payload and drops the field (`edgar_source.reduce_submissions` keeps form, accession, primary document, iXBRL flag and acceptance; the stamps cache holds the same five). Nothing else about the filing index, the stamps or the statement facts changes, and no `known_at` moves. The code is plan tasks T164b to T164e, one PR each; this amendment is T164. Of the B9 file's owner questions only B9-6 bears on this table (the event definition: `8-K` only at read time, no 10-Q/10-K fallback), and the owner took the file's recommendation (comment on #1358, 2026-10-09); the answers to B9-1 to B9-7 themselves belong in the hypothesis file's open-questions section, which the B9 family's backtest spec amendment records, not this one.

*Table.* `filing_events(cik, accession, form, items, accepted_at)` plus the four common columns, `provenance = filing` only, `source = edgar`. **UNIQUE `(cik, accession)`**: an accession is EDGAR's permanent id of one submission, never reused, so the table has no revisions by construction (Definitions) and an 8-K/A is another accession and another row; a co-registrant filing (one accession under several CIKs, as `_company_facts` already sees them) is one row per CIK that lists it. Keyed by **CIK**, not `security_id`, as `statement_facts` is (decision (c) of #660): a filing is the issuer's, and the read resolves through the master. `form` is the submissions record's form verbatim (`8-K`, `8-K/A`), and only a form in `edgar.event_forms` (default `["8-K", "8-K/A"]`, never empty) makes a row. `items` is the payload's string **verbatim** (`2.02,9.01`; the empty string when the payload has none, never NULL), so the store does not parse SEC item codes and a consumer splits on commas and matches a whole code (`2.02` never matches by substring); the codes changed on 2004-08-23 (open question 13) and the string keeps whatever the record says. `accepted_at` is the filing's acceptance from the CIK's stamps file, tz-aware UTC, and **`known_at = accepted_at`**, never the index's `filed` date: an 8-K accepted between 16:00 and about 17:30 New York on a session carries that session's `filed` date, so a `filed`-keyed row would be known a session early (the B9 file's rule 1; the count below measures how many). An accession with no stamp record, or settled as unstampable (`accepted_at` None), is never a row; it is counted. Schema version bump with an additive migration (the table is new; no existing row changes), and the table joins the rebuild helper's list.

*Source and the adapter (T164c).* `SubmissionRecord` gains `items`; `reduce_submissions` keeps it, tolerating a payload or an older page without the column: then the record's `items` is `None`, unknown, which is not the empty string (a known empty list), and such an accession is never a row until a payload carrying the column has been read for it (counted `filing_events_items_missing` below). The stamps cache carries `items` as a fifth element under a **new, separate layout constant** (`STAMPS_LAYOUT`), not a `PARSER_VERSION` bump, which also keys the facts and statement caches and would re-stamp every CIK: a stamps file in the old layout is read as it is, with `items` unknown for its accessions, and rewritten in the new layout only when the CIK is next stamped; **every pre-existing stamp (`form`, `accepted_at`, including every `accepted_at = None` sentinel) is byte-identical after the migration**, which T164c tests on the fixture cache and shows on the owner's cache by a read-only diff of every CIK's old and new stamps file. That rule is what "no `known_at` moves" means here: `filing_events` takes the stamp every other filing-derived row of that CIK already carries, so the table can never disagree with `securities.known_at` or a `statement_facts` row about when a filing became public. The adapter answers `filing_events(cik)` from the stamps file: one `FilingEvent` (cik, accession, form, items, accepted_at) per stamped accession whose form is in `edgar.event_forms` and whose `items` is known. **One refresh rule.** A new stamp carries `items` whatever the switch says, because the record already holds it (the stamping paths change nothing else). An accession stamped before the layout change has `items` unknown, and the only thing that fills it is the **refresh**: on the first EDGAR run with `edgar.filing_events_enabled = true`, the fetch pass re-reads each issuer CIK's submissions member **once** (from `submissions.zip` under `--bulk-from-cache`, as the statement facts' first load did; otherwise the per-CIK API, counted as a request) and writes `items` for the accessions already stamped, **touching no existing stamp's `accepted_at`, `form`, document or iXBRL flag** and stamping nothing (an accession the file lacks is stamped only by the existing stamping rules, never by the refresh). A CIK whose stamps file is already in the new layout is refreshed never again: the layout is the once-per-CIK marker, so a run with the switch off re-reads nothing and makes no request for it, and a run with it on re-reads only CIKs still in the old layout. An accession whose payload carries no `items` column at the refresh keeps `items` unknown as a **terminal, counted state** (`filing_events_items_missing`, below): it is never re-fetched and never a row. The refresh also runs the clock detector below on what it reads.

*As-of read (T164d).* `filing_events_as_of(T, forms=None, items=None)` returns the `filing_events` rows with `known_at ≤ T`, joined through `securities_as_of(T)` on `cik` as `statement_facts_as_of` does: **one row per `(security_id, accession)`**, so a dual-class issuer's event appears once per listed class and a CIK with no securities row at T is invisible at T; `forms` filters on `form` exactly, `items` on the split list (any listed code present, whole-code match), `None` meaning no filter; sorted by `(known_at, accession, security_id)`. Columns: `security_id` then every table column in schema order. The look-ahead cases (backtest spec req 13 as #720 extended it): an 8-K accepted after `close(T)` is invisible at T and visible at the next read; the **`filed`-date trap with teeth** on the fixture row accepted between 16:00 and 17:30 New York on session T (its `filed` date equals T): invisible at T's open and at `close(T)`, first visible at T + 1, both of which a `filed`-keyed read fails; the 20:30 row as the lag case (`filed` = T + 1), which a `filed`-keyed read passes by accident and so proves nothing alone; truncation invariance with the table in the truncated set.

*Fixture (T164b).* Three 8-K rows for one fixture issuer: a `2.02,9.01` accepted between 16:00 and 17:30 New York on a session (the trap), a `2.02,9.01` accepted at 20:30 (the lag case), and a `5.02` (not an event for B9). The `FilingEvent` record refuses a naive datetime, as every record does.

*Ingest, config and health (T164e).* The fetch pass asks `filing_events(cik)` for every issuer CIK before the lock (req 1, #173), **full history every run** as the filing index is (`--since` does not limit it); the write phase inserts-or-skips on `(cik, accession)` per CIK inside the run's one transaction, bulk-inserted as `statement_facts` are; a re-run adds zero rows; a dry run writes nothing. **A record whose `accepted_at` is after the run's clock is skipped and counted (`filing_events_not_yet_known`), never clamped and never fatal**: the two integrity rules `known_at = accepted_at` and `known_at ≤ ingested_at` cannot both hold for it, so it has no row until a later run whose clock has passed it (the facts path raises on that case today; this table does not, because a moved stamp (The clock, below) can sit hours ahead of a run soon after the close). The whole path is behind **`edgar.filing_events_enabled`, default `false`**: off, the ingest never asks the question, the adapter makes no request and no cache write for it (the refresh included), and the backfill is unchanged. **The default is flipped only when the real-store run has passed, and it has passed when all four hold:** `health --check` is green with the table's rules; a second run adds zero rows; the quarterly share of universe CIKs with a `2.02` `8-K` row, computed from the store at each quarter-end close of 2020Q3 to 2023Q4 as TP-1 did, is within one percentage point of TP-1's column for every quarter; and the owner has closed #1382 (The clock) with a decision, because until then the table carries early stamps that are a look-ahead for B9. T164e turns the switch on for the owner's store and flips the default in the same PR, as T78 did, under a named data release (ADR 0016 point 7; T140's runbook); if #1382 is still open when T164e is otherwise done, T164e lands with the default `false` and the flip is a later size-S PR. `tradepartner ingest --source edgar --rebuild-filing-events` deletes every row and re-inserts the table from the stamps cache in the run's one transaction, the run row recording the counts before and after, never a back-dated row or a partial fix; a rebuild changes what a past as-of read returned, which the registry's export rule covers as for `statement_facts` (the flag is wired in `cli.py` and tested in `tests/test_cli.py`, which the T164e plan line does not name yet; the orchestrator adds them, or a declared T164f, before T164e is claimed). The run row counts: added, `filing_events_unstamped` (accessions with no stamp or a None one), `filing_events_items_missing` (stamped accessions whose `items` is terminally unknown), `filing_events_not_yet_known`, `filing_events_clock_suspect` (below), and with the rebuild flag the row count deleted. `health.py` (the page renders what `health.py` returns, as for every section; no page change is part of this amendment): the row count and the latest `accepted_at` per form, the last run's counts, with a warning while `filing_events_items_missing > 0` or `filing_events_clock_suspect > 0`, and the switch's state; coverage is reported, not thresholded. Integrity rules for `--check`: the four common-column rules; `known_at = accepted_at` on every row; `form` in `edgar.event_forms`; `items` not NULL; `(cik, accession)` unique. **The clock detector** (a health finding, never a repair, never a pass): at the refresh, and at every later stamping of an issuer's accession, the adapter compares each stamp with the payload's `filingDate` and counts an accession whose stamp fits only a moved clock: accepted on a session before 17:30 New York and dated a later day (the stamp is early), or accepted on a New York date after its filing date (the stamp is late); the accessions are listed on a plain attribute for the run message, as `statement_conflict_keys` are. It catches only the moves that cross the filing-date rule (about a tenth of the early ones, below); the SGML header is the only per-filing truth. **The B9 engine tasks (the file's "The family question") enter the backtest plan by their own amendment and need T164d; nothing here reads the table.**

*The coverage count (T164's first step, read-only; the evidence is on the T164 PR).* On a scratch copy of the owner's store (schema 18) and the cached `submissions.zip` and stamps, `universe_as_of` at the close of the last session of each calendar quarter 2020Q3 to 2023Q4 (14 quarters, from July 2020 rather than the plan's August, so that quarters are whole; 1,000 CIKs each, 1,421 distinct; a name that left the universe inside a quarter is not in that quarter's denominator): **979 to 993 of the 1,000 universe CIKs (97.9% to 99.3%) have an `8-K` whose `items` names 2.02 accepted in the quarter**; 1,014 to 1,125 such filings a quarter (about 4,200 a year; the report estimated about 4,000); 32 to 121 CIKs a quarter file two or more (most in Q1), which B9's dedupe rule exists for; an `8-K/A` carrying 2.02 is at most one issuer a quarter. 988 to 998 CIKs file a 10-Q/10-K in the quarter, and of 14,002 periodic filings 687 (4.9%) have no `8-K` 2.02 in the 60 days before them (701, 5.0%, within 45 days): the names a 10-Q/10-K fallback (B9-6 option c) would add, at a different and later event time, so the file's recommendation (8-K only, no fallback) stands. The lag from the latest 2.02 8-K to the periodic filing: median 1.00 day, p25 0.16, p75 7.4, p90 15.9; 50% within a day, 73% within seven; about a week in Q1 (10-K season), under a day otherwise. Time of day (New York) over the 7,845 events whose stamps the per-CIK API wrote on 2026-10-02 to 10-07, a clock presumed true (it shows neither moved signature, below, and the three fixture CIKs' API stamps match their fixtures) and a non-random subset: **44.9% accepted before 09:30, 2.5% during the session, 49.9% between 16:00 and 17:30 and dated that same session (the `filed`-date trap: half of all events), 2.7% after 17:30**, of which 90% carry the next day's date (the "about 17:30" cutoff holds for 8-Ks; 21 same-day exceptions in 210). The count is recorded as claim TP-1.

*The clock (found by the count; its own issue #1382, open question 11).* In October 2026 the SEC's submissions data serves `acceptanceDateTime` values moved by New York's UTC offset (4 h on EDT dates, 5 h on EST dates), **in both directions, per CIK member, and the set changes between builds.** Late: the 2026-10-08 bulk `submissions.zip` disagrees with the SGML header, the ground truth of [#572](../research/2026-10-02-edgar-data-pitfalls.md) E15, by exactly that offset for the two SGML-verified fixture accessions, and with the 2026-09-25 API fixtures for all 2,560 filings of the three fixture CIKs (13 Alphabet filings by twice the offset); the stamps written from the per-CIK API on 2026-10-02 to 10-07 agree with the fixtures; and the per-CIK API itself answered moved values for Apple on 2026-10-09 (SH-12's example times are 4 h late: the 8-K was accepted 20:30:28Z, 16:30 New York, and the 10-Q 10:01:02Z). Early: the stamps written by the 2026-10-01 bulk backfill equal that day's zip, and among the universe's 11,131 bulk-stamped `8-K` 2.02 events **103 fit only an early stamp** (accepted on a session before 17:30 New York by the stamp, dated the next day by the index; for example CIK 0000077877's 0000077877-21-000080, stamped 13:40 on 2021-11-03, filed 11-04), from 34 CIKs whose whole 2.02 history carries the early signature (stamps at 01:00-05:59 and 11:00-13:59 New York, none at 16:00-17:30); the same signature in the rest of that group (741 stamps at 01:00-05:59 and 1,039 at 11:00-12:59, against 5 and 52 among the API-stamped events, and no excess at 20:00-21:59) puts the early share at about an eighth to a sixth of the bulk-stamped events by estimate, which the SGML header alone can settle per filing. **An early stamp is a look-ahead**: an after-close release stamped 11:00-13:30 of session T is visible at `close(T)`, so a B9 read at `close(T)` would hold the announcement gap, in the direction the signal bets; a late stamp is not (it moves a pre-open 8-K's day 0 one session later). Every filing-derived `known_at` of those CIKs (`securities`, `statement_facts` and the rest) carries the same stamps, so an H1 or B3 month-end read on a filing day is exposed too, in a small way. `filing_events` takes the stamps as they are, so the table never disagrees with the rest of the store, and one rebuild carries a repaired cache into it; the repair, which is look-ahead work and not hygiene, its scope, the registry note on which logged trials to re-run after it, and the guard on stamps written now (both the bulk path from today's zip and the per-CIK API would write moved values) are the owner's call on #1382; **until it is closed the switch stays `false` and no trial reads the table** (the T164e rule above). Claim TP-2 records the finding; EP-P12 ("acceptance times come only from submissions, true UTC") stays the rule for *which field* is read and no longer describes the values.

**Amendment 2026-10-11 (#1498, [ADR 0020](../decisions/0020-fred-risk-free-rate.md)): the `rates` table and the FRED rate source.** The store gains the 3-month T-bill rate as a point-in-time series, because the backtest's Sharpe reads a constant `metrics.risk_free_rate = 0.0` (backtest spec req 7) and the two reports that need a cash rate ([B11](../research/2026-10-10-b11-beta-alpha-split.md), [beyond long-only](../research/2026-10-10-beyond-long-only.md) step 0) had to fetch FRED's `DTB3` by hand. The source, the terms and the options are the ADR's; the metrics' use of the table is the [backtest spec amendment of the same date](backtest.md#amendment-2026-10-11-1498-the-risk-free-series-from-the-store). The code is plan tasks T169 to T170d; this amendment and the ADR are the class-B part.

*Table.* `rates(series, observation_date, value, vintage_date)` plus the four common columns; `source` is the adapter's name (`fred`; the fallback adapter the ADR names writes its own), `provenance = release` (Definitions: a published statistical release whose source gives a release date and no time). `series` is the source's series id verbatim (`DTB3`); `observation_date` the business day the rate is for; `value` the published number in the series' own unit, a DOUBLE (DTB3: percent, discount basis; the table converts nothing, the consumer does); `vintage_date` the date the source first served that value for that day. **UNIQUE `(series, observation_date, known_at)`.** A missing value (FRED's `.`: a bank holiday, a day not yet posted) is never a row; it is counted (`rates_missing_values`). Schema version bump with an additive migration (the table is new; no existing row changes); the table joins `TABLE_NAMES`, `TABLE_PROVENANCE_VALUES` (`release` only), the truncation harness's fact tables and the export. It is **left out of the data vintage** (strategy-lab spec, Definitions "Vintage", amended 2026-10-11): no trial registered before it existed read it, and a backfill whose rows carry 2016 to 2026 `known_at`s would otherwise stale every logged trial on the day it lands, one rerun each, every rerun counting in N, for a table none of them read. What that gives up: a row that lands with a past `known_at` after a `store` trial ran, a revision, a hole the lapse rule below refetches, or a backfill re-run, changes what that trial reads and marks nothing stale. DTB3 has no revisions (the probe), the lapse rule leaves no hole for longer than one run, and health counts both kinds of row (`rates_revisions`, `rates_late_rows`, below) and warns while the last run wrote any, which is the owner's cue to decide whether the trials that read the series rerun (the trial's frozen set says which read `store`).

*`known_at`.* `known_at = calendar.session_close(calendar.next_session(vintage_date))`: the close of the first XNYS session strictly after the vintage date, a pure function in `adapters/rates.py` (`rate_known_at`) that the ingest applies; **adapters stamp nothing** (ADR 0003 rule 2: the timing rule lives in the core). The one-day posting lag reads as follows: an observation for business day D is published by the Board at 16:15 Eastern on D+1 (H.15's schedule; the ADR's probe saw the same lag on FRED), its FRED vintage date is D+1, and the rule stamps it at close(D+2). Why this rule and not another (ADR 0020 point 2, in short): FRED gives a date and no time, and 16:15 is after close(D+1), so close(D+1) would be early; the end of D+1 in New York time is a second rule and can be early by an hour against FRED's Central-time day; the open of D+2 and the close of D+2 are the same stamp for every read the system makes (the metrics read at a rebalance close; the paper path does not read this table), and the close is the rule req 5 already has for a corporate action known by date only. The cost is one session of staleness on a series that moves by basis points a week. The stamp is recomputable from `vintage_date`, so `health --check` recomputes it on every row. `vintage_date` is the FRED observation's `realtime_start` **as returned**: FRED bounds it below by the request's `realtime_start`, so when the two are equal the stored date is a lower bound on the real vintage, which can only make `known_at` later, never earlier; the daily request below is shaped so that a value first served on day V is asked for with a `realtime_start` before V and keeps V. **Not yet known:** a row the rule would stamp after the run's clock (the observation for D, fetched on the evening of D+1, stamped close(D+2)) is skipped and counted (`rates_not_yet_known`), never clamped and never fatal, exactly as `filing_events_not_yet_known` is; the next run stores it with its true vintage. So after the run on the evening of D+1 the store's latest observation is D−1, which is what a reader at close(D+1) was entitled to. `ingested_at` is the run's clock after the lock, and `known_at ≤ ingested_at` holds on every stored row by the skip.

*Vintages are revisions.* For a returned (series, observation_date, value, vintage_date) the ingest writes a row iff the stamp is at or before the run's clock and the store has no row for `(series, observation_date)` in effect at the record's stamp, or the row in effect there (the stored row for that key whose `known_at` is the greatest at or before the record's stamp) holds a different value; a record whose exact `(observation_date, known_at)` key is already stored is never written again (a different value there is counted `rates_colliding_vintages`; within one fetch, two values for one key keep the later vintage and count the same way); otherwise nothing (*wording 2026-10-18, #1602, after #1594: "the latest revision" would have skipped an earlier vintage that re-states a value a later vintage already holds, and written a later vintage that repeats the value in effect*). A written row whose key already has one is a **revision**, counted (`rates_revisions`); it carries its own vintage's stamp, never the first row's and never the run's clock (the vintage date is the source's own statement of when the value changed, unlike a re-fetched bar, whose source says nothing, req 1). Rows are never updated or deleted; `rates_as_of` takes the latest revision per `(series, observation_date)` among rows with `known_at ≤ T`, so a reader between the two stamps sees the first value and a reader after the second sees the second.

*Source and requests (`fred_raw`, `fred_rates`).* `FRED_API_KEY` (`Settings.fred_api_key`, `SecretStr`, read only by the raw client; `ingest --source fred` refuses and names it when blank, as the EDGAR user agent is refused) travels as the `api_key` query parameter of `https://api.stlouisfed.org/fred/series/observations`, with `file_type=json`, `output_type=1` (observations by real-time period: one row per observation and value, with the real-time window in which that value was current), `limit` at its 100,000 maximum and `offset` paging when the response's `count` exceeds it. The raw client never logs a URL or a query string, and keeps the `httpx` and `httpcore` loggers from doing so either (their INFO request line carries the full URL: the client installs a filter on both, or holds them at WARNING, for the life of its requests); a transport or status error is re-raised as the client's own error naming the endpoint and the status, `from None`, so no chained `httpx` exception carries the URL into a traceback; the recorder's scrub (`cli_record.scrub_text`) covers an `api_key=` query value; no payload is recorded as a fixture (ADR 0020 point 5: nothing from FRED enters the repository; the fixture adapter reads a generated CSV). The parser (`fred_rates.parse_observations`) turns the payload into `RateObservation(series, observation_date, value, vintage_date)` records, `vintage_date` the row's `realtime_start`, and drops a `.` value into the missing count; a payload without `observations`, a date it cannot parse or a non-numeric value other than `.` raises, as every other schema drift does. Only the parser is unit-tested, on a constructed payload in the API's shape.

*Ingest.* `tradepartner ingest --source fred` (and `--source all`), one source-level atomic chunk per series per run as the other sources have (req 9), each series its own `ingestion_runs` row (`source = fred`, `mode` as the other sources). **Backfill** (`--backfill --since DATE --source fred`): per series one request, `realtime_start = since`, `realtime_end = 9999-12-31`, `observation_start = since`, paged; every returned record stamped by the rule, the not-yet-known ones skipped and counted; the cursor is `rates;series=<id>;through=<latest observation stored>` and resume is a re-run (insert-or-skip on the key makes it idempotent: a completed backfill re-run adds zero rows). Because of the bound above, the first stored observation is the one for `since` itself, with vintage `since`+1 business day and `known_at` close(`since`+2 sessions): a consumer that needs a rate at an earlier session gets none (the backtest spec's `risk_free_missing`), so the owner's backfill starts at 2016-01-04, ADR 0009's first bar, and the first registered period that reads it starts a month later. **Daily:** per series one request with `realtime_start = observation_start = W` and `realtime_end = today`, where today is the New York date of the run's clock and **W is the earlier of `today − rates.refetch_days` (calendar days; default 10) and the day after the series' latest stored `observation_date`**, so a lapse in daily runs, however long, is refetched whole by the next run and leaves no hole; a store with no row at all for the series refuses the daily run and names the backfill command (a daily run never backfills by accident). The window re-returns the recent values with their true vintages (a value first served on V has `realtime_start = V` when V is inside the window), so a record skipped yesterday as not yet known is stored today with its vintage, and a revision inside the window lands as a revision row with the vintage FRED gives it; a value whose true vintage is before W comes back with `realtime_start = W`, the lower bound above, so a hole refetched late is stamped late, never early. `--since` does not apply to a daily run. **Staleness** (req 10's idea, source-level): the latest observation the source *returned* this run (stored or skipped) must be no more than `rates.max_stale_sessions` (default 3) XNYS sessions before the run's expected session (the calendar's last completed session minus `ingest.settle_delay_minutes`, as for bars): the normal lag is one business day, a bank holiday that is an exchange session (Columbus Day, Veterans Day) adds one, and the next-session rule adds none because the check reads what was returned, not what was stored. A stale series halts non-zero for the source with only its run row written, as req 10 does. A `--dry-run` fetches and writes nothing to the fact table. The run row counts: `rows_added`, `rates_revisions`, `rates_not_yet_known`, `rates_missing_values`, `rates_late_rows` (rows a daily run writes whose `known_at` is more than `rates.max_stale_sessions` sessions before the run's expected session: a refetched hole or a late revision; a backfill writes late rows by design and does not count them), and the latest observation and vintage returned.

*Health.* `health.py` and the page gain a rates block, per series: row count, latest `observation_date`, latest `vintage_date`, latest `known_at`, the number of revised keys (`(series, observation_date)` with more than one row), the last run's counts above and its staleness, the longest gap in XNYS sessions between consecutive observation dates, and a **warning** while the revised-key count is above zero, the last daily run's `rates_late_rows` is above zero, the longest gap exceeds `rates.max_stale_sessions`, or a value lies outside `rates.plausible_range_percent` (default `[-1.0, 25.0]`: the lowest T-bill prints were a few basis points negative in 2015 and 2020, the highest in the series' history about 16% in 1981; a value outside the band is a unit or parse error until the owner says otherwise; a warning, never a `--check` failure, because a real print is a fact). The block carries the attribution FRED's API terms require ("This product uses the FRED® API but is not endorsed or certified by the Federal Reserve Bank of St. Louis."), and the README carries the same sentence under "Where things live". `last_ingests` lists `fred` beside the others. Integrity rules for `--check`: the four common-column rules; `provenance = release`; `known_at = rate_known_at(vintage_date)` on every row; `(series, observation_date, known_at)` unique; `value` finite. Coverage is reported, not thresholded.

*Point in time.* `rates_as_of(T, series=None)` (`store/asof.py`) returns, for the named series (default every stored one), the latest revision per `(series, observation_date)` among rows with `known_at ≤ T`: columns `series`, `observation_date`, `value`, `vintage_date`, `known_at`, sorted by `(series, observation_date)`; a bare date raises as everywhere. The frame carries `known_at` so a consumer can filter it again at an earlier instant inside the same read (the backtest spec's per-session rule), and the two are equal by construction. Truncation invariance holds with `rates` in the truncated set, with probes just before and just after every distinct `known_at` of the fixture (the T6 harness). The broken adapter gains a rate stamped at close(`observation_date`), the day it describes, and a named check (`early_rate_stamp`) catches it; one stamped at the vintage date's own close is caught by the same check.

*Fixture (generated, never recorded).* `tests/fixtures/universe/rates.csv`, table-level rows from `scripts/make_fixture_universe.py`: a daily `DTB3`-shaped series that starts at least five business days before the fixture's first session (so a rate is known at every fixture session's close, and a registration that reads `store` runs on the fixture store from its first period) and runs through the fixture window, with (a) ordinary observations D with vintage D+1 (`known_at` = close(D+2)); (b) a vintage dated a Friday, stamped at Monday's close; (c) the fixture's exchange holiday, with no observation and the next session's accrual covering it; (d) a bank-holiday gap of two business days with no observation; (e) one observation with two vintages and two values (a revision), the second stamped at its own vintage's next close; (f) a vintage dated a Saturday, stamped at Monday's close. The fixture `RateSource` (`adapters/fixture_rates.py`) serves the CSV's observations by `(realtime_start, observation_start)` so the ingest's own rules, not the adapter's, make the rows.

*Terms (ADR 0020 point 5).* Rows are the owner's personal, non-commercial copy; nothing from FRED is redistributed: no row, payload or recorded fixture in the public repository, the Parquet export and backups private as they are for Alpaca bars. Revisit per the ADR if the Bank objects or the API changes. **Termination** (the API terms: "destroy all copies"): the `rates` rows written by `source = fred` are deleted under a named data release ([data-releases runbook](../runbooks/data-releases.md) rule 1 covers a hand fix that deletes rows; the backup is listed so the deletion can be walked), the only deletion this table allows; because the table is outside the data vintage, the release's `after` record lists the trials whose frozen set reads `store` as the ones that no longer reproduce, and the owner decides their rerun; exports and backups made before the release are deleted with the rows. **The fallback adapter** (the Board's H.15 download, option 2 of the ADR) has no vintage: it writes `source = h15` and `vintage_date` = the first business day after `observation_date` (the H.15 release that first carried the value; the Board posts each business day with the prior day's rates), a derived date the adapter's docstring says so (ADR 0003 rule 2's clause for a source with no real `known_at`), so `known_at` follows the same rule and `--check` recomputes it the same way; what it cannot see is a late posting, which FRED's vintage would show.

**Interfaces** (`src/tradepartner/adapters/`): `PriceSource.bars(ids, start, end)`, `.corporate_actions(ids, start, end)`; `FilingSource.filing_index(since)`, `.companies_snapshot()`, `.facts(cik, names)`, `.statement_facts(cik)` (#660: `StatementFactRecord` = cik, fact_name, xbrl_tag, period_start, period_end, value, unit, form, accession, accepted_at (None for an accession with no stamp record), filed (the companyfacts date, read by the ingest's hold rule only, never stored), comparative, reported values only; the parser keys and flags, the adapter stamps, emits unstamped entries rather than dropping them, and withholds conflicts onto its `statement_conflict_keys` list (accession, fact, period, values), the ingest keeps the first vintage, holds and derives gross profit), `.filing_events(cik)` (#1358: `FilingEvent` = cik, accession, form, items (the verbatim string), accepted_at, all set; an accession with no stamp or a settled-unstampable one is never a record, it is counted), `.filing_headers(cik, forms)`, `.cover_pages(cik)`, `.delistings(since)`; `RateSource.observations(series, realtime_start, observation_start)` (#1498: `RateObservation` = series, observation_date, value, vintage_date, all set; a missing value is never a record, it is counted; the adapter stamps nothing, the core does); raw clients `alpaca_raw.*`, `edgar_raw.*`, `fred_raw.*` return raw payloads; `Broker.submit/cancel/positions/fills`.

**Config keys**: `store.path`, `store.lock_retry_seconds=60`, `ingest.settle_delay_minutes=60`, `ingest.reference_symbol=SPY`, `ingest.max_missing_share=0.05`, `ingest.max_dark_share=1.0` (#796), `alpaca.max_catchup_sessions=5` (#1572, from #1550), `edgar.cache_dir`, `master.transfer_window_sessions=5`, `master.issuer_forms=[10-K,10-Q,8-K,20-F,40-F,S-1,F-1,10-12B,25,25-NSE]`, `master.static_columns=[name,ticker,exchange]`, `benchmarks=[SPY,MTUM]`, `execution.fill_price=close`, `alpaca.historical_feed=sip` (T3), `universe.security_types=[common]`, `universe.exchanges=[NYSE,NASDAQ,NYSE_AMERICAN]`, `universe.exclude_sic_ranges=[]` (amendment #1540, from plan task T176; `[[4900,4999]]` and guarded before it), `universe.min_price=5`, `universe.liquidity_rule_enabled=true` (T3; was `false`, `universe.min_median_dollar_volume=5_000_000`, `universe.liquidity_window=20`, `universe.min_history_months=12`, `universe.max_shares_age_days=400`, `universe.top_n_by_cap=1000`, `gap.missing_tail_sessions=5`, `gap.count_share_threshold=0.05`. Amendment #660: `edgar.statement_facts_enabled=false` (T78 flips it), `edgar.statement_tags` (the five ordered fallback lists above), `edgar.statement_forms=[10-K,10-Q,10-K/A,10-Q/A,10-KT,10-QT]`, `edgar.statement_units=[USD]`. Amendment #787: `universe.max_jump_ratio=2.5`, `universe.min_jump_ratio=0.4`, `universe.accepted_price_jumps=[]` (the issue's thresholds; the accepted list is the owner's). Amendment #845: `universe.max_shares_ratio=100`, `universe.accepted_shares_facts=[]` (the owner's). Amendment #855: `universe.accepted_same_day_pairs=[]` (the owner's; read by `health` only). Amendment #1199: `gap.stale_listing_sessions=63` (proposed in the ADR 0003 amendment; lands in `config.py` with step 2 of #1199, frozen with `gap.*`, with the `FROZEN_KEY_DEFAULTS` entry the owner's answer to the ADR's open question 1 decides). Amendment #1358: `edgar.filing_events_enabled=false` (T164e flips it once the real-store run has passed, as T78 did), `edgar.event_forms=[8-K,8-K/A]` (never empty; a form outside it is never a row). Amendment #1575: `health.min_session_rows_share=0.0` (an XNYS session in the `universe.min_history_months` window whose `prices_daily` rows known at `t` are below this share of the previous session's fails the `session_holes` rule and `health --check`; a session with no row at all always fails it, and a session after the latest stored one is staleness, not a hole).

Amendment #1498: `rates.series=[DTB3]` (the series ingested, each a FRED series id), `rates.max_stale_sessions=3`, `rates.refetch_days=10`, `rates.plausible_range_percent=[-1.0, 25.0]` (a warning band, never a `--check` rule); the metrics-side keys are the backtest spec's (`metrics.risk_free_source`, `metrics.risk_free_series`, `metrics.risk_free_basis`).

**Amendment 2026-10-11 (#1540): utilities enter the universe.** The [ADR 0006 amendment of the same date](../decisions/0006-universe-and-cadence.md#amendment-2026-10-11-utilities-enter-the-universe-1540) drops rule 3 (the SIC 4900-4999 exclusion, mis-recorded as a compliance decision) and guard (b); its nine properties are the acceptance criteria of plan task T176, and this paragraph names the code. The family rules (strategy-lab spec, Definitions) are unchanged: every family registered before T176 keeps `[[4900, 4999]]` in `family_rules.fixed_params`, a later file in it must name that value or is refused by name (ADR property 9), and a family first registered after T176 fixes `[]` unless its first file names otherwise. (1) `config.py`: `UniverseConfig.exclude_sic_ranges` defaults to `()`; `_GUARDED_EXCLUDE_SIC_RANGES` and the `_guard_exclude_sic_ranges` validator go; a shape validator (each range `start <= end`, as `ProfitabilityConfig` has) takes their place, so an environment override or a hypothesis file may name any well-formed ranges and a registration storing `[[4900, 4999]]` still loads through `load_frozen`; the module docstring's "guarded" paragraphs and `alpaca.paper`'s comparison are rewritten (`alpaca.paper` stays guarded on its own terms, paper-trading spec req 2). (2) `health.py`: the `guarded_sic_default` rule, `_guarded_sic` and the import go (req 11 as amended above); `_GUARD_SCHEMA` stays only if another rule reads it. (3) `universe.py`: rule 3 keeps its code and its `sector` reason, which counts zero rows under the default; the docstring says so. (4) No `FROZEN_KEY_DEFAULTS` entry: the key is a `LAB_BASELINE_FROZEN_KEYS` member, stored by every registration, so no overlay applies and no stored set, hash or fingerprint moves. (5) `.env.example` gains a commented `UNIVERSE__EXCLUDE_SIC_RANGES='[]'` line beside the `profitability` one. Acceptance (T176's tests): the default is `()`; `[[4900,4999]]`, `[[4900,4949]]`, `[[4900,4999],[6000,6099]]` and `[[1,2]]` load from `Settings` and from the environment (`tests/test_universe_config.py`'s `GUARDED_KEYS` and its two guard tests become those cases) and `[[2,1]]` is refused (`tests/backtest/test_sweep.py`'s fixed-block case, which today uses `[[1, 2]]` and relied on the guard, uses `[[2, 1]]`); `load_frozen` on a fixture registration storing `[[4900, 4999]]` returns it under the new default, and a fixture rebalance on that registration still excludes the fixture's SIC 4911 name (`tests/test_universe.py::test_utility_sic_fails_rule_3` becomes that case, run under an explicit range, and a second case shows the same name admitted under the default with zero `sector` rows in the gap report); `frozen_params` and `fingerprint` of the H1 file under live defaults (computed, not registered: a lab store refuses the standalone file) give `[]` and a fingerprint other than `H1_FINGERPRINT`, while every pinned value (`H1_*`, `TWIN_*`, `COMBINED_*`, `B3_*`, `ORACLE_TWIN_*`, `TWIN_CALL_LOG_SHA256`, the reference metrics) is unchanged because those tests register, compute or run under settings naming `[[4900, 4999]]`; on the fixture lab store a `momentum` file omitting the key is refused naming `universe.exclude_sic_ranges`, the same file naming `[[4900, 4999]]` registers, and the H1 twin's unchanged file is refused instead of returning its record: by name from `sweep register` (the family-rule comparison, `tests/backtest/test_sweep_register.py`) and by the standalone or fingerprint refusal, which does not name the key, from `hypothesis register` (`test_hypothesis.py`; *wording 2026-10-18, #1547*); `health --check` has no `guarded_sic_default` rule and passes on the fixture store; `tests/lookahead/` universe suites unchanged in what they assert. `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` unchanged; no store row changes; no hypothesis or sweep file under `docs/` is edited (frozen once registered).

**Env vars**: `ALPACA_API_KEY`, `ALPACA_API_SECRET`, `SEC_EDGAR_USER_AGENT`, `FRED_API_KEY` (#1498; `Settings.fred_api_key`, a `SecretStr`, read only by the FRED raw client).

**CLI**: `tradepartner ingest [--source alpaca|edgar|fred|all]` (#1498: `fred` is a source; `all` includes it) `[--bulk-from-cache] [--rebuild-statement-facts] [--rebuild-filing-events]` (#660: reuse the cached `companyfacts.zip` with no request for it; delete and re-ingest the `statement_facts` table in the run's transaction; #1358: the same for `filing_events`, from the stamps cache), `health [--check]`, `repair-resolution [--dry-run]` (#819), `master-retract [--apply --expect-rows N --expect-digest D [--allow-traded]]` (#859), `dashboard`, `export` (Parquet of every table), and `python -m tradepartner.cli_record` (owner-run recorder, before the CLI exists; #660: its company-facts trimmer keeps the configured statement tags).

**Amendment 2026-10-11 (#1541): cover-page listings under the exchange axis, placeholder and issuer-name titles, and a coverage check against an outside reference.** The 2026-10-11 read-only coverage check (owner question "let's see what we are missing and why"; the full report, which uses vendor rows, stays in the owner's local research library) found that the two cover-page parsers drop every listing a filer tags by exchange, and that nothing in `health` could have caught it, because every check compared the store with itself. The owner chose the fix on #1541 ("go with fix"). Five items, in the order they land: (1) the parse fix with its re-derive under a named release, (2) the title fallback, (3) a standing coverage check, (4) the ticker-reuse refetch for the old holders, as an owner run inside the same release, (5) the failed-banks disclosure. The plan's amendment of the same date sizes them (T178 to T182). **Nothing changes in the store until the release:** the new rules sit behind two `edgar` switches, default `false`, that the release's own PR flips together with the cache version bumps, so a nightly ingest after a plain merge of the build tasks re-derives nothing (the `statement_facts_enabled` and `filing_events_enabled` pattern). The release lands **before any H1 re-run or holdout spend**.

*The defect (item 1).* A listed class is one cover-page context holding `dei:Security12bTitle`, `dei:TradingSymbol` and `dei:SecurityExchangeName`. Both parsers accept a context dimensioned by nothing or by `us-gaap:StatementClassOfStockAxis` and skip every other axis: `parse_cover_page` returns such a context in `other_contexts` (it was written for a co-registrant's `dei:LegalEntityAxis`), and the FSN path's `_fsn_segments` returns `None` for any segment naming an axis other than `ClassOfStock`. The DEI taxonomy's own `dei:EntityListingsExchangeAxis` ("this class on this exchange", member an ISO MIC such as `exch:XNGS`) is therefore a reason to drop the listing. Evidence, all EDGAR and store rows, read-only on 2026-10-11: ANSYS's 10-Q instance tags all three listing facts in the context `FD2019Q2YTD_dei_EntityListingsExchangeAxis_exch_XNGS` and nothing else, and its FSN cache records hold `listings: []` on every 10-K and 10-Q; in the owner's `fsn/v2` cache 3,111 of the 11,421 CIKs with a 2019-or-later cover-page filing have no extracted listing at all (most are registrants with no listed class; the large ones below are not). In the store copy taken before `restamp-1382a`, IBM (0000051143), AbbVie, Abbott and 3M each have exactly one listing, `snapshot_static`, `known_at` 2026-10-04T02:16:55Z; ANSYS, Allstate, Fifth Third, Whirlpool and US Steel have a `securities` row and no listing; P&G's primary id `0000080424` carries the `1.375% Notes due 2025` (`PG25`) from 2022-10-19, because its common is tagged by exchange and its notes are not, so the first class the builder saw was a note (the partial variant). The consequences follow from rules already in this spec: an as-of read before the snapshot's `known_at` sees no listing (universe rule 2, `not_listed`), a name delisted before the snapshot has no listing at all, so its Form 25 lands in `unmatched` and `_window_names` fetches no bars for it; and `master.py`'s snapshot fallback attaches nothing to a CIK with several tickers in one fetch.

*The rule.* Under `edgar.exchange_axis_listings` (default `false`; a keyword on the pure parsers, wired by `edgar_source`), both parsers read a context dimensioned by `dei:EntityListingsExchangeAxis` as a listing context. The allowed axis set is {the class axis, the exchange axis}; any other axis still goes to `other_contexts` (per document) or is dropped (FSN), as today. Per document, each context stays one listing, so a class on two exchanges is still two contexts. In FSN, the listing group key becomes (class member, exchange member), so one class on two exchanges is two groups and never "two values for `SecurityExchangeName`"; the class member stays the `ClassOfStock` member, or `""` when the context has none; the `�` guard that fails an undecodable `ClassOfStock` segment closed covers the exchange axis too. The exchange written is still `normalize_exchange(SecurityExchangeName)`: the axis member is a grouping key, not a source. A context whose member MIC names another exchange than its `SecurityExchangeName` value is counted on the run message (`exchange_axis_mismatch`) and the value wins, the shape of owner decision #224 (skip and count, never fail a filing for a cover-page slip): **open question 1 below**. A shares fact in an exchange-axis context is read with its class member; a context pair with equal values collapses, different values fail closed as today, which can newly fail a filing that parses today: the release's dry run reports every new failure against the v2 run and stops on a universe CIK (T181). With the switch off, every parse is byte-identical to today's. The switch's PR (T181) also sets `FSN_VERSION` to 3 (every period since `edgar.fsn_first_year` is re-downloaded and re-extracted on the next EDGAR ingest, about 47 zips of hundreds of MB each, deleted after extraction) and `COVER_VERSION` to 3 (every per-document cover-page parse, which `_cover_sources` serves before the FSN row, is re-fetched and re-parsed; the lag window's recent 10-Qs are among them); the PR states both costs. No SIC or statement row changes with the re-extraction, and the release checks it. The FSN spelling of the axis in `dim.tsv` is pinned from one real period's file, quoted in the PR, never guessed.

*Point in time: what the re-derive restores, and what it does not.* A listing key the builder derives for the first time (IBM's `(0000051143, IBM, NYSE, 2019-07-30)`-shaped rows) is written with `known_at` at that filing's acceptance (the stamps cache, after the #1382 repair) and `ingested_at` at the run: what was public then becomes readable then, honest bitemporal stamping. The data vintage at every cutoff after the earliest such acceptance moves, so every registered trial goes stale, store-wide; that is intended, and it is why the release is ordered before any H1 re-run. Two things the same ingest rules do **not** restore, stated so no evidence line expects them: (i) a *filed* row whose key already exists keeps its own `known_at` only when it is later than every stored row of the key; otherwise one row with the new values is stamped at the run (ingest module docstring, "Filed rows"). Classifications are keyed by `security_id` alone, so item 2's reclassification of a name that already has a `title_unrecognized` row (Shockwave, HD Supply, MGM Growth Properties, Sculptor) lands at the run, and every as-of read before it still says `unclassifiable`: forward-only, unless the rows are rebuilt (T182, **open question 5**). (ii) Where the old rule mis-assigned a primary id (P&G's note under `0000080424`), the corrected build derives the common under the primary id from 2019 and the note under its class id; the stored note row on the primary id is `underived` (#859) and `master-retract` withdraws it, stamped at the correcting run, never back-dated. `_current_listing` picks the latest `valid_from` on or before the session, so at every session from 2022-10-19 until the release P&G's primary id still reads as the note (`PG25`), and the primary id's stored `title_debt` row keeps the same span unless rebuilt: a known residual, named in the evidence, **open question 4**. The `snapshot_static` rows IBM and the others carry today stay: the earliest cover page's ticker and exchange match the snapshot's, so the static span before 2019 is still derived (snapshot listings, above), and the new `filing` rows from 2019 on are what as-of reads before 2026-10-04 now see.

*Titles (item 2).* Classification rule 6 reads a title's head, up to the first comma, and calls anything it does not recognise `unclassifiable` (`title_unrecognized`), while the resolver's `listing_kind` already reads the same titles as equity, so such a name has bars and no universe membership. In the store copy, of the securities whose latest classification is `title_unrecognized`: 124 securities carry a placeholder title (over the `filing` listing rows: `NONE` on 87 rows, `N/A` on 64, and `NA`, `NO`, `NONW`, `0.01`), 5 a blank one, 13 an issuer-name-first title with the class after the comma (`ShockWave Medical Inc., common stock, par value $0.001 per share`; `HD Supply Holdings, Inc. common stock, par value $0.01 per share`; `BioLife Solutions, Inc. Common Shares`), several a class-letter title (`Class A Shares` for MGM Growth Properties and Sculptor), and 225 something else, many of them genuine non-equity (depositary preferred shares spelt "Depository", trust preferred, ETF shares). Under `edgar.title_fallback` (default `false`), rule 6 changes in four ways, in this order, before `title_unrecognized`. (a) A null, blank or placeholder title (its normalised head has no letter, or is in the module constant `_PLACEHOLDER_TITLES`: `none`, `n a`, `na`, `no`, `nonw`; a word list beside `_TITLE_TYPES`, not a tunable, so no frozen key is added) does not count as a title: `_listing_timeline` keeps the class's last real title as `titled`, so a later placeholder never masks it; a class with no real title falls to rule 7 (ticker suffix) and then to rule 8 (`common_default`), but rule 8 applies only when no other class of the CIK has a title naming common, so a placeholder-titled note or preferred beside a titled common stays `unclassifiable`. (b) The type words match whole words (`\bunits?\b`, `\bnotes?\b`, so "United Therapeutics Corporation, Common Stock" is no longer a unit; "depository" joins "depositary"). (c) A head no word matches is re-read over the whole title, comma segment by comma segment: the first segment naming a type word gives `title_<type>`, else the first naming a common word gives `title_common`, never for a title containing `%` (a coupon instrument "convertible into Common Stock" stays out). (d) A head that names only a class letter and shares (`class a shares`), with none of the non-equity words anywhere in the title (the `_TITLE_TYPES` words, `%`, `beneficial interest`, `limited partner`, `trust`), is `title_common`. "Shares of Beneficial Interest" alone stays `title_unrecognized`, as ADR 0006 notes; "Commorn Stock" stays too. Step (d) reads an LLC's "Class A shares, representing limited liability company interests" as common, as an MLP's "Common Units" is a unit today: both are the open owner questions of PR #122, unchanged. With the switch off, every classification is identical to today's. Shares facts are keyed by class member and never by title, so they cannot stop when a title arrives; T179 pins that on a Shockwave-shaped fixture, and the release's evidence counts the facts per year for the named names and lists every class on a `universe.exchanges` exchange with bars that the steps move to common.

*The coverage check (item 3).* `health` gains a `coverage_reference` block, computed against a named list outside the store's derivation and cross-checked against the companies snapshot. Config section `coverage`: `reference_names` (entries `CIK:TICKER[:FROM[:TO]]`, the sessions the entry applies on; the default is a list of large, long-listed US common stocks the owner may edit, by ticker here and with the CIKs pinned in T180's PR from `company_tickers_exchange.json`: AAPL, MSFT, AMZN, GOOGL, META, BRK.B, JNJ, JPM, XOM, UNH, V, HD, CVX, MRK, KO, PEP, PFE, CSCO, WMT, BAC, DIS, TMO, and the live names this check found, IBM, ABBV, ABT, MMM, ALL, FITB, WHR; a name delisted since, ANSYS or US Steel, is not in the default list and is checked by the release's evidence instead; P&G enters as `0000080424:PG::2022-09-30` and, after the release, a second entry from the release's session, open question 4), `sample_months` (default `[6, 12]`), `sample_from` (default `2019-12-31`, after the 2019 cover-page ramp), `check_enabled` (default `false`: report-only until the owner flips it in T181). The sample sessions are the last XNYS session of each sample month from `sample_from` to the last completed session at `t`, from the calendar. For each sample session S, at `t`: (a) `reference_missing`: the reference names whose window covers S, absent from `universe_as_of(close(S))`, each with the universe's exclusion reason; (b) `late_known`: the securities listed on S by the rows known at `t` (`valid_from ≤ S`, not ended on or before S by `listing_ends_as_of` at `t`) that have no row covering S with `known_at ≤ close(S)`: names the store lists on S that no read at S could see, as a count and as a share of the names listed on S (a name with a back-dated `snapshot_static` row and a `filing` row known before S is not late); (c) the member count on S, and, on the latest S only, the count of CIKs with a `snapshot` or `snapshot_static` listing on a `universe.exchanges` exchange known at `t` (the `company_tickers_exchange.json` rows: a store cross-check against the SEC's current list, not an independent source) and the ratio of the two, reported with no threshold. The rule `coverage_reference_names` is reported from the block, not from `INTEGRITY_RULES` (whose every rule is emitted on every run, with no skipped state): `HealthReport.failures` includes it only when the block was computed and (a) is non-empty on some sample session, so `health --check` fails on it only while `check_enabled`; the page shows "not computed" otherwise, never a pass it did not check. The cost is one `universe_as_of` per sample session (about fourteen today), so the block is computed only when `health` is run with `--coverage` or while `check_enabled` is on; the page's card computes it on demand, never on every refresh; T180's PR states the time on the fixture store and T181's evidence the time on the owner's store.

*The ticker-reuse refetch (item 4).* No code. In the store copy, ten old holders have exactly one listing, zero bars and a Form 25 (`delistings.effective_on` in brackets): Coherent `COHR` (2022-07-11), Healthcare Realty `HR` (2022-07-31), Physicians Realty `DOC` (2024-03-11), CoreSite `COR` (2022-01-07), Safehold `SAFE` (2023-04-10), SunPower `SPWR` (2024-09-30), the old Caesars `CZR` (2020-07-30), Barnes `B` (2025-02-06), AVX (2020-04-09), Bed Bath & Beyond `BBBY` (2023-07-20): the ticker is another company's today and the old holder was never asked for with `asof`. They are holes that `ingest --source alpaca --backfill --fill-holes` (#831) lists and #1317's own-span `asof` request fills; they go in the release's fill-holes step beside the names the parse fix lists. Two names' bars stop weeks before their Form 25: Biohaven `BHVN` (last bar 2022-08-09, Form 25 filed 2022-10-04, effective 2022-10-14) and Avidity `RNA` (last bar 2025-12-09, filed 2026-02-27, effective 2026-03-09); they are attempted in the same step and reported: their span end is dated by the successor's row (rule 8, #1314 item 2), and where that row is a `snapshot_static` one back-dated to the successor's first session, the end is wrong by the hand-over lag and the fix is #869's (dating the change), not a refetch. The local check also named GCI Liberty and Lionsgate Studios; the fill-holes dry run lists them if the store has their holes. The old Match (`0000891103`) is a successor case with bars under both ids (`master.keep_successors`), not a hole.

*The bank-level registrants (item 5).* Recorded once, in [2026-10-11-h1-known-data-holes.md](../research/2026-10-11-h1-known-data-holes.md) and as claim TP-4, not repeated here: the store has no `securities` row for First Republic Bank, Signature Bank or Cadence Bank (bank-level registrants file with their banking regulator, not EDGAR), so two of the three large 2023 bank failures are outside the universe on every session, while SVB Financial Group is in the store through its last session; Cadence Bank did not fail and is a hole in coverage of a survivor. Only a vendor or a hand seed fills the class; no pipeline. H1's hypothesis file is frozen since registration and is not edited.

*Acceptance (the plan's T178 to T182 carry the files).*
- [ ] Parsers (T178): with `exchange_axis_listings` on, an iXBRL document in ANSYS's shape (one context, the exchange axis alone, the three listing facts and the shares fact) yields one listing (`ANSS`, `NASDAQ`, `Common Stock`) and its shares; IBM's shape the same; P&G's shape (common under the axis, notes undimensioned) yields the common first and the notes after it; a context with both the class axis and the exchange axis keeps its class member; one class in two exchange contexts yields two listings; a `dei:LegalEntityAxis` context is still `other_contexts`; an exchange-axis shares fact equal to the undimensioned one collapses, a different one fails closed. FSN rows with segments naming the exchange axis alone, and with it beside `ClassOfStock`, yield the same listings; the group key separates two exchanges of one class; an undecodable byte in the exchange segment fails closed. Every one of these fails on the merge-base. With the switch off, the same inputs give today's parse, byte for byte. The re-extraction leaves SIC and statement rows unchanged (fixture).
- [ ] Master and universe (T178): on a fixture store built from the five names' real cover-page shapes (IBM, AbbVie, Abbott, 3M and P&G common, with synthetic bars and shares over 2020 to 2021, P&G's notes included), with the switch on, the five common ids are members of `universe_as_of(close(2021-06-30))`, their listing at that `t` is a `filing` row with `known_at` equal to the 2019 filing's acceptance, P&G's primary id carries `PG`, and at the close just before the earliest acceptance all five are `not_listed`; the same fixture on the merge-base gives the expected reason per name (rule `exchange`, reason `not_listed`, for the four, and for P&G whichever rule its undimensioned notes trigger, named in the test).
- [ ] The residual (T178): a fixture store built with the merge-base rules through 2022-10-19 (P&G's note on the primary id) and re-ingested with the new rules reads P&G's primary id as the note at `close(2023-06-30)` with the retraction stamped at the run, so the documents match the code.
- [ ] Titles (T179): with `title_fallback` on, the four steps on the real titles above, including "United Therapeutics Corporation, Common Stock" (common, not unit), "Depository Shares, each representing ... Preferred Stock" (preferred), a `%` title with "Common Stock" after the comma (not common), a placeholder-titled class beside a titled common (unclassifiable), a placeholder title with a `W`-suffixed ticker (warrant by rule 7), a later placeholder after a real title (the real title decides); `Shares of Beneficial Interest` and `Commorn Stock` stay `title_unrecognized`; shares facts of a Shockwave-shaped issuer continue after its titled listing; fails on the merge-base; with the switch off the fixture universe's pinned classifications are unchanged.
- [ ] Classification rebuild (T182): `ingest --source edgar --rebuild-classifications` deletes every `classifications` row of the CIKs the build covers and writes the build's rows with the build's `known_at` (the instant each classification first became knowable, never the run) in the chunk's transaction; on the fixture store a rebuild with unchanged rules leaves every as-of read identical; with `title_fallback` on, a Shockwave-shaped name reads `common` at the close after its titled listing's acceptance and `unclassifiable` under the merge-base rules; the flag is refused without `--source edgar` and on a dry run writes nothing.
