- [ ] Coverage (T180): on a fixture store, a reference name whose only listing is `snapshot_static` known after S is in `reference_missing` on S with rule `exchange`, reason `not_listed`, and in `late_known`; with a `filing` row known before S beside the static one it is in neither; an entry's `FROM` and `TO` bound the sessions it is checked on; `--check` fails `coverage_reference_names` only with `check_enabled`; a month-end on a weekend samples the Friday; `health` without `--coverage` and with the default off computes nothing for the block; the page renders the card and computes nothing until asked.
- [ ] Release (T181, owner): the evidence line in the plan, including zero `reference_missing` outside the entries' windows on every sample session from 2019-12-31 after the release, and the named names present on 2021-06-30 and 2023-12-29 on the owner's store, P&G excepted on the sessions open question 4 names.

*Open questions (the owner's; a recommendation beside each). The build task a question touches codes the recommended option unless the owner records another choice on #1541 before it is claimed (plan amendment); the rest are recorded at T181's step 0.*
1. **A mismatched exchange member.** Count and keep the value (recommended: the value is what every row so far was built from, and #224 chose skip-and-count for cover-page slips), or fail the filing closed as a two-value conflict does.
2. **The open paper windows.** The release writes listing rows known from 2019 on and bars from 2016 on, so `universe_as_of` changes on every session since 2019, including the sessions inside H1's window 1 (open since 2026-10-09, first rebalance 2026-10-30) and the Daily book's window 2 (first rebalance 2026-10-12); runbook rule 5 and ADR 0016 point 7 forbid that while a window is open, the #1497 exception covers stamps only, and each window's `started_at` already counts as a read instant, so no "nothing read yet" exception is available. (a) Stop both windows (`paper stop --reason`), run the release, start new ones: the shakedown span restarts, H1's forward exam starts on the corrected store, and nothing in ADR 0016 changes; (b) wait for both windows to close, months for H1. Recommended: (a), run before 2026-10-30.
3. **Order against the stamps repair.** The new listing rows take their `known_at` from the stamps cache, which `restamp-1382a2` repairs for the universe-backing CIKs only; the names this fix adds (ANSYS, Allstate, Fifth Third, Whirlpool, US Steel and the rest) are not in that phase. (a) Run the release after `restamp-1382b` (recommended when phase B closes by 2026-10-26); (b) run it first and let phase B's restamp move the new rows too (T166b moves every row whose `known_at` is a changed stamp), accepting that until then those names' listings may be known early by New York's offset; (c) a phase over the CIKs the release's dry run lists, in the same sitting, with T166d's driver. If phase B slips past 2026-10-26, question 2's deadline gives way to (c), not the other way round: the release waits for a repaired cache. T181 names (a) and takes the owner's choice.
4. **The mis-assigned primary ids (P&G).** The retraction is stamped at the run (#859), so P&G reads as the note on every session from 2022-10-19 to the release. (a) Accept the residual (recommended: one name so far; the dry run counts the P&G-type primary ids, and the evidence and the reference list name them); (b) a recorded restatement for exactly these keys, a back-dated retraction at the false row's own `known_at`, which is an exception to #859's "never back-dated" and a size-S change to `master-retract` by a later amendment, if the dry run finds more than a handful.
5. **The classification rebuild (T182).** Classifications are derived rows (forms, SICs, titled listings at instants), not facts, and `classify.py`'s timing rule already stamps each row at the instant it first became knowable; a rebuild re-derives them from the same evidence, so no row is stamped earlier than its evidence, and the backup keeps what the store said before (the #660 `--rebuild-statement-facts` pattern). (a) Build T182 and run it in the release (recommended: it is what makes item 2 reach the sessions H1 reads, and it gives P&G's primary id `common` from 2019, so whether `universe_as_of` then admits P&G under the note's listing row is read in the evidence, not assumed); (b) forward-only: drop T182 and the item 2 evidence lines, and record that the reclassified names enter the universe from the release on.

**Amendment 2026-10-12 (#1597): point-in-time listings before 2019 from filing-time evidence.** The store has no listing row known before 2019-06 for anyone: tickers come from iXBRL cover pages (2019-06 on) and from the companies snapshot (`snapshot_static`, known at its fetch, #35). So `universe_as_of` is empty before 2019, the #974 lead gives a survivor its 2016-2018 bars but no membership, and the backfill never asked Alpaca for a name that delisted before 2019 (47 of 779 equity filers with a 2016-2018 Form 25 or 25-NSE have bars). The owner asked on 2026-10-12 for rows for every equity filer, survivors included, since delisters alone would bias the 2016-2018 universe the other way. *The evidence (#1597, its issue text and spike comment; read-only; vendor rows stay in the owner's local library):* Alpaca has bars for 51 of 51 sampled 2016-2018 delisters once asked under the right ticker with `asof` inside the name's own span (40 to the delisting, 11 to the exchange suspension, none empty; 9 of 45 return today's holder without `asof`; one series padded with zero-volume bars to 2019). A Nasdaq Form 25's exhibit file name carries the ticker (`<tkr>-form25.txt`, `<tkr>delistreason.txt`: 464 of 467); an NYSE-family one does not (0 of 312; the notice text names none either). The XBRL instance of an issuer's own 10-K is `<prefix>-YYYYMMDD.xml` and the prefix is the ticker of the time for 36 of 40 seeded survivors over three filings each (one class of two for Alphabet, `GOOG`; a rename the filings date, Herc's `HTZ` then `HRI`; an abbreviation in all three years, Synovus's `SYN`; one filing with no instance) and 10 of 10 spot-checked delisters; 92 of the 2,847 survivors' 33,954 periodic filings have no XBRL. A 10-K's untagged Section 12(b) table names the exchange for 36 of 40 (35 equal to the 2019 cover exchange; the one difference, PW on NYSE MKT, is real); `dei:TradingSymbol` is tagged in about half of pre-2019 10-K/10-Qs (#1311), with no exchange. A 10-K's file number is no listing test (23% of the survivors' 10-Ks are filed under `000-` numbers). These are facts of the filings themselves, which the #974 amendment's "Not chosen" could not say of a historical ticker file or the submissions record; open question 1 is answered here. Only 1,748 of the 2,847 survivors have a `snapshot_static` row today; the rest have no listing before their first cover page.

*The rows.* `listings` gains rows with **`provenance = filing_name`**: a ticker read from a filing's file names, not from a tagged fact; `known_at` = that filing's acceptance (the stamps cache, as every filing row), `ingested_at` the run, `source = edgar`, `class_title` NULL. Classification rule 7 reads the ticker's suffix on such a row as it does a snapshot ticker, at the row's `known_at`, and the classification row it writes carries the listing row's provenance (`filing_name`, never `snapshot_static`); rule 8's `common_default` applies otherwise. Every reader that tells `snapshot` and `snapshot_static` rows from filed ones (health's `_SNAPSHOT_PROVENANCES`, the staleness rule's static-only names, the #974 lead's placeholder test) treats a `filing_name` row as filed, which they do by construction. The value enters the allowed sets of `listings` and `classifications`; those sets are `CHECK` constraints of the tables, so an existing store takes a migration that rebuilds the two tables with the wider set and a `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump, rows unchanged. The *Underived* rule of the #859 amendment judges a `filing_name` row as it judges a `filing` row (it comes from full-history inputs too), so `master-retract` proposes one the build no longer derives. Two readings, both behind `edgar.filename_listings` (default `false`; the release flips it):
1. **The issuer's own 10-K** (`edgar.filename_listing_forms`, default `10-K` and `10-KT`; never a 10-Q, 20-F or 40-F; a 10-Q's prefix would date a rename to the quarter at four times the requests, not taken), accepted on or after `edgar.filename_listings_since` (default 2015-01-01, so the FY2014 10-K covers 2016-01-04; earlier buys nothing, 2016-01-01 leaves most names unlisted until March) and before the security's first cover-page listing's acceptance known at the run. One row per 10-K: ticker = the instance file-name prefix (`<prefix>-YYYYMMDD.xml` or `_htm.xml`, never a linkbase, from the filing's `index.json`), read as a symbol by amendment #844's rule; **`valid_from` = the acceptance's New York date** (`store.master.session_of`, as `build_delistings` dates a Form 25; never EDGAR's filing date, which is the next business day after 17:30 ET); exchange = the one exchange the filing's Section 12(b) table names in the primary document's text, read by the same `normalize_exchange` the cover pages and Forms 25 use so a Form 25 matches it (per class, the common class's line; a table naming none, 12(g) only, or no table within `edgar.filename_document_max_bytes`, default 4 MB, the read stops at the table or the cap: no row, counted `no_exchange`). Form 8-A12B, the registration of a class on an exchange, is the cheaper exchange source not taken: its coverage before EDGAR's full-text window is unmeasured; it is the fallback if the dry run's document volume is too large for one sitting. The adapter keeps only the extract (names, prefix, the 12(b) class and exchange pairs) in a per-CIK cache keyed by accession, never a document.
2. **A Form 25's exhibit file name** (Nasdaq; a 25 or a 25-NSE, both ingested by req 4, the exhibit convention being the exchange's), accepted on or after the store's first session: the ticker the name reads, the class and exchange the filing names (`class_title` stays NULL; the class picks the security id as `build_delistings` does), `valid_from` = the security's first session by the #974 rule, `known_at` = the Form 25's acceptance. In one run the 10-K rows of the CIK are derived first, and the Form 25 row is written only when no listing of the security under that ticker, stored or derived in this run, covers the Form 25's acceptance date: a row of last resort that serves bars and never membership (the span is ending when it is known), as a `snapshot_static` row serves a survivor. Written or not, the exhibit ticker is a reading for the confirmation below.

*Confirmation: a membership deviation, bounded and counted.* The prefix is a heuristic (the Filer Manual asks for the ticker or an abbreviation), so at the run a 10-K's prefix is judged against the security's other readings known at the run, by a pure rule (readings in, verdict out). The readings: the same filing's `dei:TradingSymbol` (the FSN record keeps a symbol-only tag from the next `FSN_VERSION`; such a tag never becomes a `filing` listing row and never changes `incomplete_listings`); the prefixes of the security's later 10-Ks in the window and of its first cover-page filing (one more `index.json`); the cover-page tickers of the CIK; the Form 25 exhibit ticker. Prefixes of one issuer are one reading; a tag, a cover page and an exhibit are each independent. A `snapshot_static` row is **not** a reading, not even refusal-only: it is today's venue ticker back-dated, so it would confirm a prefix with a ticker learned after `t` and its current ticker would refuse a pre-rename prefix (item 2 of the 2026-10-14 amendment); the readings come only from each filing's own text, at that filing's acceptance. In this order (*re-ordered 2026-10-18, #1605, as the code has it*): (0) a span the owner lists in `edgar.filename_listings_refused` (`CIK:TICKER` entries, default empty; a malformed entry refuses the config) is refused (`refused_owner`) before any reading is consulted, so a tag-confirmed prefix can be withdrawn too; the build stops deriving it and `master-retract` withdraws it, and the same entry refuses the Form 25 exhibit row of last resort (reading 2) for that CIK and ticker, which is asked with `asof` too and can collide; (1) the filing's own tag differs: **refused** (`refused_contradicted`); (2) its own tag equals it: `confirmed`, whatever a later reading says; (3) no tag, and a cover-page or exhibit reading differs: refused unless a later prefix of the security equals that reading, in which case the prefix stands as `renamed` (Synovus: `SYN` three times against `SNV`, refused; Herc: `HTZ` with `HRI` after it, kept); (4) no tag, a cover-page or exhibit reading equals it: `confirmed`; (5) no independent reading at all (an NYSE-family delister with no tag): written and counted `single_reading`. A confirmed prefix lands on the class of the CIK whose cover page or Form 25 names it, else the primary id; a second class with no reading of its own before its first cover page stays as today (counted `class_unread`). **The deviation:** whether a `filing_name` row exists at all is decided from readings known at the run, so `universe_as_of(T)` before 2019 depends on filings after T, and the truncation harness, which filters stored rows, cannot see it; the #735 "Look-ahead" paragraph's deviation reaches only the price-to-security mapping, this one reaches membership. It is accepted because every other choice is worse (refusing every unconfirmed prefix biases the 2016-2018 universe toward Nasdaq exits; writing every prefix admits wrong names), and it is bounded: it removes names (`refused_*`) and never adds one no filing of the security named; every verdict is counted on the run row; the fixture holds each case; and it is recorded under "Risks & domain checks". **Collisions, the done-test for a written row:** a wrong prefix that is another company's live symbol returns that company's bars under `asof`. Rule 7 ends the delister's span at `effective_on`, so no stored bar can show it; the raw payload can. The Alpaca backfill therefore counts, for every `filing_name` span with an end that it asks with `asof`, a **collision** when the payload holds traded bars more than `master.transfer_window_sessions` XNYS sessions after the span's end, lists the spans on the run row (`filename_collisions`), and stores nothing of that payload for the span. The release's rule: every listed span goes into `edgar.filename_listings_refused`, the next EDGAR run derives it no more, `master-retract --release` withdraws its rows, `repair-resolution` moves any bar stored under it. A wrong prefix that is no Alpaca symbol returns no bar, fails universe rule 6 and is counted by health (`no_bars`). A survivor's wrong prefix is caught by rule (3). Fewer names, never a knowingly wrong one.

*What the rows change, and what they do not.* Universe rule 2 passes from the 10-K's acceptance on; rule 1 by `common_default` or rule 7; rules 7 and 8 read the FSN shares facts, which exist before 2019; rule 6 needs `universe.min_history_months` of bars, and Alpaca's floor is 2016-01-04, so the first non-empty universe is 2017-01 whatever the rows say. The resolver's rules are unchanged: a `filing_name` row is a filing row of its span; a survivor with a `snapshot_static` row keeps one span (consecutive rows with one ticker), so its bars resolve as before and `repair-resolution` moves nothing; a survivor without one gets its first span from its 2015 or 2016 10-K instead of its 2019 cover page, with the #974 lead covering the sessions before it, so its stored bars are the same bars; a delister gets a span the backfill never had, asked with `asof` inside it by #1314's rule, ended by rule 7 at the Form 25's `effective_on`, so a successor on the same ticker (KEG, ARRS, CCE, ASH, NAKD) never leaks in and the zero-volume padding after the last trade is either dropped by the adapter's placeholder rule (`v == 0` and `n == 0`) or cut by the span's end; the successor's own #974 lead is refused where the old span covers (rule 5, counted), as a reuse the master knows of. A new span can also change a third security's resolution (a rule 6 claim, a contested span, rule 8's span lag); the backfill counts such changes (`filename_disputes`, the sessions another security loses to a `filing_name` span) and the release reports them. A Form 25 that matched no listing before now matches, so `delistings` rows appear for pre-2019 exits with their own `known_at`, and the survivorship gap's delisting branch reads them; a Form 25 whose exchange matches no `filing_name` row of the security (a 12(b) text the normalizer read differently) stays unmatched and is counted (`filename_form25_unmatched`), since the span then never ends; having no end, such a span gets no collision check from the backfill (the collision count above covers spans with an end), so the release's reading of that count lists those names for the owner's hand review, or the exchange is matched by hand first (*wording 2026-10-18, #1605*). *Accepted cost, transfers:* a 2016-2018 exchange transfer's new row arrives with the next 10-K, months after the Form 25 and outside `master.transfer_window_sessions`, so req 4 reads the name as delisted from the Form 25 until that 10-K, and a backtest takes a `delisting_exit` on it; the run row counts such Forms 25 (one whose security has a later `filing_name` row on another exchange of `universe.exchanges`) and the fixture holds the case. `--fill-holes` (#831, #876) lists and fetches the newly spanned and led months. The data vintage at every cutoff from 2015 on moves, so every registered trial goes stale, store-wide and once, as the #1541 release does. *Out:* the OTC tail after an exchange suspension (Alpaca has none; the 11 of 51 end at the suspension, which the delisting-return rule already covers); sessions before 2016-01-04 (no bars; rows from 2015 filings exist only to cover 2016-01-04); 10-Qs; 20-F and 40-F filers (`foreign`, outside the universe); the second class of a dual-class issuer; filers without XBRL (a Nasdaq delister falls to reading 2, a survivor waits for its cover page as today; counted `no_instance`).

*Counts and health.* The EDGAR run row counts: 10-Ks read, `confirmed`, `single_reading`, `renamed`, `refused_contradicted`, `refused_owner`, `no_instance`, `no_exchange`, `class_unread`, Form 25 rows written, `filename_form25_unmatched`, transfers read as delistings, index and document fetches. The Alpaca run row counts `filename_collisions` (listed) and `filename_disputes`. `health` gains a `filename_listings` block at the latest T: securities whose only listing at T is `filing_name`, rows by count, rows whose span holds no traded bar known at T (`no_bars`); the page shows it; `--check` keeps the allowed-set rules with the new value and, for `filing_name` rows, `valid_from == session_of(known_at)` for a 10-K row and `valid_from <= session_of(known_at)` for a Form 25 row (the two are told apart by `valid_from` against the security's first session). The static reliance count falls by the names these rows now list.

*Acceptance (the plan's T184 to T184h carry the files).*
- [ ] Parsers (T184), pure: an `index.json` listing gives the instance prefix (`jci-20160630.xml`, `abc-20171231_htm.xml`; `_cal`/`_def`/`_lab`/`_pre` never; none when no instance), a Form 25 listing gives the exhibit ticker (`fsys-form25.txt`, `spdcdelistreason.txt`; `ruleprovisionnotice.htm` none), the 12(b) extractor on four text shapes (one exchange; two classes on two exchanges, the common class's taken; 12(g) only; no table) returns `normalize_exchange` tags and stops at the table or the cap; the FSN parser keeps a symbol-only tag without changing `incomplete_listings` or the listings; each fails on the merge-base.
- [ ] Confirmation (T184c), pure: the six verdicts above on constructed readings, including the own-tag precedence, the rename kept by a later prefix or by the first cover-page filing's prefix, the owner deny-list, and the class assignment; a malformed deny-list entry refuses the config.
- [ ] Rows (T184e), on a fixture universe with the required cases of req 13: a survivor with three 10-Ks and a 2019 cover page has `filing_name` rows with `known_at` at each 10-K's acceptance and `valid_from` its acceptance's New York date, `universe_as_of` reads `not_listed` at the close before the first acceptance and lists the name at the close after it (a member once rule 6 holds), and every as-of read is truncation-invariant around every new `known_at`; `build_master` raises for a record accepted after `ingested_at`; a Nasdaq delister whose prefix the Form 25 exhibit equals is `confirmed`, its span ends at `effective_on`, its `delistings` row exists, and the successor's bars after it under the same ticker are not its own; a 10-K-less Nasdaq delister gets the Form 25 row of last resort and a 10-K-bearing one does not; an NYSE delister with no tag is `single_reading` and written; a pre-2019 transfer reads as delisted until its next 10-K and is counted; a Form 25 on an exchange the 12(b) text named differently is counted unmatched; with the switch off, nothing is written and no fetch is made.
- [ ] Resolution (T184e, T184f): a survivor with a `snapshot_static` row resolves every session as on the merge-base; one without resolves the same sessions to the same security, with the first span earlier and the lead shorter; a delister's `asof` day is inside its new span; no other security's resolution changes on any session except the counted `filename_disputes`; a colliding prefix (a span whose `asof` payload runs past its end) is listed in `filename_collisions` and stores nothing.
- [ ] Health (T184g): the block and its counts on the fixture store; `--check` passes the fixture and fails a 10-K `filing_name` row whose `valid_from` is not its `known_at`'s New York date.
- [ ] Release (T184h, owner): the evidence line in the plan, with the member count and the gap at every month-end from 2017-01 to 2019-06 on the owner's store, before and after; the release stops at the plan's stop rules.

*Open questions (the owner's; a recommendation beside each; the build task a question touches codes the recommendation unless the owner records another choice on #1597 before it is claimed).* Listed as 22 to 24 under "Open questions". The calls on 10-Qs, the exchange source and the window's start are decided above, with their alternatives.

**Amendment 2026-10-12 (#1613): one cash dividend stored twice.** Alpaca's corporate actions sometimes carry one declared cash dividend as two records under two ids (most often two CUSIPs of one class; sometimes a `special` flag or a payable-date variant), and the first-seen rule of req 5 stores both: two live `dividend` rows with the same `security_id`, `ex_date`, `ratio_or_amount` and `known_at` under two `source_action_id`s, and the total-return marking (`adjusted_prices_as_of` with dividends) applies both. The read-only known-answer index test (#1598) found it on the `ops-1549` backup: 183 such groups with ex-dates from 2019-09-27 to 2026-09-30, 51 in universe member-months; AIV 2020-11-03's $8.20 twice reads the month about 50 points too high, TDG 2025-09-02's $90 twice about 6.4. Not every equal pair is a duplicate: Ford's 2025-02-18 $0.15 is a regular and a supplemental dividend, both paid.

*The evidence and the rule (#1613: Codex's read-only classification of every group against the raw Alpaca records, fetched owner-side for the 181 groups with a listing at their ex-date, 180 of them with both raw records, with issuer releases for the edge cases; the raw rows stay in the owner's local probes directory).* The property: **the number of live cash rows for a security, ex-date and amount equals the number of separately declared cash entitlements to that security.** Two vendor ids are evidence of two records, not of two payments. Of the 183 groups, 169 are one entitlement stored twice (48 member-months); 11 are two declared payments of equal amount (2 member-months: Ford's regular plus supplemental, Chord's base plus variable, two Blackstone funds' December and January distributions sharing an ex-date, MarketWise's regular plus special in 2025-08 and 2025-11, five PennantPark base-plus-supplemental months in 2026); 3 cannot be judged (1 member-month: Seagate 2021-05-11, whose ids Alpaca no longer returns, and two 2021 groups of `0001372807` with no listing at their ex-dates). No stored or raw field separates the classes: among the 169 duplicates the two records differ by CUSIP only (145), by nothing (5), by `special` only (5), by CUSIP and `foreign` (3) or by a date variant (11), while the PennantPark real pairs are identical in every raw field but the id, and TransDigm's one $90 payment carries one `special` and one regular record. So **ingest never dedupes**: a key on CUSIP, `special` or field equality would erase real payments or keep duplicates, and only evidence from outside the feed settles a group. Three parts follow: a reviewed list, a repair under a named release, and a health rule that makes every later group visible instead of silently double-counted.

*The reviewed list.* A committed file, `src/tradepartner/store/dividend_reviews.csv`, read only through `store.dividend_reviews.reviewed_groups()`, one row per adjudicated group: `security_id`, `ex_date`, `amount` (parsed to the `DOUBLE` the store holds in `ratio_or_amount`; every comparison below is exact equality with the stored value, never a tolerance), `verdict` in `{duplicate, distinct, unresolved}`, `retract_source_action_id` (non-empty exactly when the verdict is `duplicate`), `ticker` (the listing at the ex-date, for the reader) and `evidence` (an issuer release URL, or the raw-record difference in a few words; the repo is public and no vendor row is copied). The loader refuses a malformed file, naming the row: a verdict outside the set, an `ex_date` that is not an XNYS session, a non-positive amount, a key listed twice, a retract id on a row that is not `duplicate` or missing on one that is. Every listed group was reviewed as **exactly two identities**, and that count is part of the verdict: the rule below holds a listed key to it. **Which of a group's two ids is retracted is immaterial to every read** when the two rows agree in every column a read uses (`security_id`, `action_type`, `ex_date`, `ratio_or_amount`, `known_at`), which is how the 183 groups were found (the group key included `known_at`); the list names the greater id in string order, so the file is reproducible from the store alone and a reviewer checks a row without the raw records, and the repair refuses (below) any entry where that agreement no longer holds. The first file is the 183 groups above, built from the issue's three lists and the owner-side raw export; a later group enters the file only with its evidence, by a PR, and is applied by a release of its own.

*The repair: a delete, not a cancel.* The spec's own withdrawal of an action is a `cancelled` revision stamped at the run and never back-dated (req 5), and an as-of read at a T before that stamp still returns the event. That is right for an event the source withdraws, and useless here: a backtest reading AIV at 2020-11 would still count $8.20 twice, and the index test would still fail. The duplicate row is not a fact that was true until the run; it has no `known_at` at which it was right, which is the reasoning `repair-resolution` and `repair-bars` (#819, T140c) already apply when they delete every revision of a row that was never the security's fact. So the repair deletes. `tradepartner repair-dividends --release <name> [--to <session>] [--dry-run] [--expect-action-rows N]` (`repair.retract_dividends`) takes every `duplicate` row of the list with an ex-date on or before `--to` (default: every row; a row after it is counted `deferred` and untouched, see open question 27) and, for each, the identity `(security_id, retract_source_action_id)`; it deletes every row of that identity from `corporate_actions` with `source = ACTIONS_SOURCE` and `action_type = dividend`, and nothing else, under `delete_bars`'s rules: one transaction on the write connection; a real run only with the dry run's row count and only inside the open release of the given name, else `RepairRefused` and nothing deleted; one `ingestion_runs` row (source `alpaca`, mode `repair`, status `repaired`, never `ok`) whose message gives the counts and the release. A list entry is **stale** when the retract identity has more than one revision (the vendor touched it after the review), when its row's `ex_date`, `ratio_or_amount` or `known_at` differs from the entry's key or from the kept identity's live row (an earlier-known retract row would, deleted, remove the payment from every read between the two stamps), or when no other live `dividend` row of the security shares the entry's `ex_date` and amount (the kept row is gone, so the delete would remove the security's only record of the payment); one stale entry refuses the whole run, so the list is re-reviewed, never half-applied. An entry whose retract id has no row at all is **already repaired**: counted, nothing deleted, so a second run under a later release deletes nothing and refuses nothing; the first release expects that count to be zero (a mistyped id would hide there), and T186c's step 2 stops on anything else. A delete is not a revision: no row is written and no stamp moves, so an as-of read at any T afterwards simply lacks the row, the look-ahead rules are unchanged and truncation invariance holds as before. The sessions a release touches are the ex-dates of the rows it deletes; rule 1 of the runbook makes it a named release with a backup, and the `after` record stales the trials whose window the release touched (#1329); the runbook's delete-only paragraph and question 28 say when the sweeps rerun.

*The open paper windows.* The runbook's rule 5 is met on its wording (the first list's last ex-date, 2026-09-30, is before the window open since 2026-10-09) and not on its reason: an in-window decision of a book whose strategy reads total-return prices (`strategy.signal_total_return`, the default; the shakedown book's 12-1 momentum reads about twelve months back from each decision) is recomputed by the forward exam from the live store, so deleting a duplicate whose ex-date lies inside that lookback (SGHC 2026-02-02, AU 2026-03-13 twice, MKTW 2026-03-18, FIZZ 2026-07-13, AMRZ 2026-08-18, HSHP 2026-08-21, NCDL 2026-09-30: eight rows) can change a recomputed rank with no change in the book. Open question 27 is the owner's; its recommendation is `--to`: the release deletes every row with an ex-date before the earliest formation start of any decision inside an open window (the owner reads each open book's `formation_months` and `skip_months` from its hypothesis and takes the earliest), defers the rest (they stay listed, `pending` in health, and are applied by a release of their own once those windows close), and the `before` record names the bound.

*The health rule.* `duplicate_dividends` joins `INTEGRITY_RULES`, computed by a pure function over the `dividend` rows live at `t` (`live_actions_as_of`: the latest revision per identity known at `t`, not cancelled) and the reviewed groups passed in (the packaged file is read at the edge, so tests inject a list). Per `(security_id, ex_date, ratio_or_amount)` held by more than one identity: a key the list does not hold is a **violation**; a listed key with exactly two identities is **accepted** (`distinct`), the **known gap** (`unresolved`) or **pending** (`duplicate`: the repair has not run, or deferred it); a listed key with any other identity count is a violation (a third vendor record on a real pair, or a retracted id Alpaca sent again beside a kept row and a third); a `duplicate` key with one identity is repaired and not listed. A different `known_at` or `source_action_id` is never evidence of two entitlements, so the key ignores both, and the rule has no date bound, so it is wider than the list's first count (the two 2019-06 groups the classification left out, and any pair whose two rows differ in `known_at`, show as violations until the list holds them: T186c's step 0; a pair whose rows differ in `known_at` is listed `unresolved`, since the stale rule refuses to retract it). Violations fail `--check`, listed with the key, every id and its `known_at`; the accepted and gap keys are shown, never hidden, as `accepted_same_day_pairs` are; the pending count is a warning line of the CLI, as the quarantined count is, and **not a failure**, departing from the issue's "not already repaired" wording on purpose: the paper shakedown's gate E.7 requires `health_report` to be ok, so a failing rule on a known, listed, not-yet-applied repair would block a shakedown from the rule's merge to the release and for every deferred group, which is not news; an unlisted group is. The page shows the rule through its existing integrity card; the lists and the warning are the CLI's. No threshold is added: the only numbers in the rule are one and two. The fixture universe gains no case (its pinned outputs stay); the tests inject rows into a temporary store, as the duplicate-bar test does.

*Acceptance (the tasks' done-tests).*
- [ ] Reviewed list (T186): the loader reads the committed file into typed records; it refuses each malformed shape above, naming the row; every `duplicate` row's retract id is the greater of its group's two ids; the file holds 169 `duplicate`, 11 `distinct` and 3 `unresolved` rows at this amendment (pinned, so a later edit is deliberate and carries its evidence), and the issue's named cases read as listed: AIV 2020-11-03 $8.20, TDG 2025-09-02 $90 and MKTW 2026-03-18 $0.20 `duplicate`; F 2025-02-18 $0.15, CHRD 2023-11-13 $1.25 and MKTW 2025-08-15 $0.20 `distinct`; STX 2021-05-11 $0.67 `unresolved`.
- [ ] Repair (T186): on a temporary store holding a duplicate group, a `distinct` pair and an unrelated action, a dry run reports the rows the retract identity holds and changes nothing; the real run deletes exactly those rows and leaves the kept row, the `distinct` pair and the unrelated action untouched; `adjusted_prices_as_of` at a T after the group's `known_at` applies one dividend, and at a T before it none, as before; a count other than the dry run's, a missing or differently named open release, and each stale shape (a second revision of the retract identity; a retract row whose `ex_date`, amount or `known_at` differs from the kept row's; no live partner) refuse with nothing deleted; an entry after `--to` is counted deferred and untouched; a second run deletes nothing and counts the entry already repaired; the run row is `repaired` and `last_ingests` never reads it as an ok ingest.
- [ ] Health (T186b): the fixture store passes; an injected equal pair under two ids fails `duplicate_dividends` naming the key, both ids and their `known_at`; the same key listed `distinct` passes and is listed as accepted; listed `unresolved` it passes and is listed as the gap; listed `duplicate` it passes, is counted pending and the CLI prints the warning; a third id on a listed key fails; a cancelled or superseded revision never counts; two rows whose `known_at` differ are one key.
- [ ] Release (T186c, owner): the `before` and `after` records with the `--to` bound; the pre-release `health` run with every violation moved into the list; the dry run's and the real run's counts (entries, identities found, rows, deferred, already repaired zero); `health --check` exit 0 with no violation, 11 accepted, 3 unresolved and the deferred count pending; the index test's AIV 2020-11 and TDG 2025-09 months recomputed exactly from raw bars plus one dividend each, as its other 142 months do; the sweeps rerun per the runbook's delete-only paragraph, named in the `after` entry, the holdout trial never rerun.

*Open questions:* 25 to 28 in the list below; the tasks build the recommendations.

**Amendment 2026-10-12 (#821, #1615): one continuous security across a 1:1 successor-CIK reorganisation.** When a company reorganises under a new holding company, the continuing business files from a new CIK: the successor files an 8-K12B (Section 12(b)) or 8-K12G3 (12(g)) as successor issuer, the predecessor's common is delisted by a Form 25, and the successor's first cover page lists the old ticker on the old exchange. Req 3 makes the successor a new security, so its bars, actions and share facts begin at its first listing row while the predecessor is delisted: universe rule 6 keeps the business out for `universe.min_history_months`, rule 7 until the successor's first share count, and every formation window restarts. The read-only count of 2026-10-12 on the `ops-1549` backup ([#821](https://github.com/josejuarez96/tradepartner/issues/821)): 17 clean pairs since 2019 (about two a year), 6 of them in the universe at the hand-over (XRX, AAN, BG, FERG, BLK, XOM), 55 universe-months lost to the missing chain alone (XRX 12, AAN 13, FERG 13, BLK 13, XOM 4 and running), BlackRock out since 2024-10 and ExxonMobil since the 2026-07-31 rebalance while the paper books plan from today's universe (#1615). Rule 7's "Not done" line (BG, FERG, NWE, ZYME, MTAL) is this case; MTAL is a de-SPAC and stays as it is.

*Definition.* A **chain** is an ordered pair `(predecessor, successor)` of two securities of different CIKs, each a `<cik>` or `<cik>:<class>` id, where the successor's first listing row names the ticker and exchange of the predecessor's last listing row and each predecessor share became one successor share. The chained security is the successor; the predecessor's rows are its history.

*The evidence, and why a reviewed list.* The three facts the owner named: an 8-K12B or 8-K12G3 by the successor's CIK, the same ticker on the same exchange, price continuity over the hand-over. On the count every clean pair has all three (the filing 0 to 98 days before the successor's listing row, the close ratio within 11% over at most seven days), every spin-off lacks the filing (the IAC/Match separation, Aaron's SpinCo, Biohaven, Liberty Live), every de-SPAC has a `spac` predecessor, and a merger or conversion closed with the reorganisation shows in the share count (Marvell with Inphi +21%, QuidelOrtho +60%, Noble +95%, the LP conversions). A store rule would need a bound on the share count, a price bound the EDGAR side cannot read (Macquarie Infrastructure's 8-K12B over a 91% distribution) and a window for a listing row that comes weeks late. At two a year the owner reviews instead, as for `alpaca.accepted_relistings`, `master.keep_successors` and `universe.accepted_price_jumps`: **`master.chains`** (default empty; in the owner's `.env` as `MASTER__CHAINS`, never committed, the evidence table on the release's PR) lists entries `<predecessor>-><successor>@<accession>`, the accession that of the successor CIK's 8-K12B or 8-K12G3. The config refuses an entry whose ids are not two `<cik>` or `<cik>:<class>` ids of different CIKs (a `<cik>@<date>` successor, #820, is one company already and is never chained; nor is a `master.keep_successors` id, #922, on either side), whose accession is malformed, or whose id appears in another entry on either side (`A->B` with `B->C` is refused: a chained successor's own later reorganisation waits for an amendment that defines the transitive read); every command then fails rather than chain silently. **`master.chains_dismissed`** (default empty) lists successor ids the owner reviewed and struck, so they are never counted as candidates. The count's 17 pairs are the first entries, each kept or struck by the owner at the release. A de-SPAC and a spin-off are never listed, and the ingest refuses an entry whose predecessor was ever classified `spac`. Every later entry (a candidate the owner accepts after `chains-821`) goes through a named release of its own with the same steps, so `chain_price_break` is always read before a row lands.

*The row.* `successor_chains` (schema version +1 at claim time): `predecessor_id`, `successor_id`, `evidence_accession`, `evidence_form`, `known_at`, `ingested_at`, `source` (`edgar`), `provenance` (`filing`), `retracted` (#859's pattern); one live row per successor. The EDGAR ingest's master pass writes one row per list entry when three things are known at the run: the evidence in the filing index under the successor's CIK with form `8-K12B`, `8-K12B/A`, `8-K12G3` or `8-K12G3/A`; the successor's `securities` row (its first issuer filing under req 3; `master.issuer_forms` does not list 8-K12B, so BlackRock's row of 2024-10-11 follows its 8-K12B of 2024-10-01); and the successor's earliest-known listing row naming the predecessor's last ticker and exchange, matched against the predecessor's last listing row. **`known_at` is the latest `known_at` of those rows** (the evidence filing, the successor's `securities` row, the two listing rows), never the config's edit or the run's clock: the chain is known when its last piece of evidence was, so a read before that sees two securities as today. The listing row is usually the last (Bunge's cover page of 2024-02-22, three months after its 2023-11-13 hand-over; ExxonMobil's snapshot row of 2026-10-03), and that is the point-in-time cost: the chained history is the successor's from then, not from the hand-over. A hole bar rule 9 fetches for the successor keeps req 5's stamp (its session's close) but is the successor's bar only because the chain says so: `prices_as_of` and `adjusted_prices_as_of` (and every read built on them) hide a successor bar on a session before the successor's first listing row's `valid_from` until a live chain naming that successor has `known_at <= t`, so no read before the chain sees it, a chain whose `known_at` moves later (`chains_moved`) hides them again on its own, and the truncation harness checks the read like any other. An entry whose evidence is not in the index or is another form, whose predecessor is `spac`, or whose successor has no such listing row yet writes nothing and is named on the run row under `chains_refused` with the reason (a missing listing row is retried every run, as the master pass rebuilds); the run row also counts `chains_written`. The row is append-only like the master tables, and a chain is **underived** when no entry derives a row with its `known_at`. Two cases. When the entry stands but its evidence rows were restamped (T166c), replaced (T184h) or retracted so that the derived `known_at` moves, the master pass retracts the live row and writes the newly derived one in the same run (#1566's same-run refresh; the retraction row's `known_at` is the run's clock, #859), so a chain never reads from a stamp its evidence no longer carries; the run row counts `chains_moved`. When no entry derives the row at all (the entry removed), only `master-retract` proposes it in its dry run and retracts it with `--apply`, as for a listing, inside a named release. `chain_known_at_ge_successor_rows` names a row whose stamp lags its evidence until the next EDGAR run moves it. **Candidates.** The EDGAR run row counts and names `chain_candidates` over the full-history index req 3 scans every run: every 8-K12B or 8-K12G3 filer whose first listing row names a ticker and exchange that another company's security listed within `master.chain_window_sessions` (default 65) sessions before it, that is on no entry and not dismissed; the next BlackRock is seen the day its listing row arrives. **Health**, store-only and over raw rows (never through the chained reads): a `successor_chains` block listing the live chains with the hand-over day and the close ratio across it from the stored bars; `--check` rules `chain_ids_distinct_ciks`, `chain_known_at_le_ingested_at`, `chain_known_at_ge_successor_rows` (the row's `known_at` is at or after the successor's `securities` row's and its first listing row's), `chain_successor_listed_under_predecessor_ticker` and `chain_predecessor_no_bar_after_handover` (no predecessor bar on or after the successor's first bar); and a warning, never a failure, `chain_price_break`, when the ratio is above `master.chain_max_price_ratio` (default 1.15) or below its inverse, with the latest EDGAR run's `chain_candidates` beside it. The dashboard page is not touched (store first); its block can follow when wanted.

*Reads.* At `t`, with a live chain whose `known_at <= t`, the as-of reads keyed by security (`prices_as_of`, `adjusted_prices_as_of`, `live_actions_as_of`, `price_jumps_as_of`, `dropped_dividends_as_of`, `facts_as_of`) return every row of the predecessor under the successor's id and none under the predecessor's: a query for the predecessor's id returns nothing, one for the successor's returns both histories, and a key the two share (a bar on one session; a fact on one date and class member; none on the count) is the successor's own row. A predecessor fact dated on or before the hand-over (the successor's first listing row's `valid_from`) is always the successor's. A later one is the successor's only while no fact of the successor's own is known at `t` (the cut-off is the successor fact's `known_at`, not its date): a report the two registrants file together after the hand-over (ExxonMobil's 2026-08-03 10-Q, one accession under both CIKs, whose share count the store holds under the old CIK) fills that window, while a predecessor that keeps filing as a subsidiary with a nominal count is cut off by the successor's first own fact, and rule 7's ratio guard (#845) rejects the nominal count before that. Health's `successor_chains` block lists every predecessor fact dated after the hand-over that a read returns under the successor, so the owner sees the subsidiary case. A config entry keyed by a security id and date (`universe.accepted_price_jumps`, `universe.accepted_shares_facts`, `universe.accepted_same_day_pairs`) that names a chained predecessor applies to its re-keyed rows: the reads' helper maps the entry as it maps the rows; `alpaca.accepted_relistings`, a bare id list the resolver reads, is mapped by the resolver under rule 9 the same way. `securities_as_of`, `listings_as_of`, the delistings and the classifications are unchanged: the predecessor stays a security that rule 2 excludes as delisted, and the successor's own rows are judged as today. `statement_facts_as_of` (keyed by CIK) is not chained here (open question 36). The direct bar and action queries in `execution/report.py`, `execution/check.py` and `health.py` are deliberately unchanged: they read by the ids a book holds, and a chain row is never written while an open book holds its predecessor (the `held` refusal, below). So from the day the chain is known, rule 6 sees the successor with the predecessor's bars and rule 7 with its share facts; a trial whose cutoff is after the chain's `known_at` reads one security, one before it reads two.

*The resolver (rule 9).* When a chain is known at the run, the successor's first span under the predecessor's ticker starts on the predecessor's span end under rule 7 (its Form 25's effective day, else the day after its `end_session`) where that is earlier than the successor's first listing row: the sessions between, which today resolve to nobody (Bunge 2023-11-13 to 2023-12-19, Ferguson 2024-08-12 to 2024-09-16, Zymeworks 2022-10-24 to 2022-11-07), are the successor's, and `--fill-holes` (#876) lists them as the successor's holes and fetches them with item 1's `asof` request for the successor. Where the predecessor's span runs to the successor's row (Xerox, BlackRock) nothing moves. The predecessor's own span, rules 6 to 8 and the #1314 contract are unchanged; on the count no predecessor's bars run past the hand-over, and where one day they do, `chain_predecessor_no_bar_after_handover` fails until the owner's #1314 item 3 repair. `repair-resolution` deletes nothing for a chain (no bar changes id; the rule only assigns empty sessions); its dry run lists each chain with its ratio. The run row counts the spans started early this way.

*A held position.* A backtest may hold the predecessor from a rebalance whose read precedes the chain's `known_at` and step next on a read that sees it; the predecessor then has no bar and the delisting exit would fail. The engine's rule: at the first step whose read sees the chain (a `chains(t)` read on the data provider), the held positions, the plan's targets and the book's `held_on` and `marked_at` keys are mapped through it at one point, before the marking ids are built: a held predecessor becomes the successor one share for one share, a predecessor target becomes a successor target with the same weight, and a re-keyed dividend is held from the predecessor's `held_on`; the mark is unchanged (the successor's bars now cover the predecessor's sessions), no trade is booked on the name, and `n_chained_positions` counts it on the rebalance row. The paper path gets no code of its own: the ingest's chain writer is the gate. It refuses an entry, with reason `held` under `chains_refused`, while any open paper book holds the predecessor id (any journal row of an open window naming the id: an order, an adjustment, a daily position or a lot, `master-retract`'s `[traded]` test limited to open windows, so an order in flight counts as held), on the release's ingest and on every later run alike (a retried entry, or one the owner adds after a candidate), so a row lands only when no book holds the predecessor; the owner closes the position or strikes the entry. After the row no book can buy the predecessor, which has no bars. A trial logged before the release is reproducible only from its export (the data-release rule). `successor_chains` is a trial fact table (`data_vintage`'s explicit list), so a chain row stales every registered trial whose cutoff is at or after its `known_at` (2019-11-06 for Xerox) and leaves earlier ones current, a moved row stales them the same way from its new `known_at`, and a `master-retract` of a chain, which happens only inside a named release, moves the vintage through that release's `after` record. The paper windows open since 2026-10-09 hold neither BLK nor XOM; the release writes no row with an in-window `known_at` on an in-window session (BlackRock's is 2024-11-06, ExxonMobil's 2026-10-03), but `universe_as_of` recomputed at any in-window session after those stamps admits the two names where the books did not: an in-window change to the recomputation, the kind #1613's question 27 weighs (open question 37).

*No look-ahead.* `known_at` is the latest of the four evidence rows' stamps; the reads apply a chain only when `known_at <= t`; truncation invariance holds around it (req 12's harness); the fixture universe (req 13) gains a 1:1 pair (an 8-K12B by the new CIK, a Form 25 on the old, a 20-session hole, bars and share facts on both, a dividend on the old, a predecessor share fact dated after the hand-over and a successor fact known later, the new CIK's first listing row known three sessions after its first bar and after the 8-K12B; and, as separate cases, a pair whose evidence filing is the latest stamp (an `8-K12B/A` accepted after the listing row) and one whose `securities` row is) and a spin-off pair (no 8-K12B, the old security listed on, the new one's first close well below the old one's last) that no entry names.

- [ ] Rows (T187): on the fixture universe an entry for the 1:1 pair writes one `successor_chains` row whose `known_at` is the listing row's and whose `ingested_at` is the run's clock, and the two other pairs' rows carry the evidence filing's and the `securities` row's stamp (the latest of the four in each case); a trial with a cutoff at or after the row's `known_at` reads stale after the write and one before it current; the fixture listing row restamped later makes the row underived, retracted and rewritten at the new stamp in one run; the same entry with the evidence accession of a plain 8-K, with the spin-off pair's ids, with the predecessor reclassified `spac`, or while an open paper book on the store holds the predecessor id (`held`), writes nothing and names the reason on the run row, and the `held` entry is written on the first run after the book no longer holds it; before the listing row is known the entry is `chains_refused` and written on the next run; a second ingest writes no second row; the run row counts `chains_written` and `chains_refused`; a `<cik>@<date>` id, a `keep_successors` id, a same-CIK pair, a malformed accession and an id reused across entries each refuse the config naming the entry; truncation invariance around the row's `known_at`.
- [ ] Reads and resolver (T187b): at `t` before the chain's `known_at` the six reads return the pair's rows under their own ids and `universe_as_of` excludes the successor by rule 6; at and after it, a read for the successor returns both histories in session order with the dividend adjusted in `adjusted_prices_as_of`, a read for the predecessor returns nothing, an `accepted_price_jumps` entry naming the predecessor still excuses its jump under the successor, `facts_as_of` returns the predecessor's facts under the successor, `universe_as_of` admits the successor as soon as the chained history satisfies rule 6, and the spin-off pair reads as two securities throughout; a backtest on the fixture that holds the predecessor at a rebalance before the `known_at` and steps past it renames the position to the successor with its equity unchanged and counts `n_chained_positions`, where before this change the step raised; a predecessor held and kept as a target across the `known_at` shows no trade on the name at the next fill and its re-keyed dividend is still counted; the resolver with the chain gives the successor the hole sessions and `--fill-holes` lists them as the successor's, without it they resolve to nobody as today, and a filled hole bar is absent from `prices_as_of` for the successor at any `t` before the chain's `known_at` and present from it, and absent again before the new `known_at` once the fixture's listing row is restamped later; a predecessor fact dated after the hand-over reads under the successor at a `t` before the successor's first own fact is known and not from that fact's `known_at`, with truncation invariance around it; `repair-resolution --dry-run` lists the chain with its ratio and proposes no deletion; the pinned H1 and TWIN fingerprints are unchanged (the fixture's new names stay outside their universes, or the task says which pin moved and why); truncation invariance of the reads and of `universe_as_of` around the `known_at`.
- [ ] Health, retract and candidates (T187c): `health --check` passes on the fixture store, fails each of the five rules on an injected violation naming the ids, and warns `chain_price_break` on an injected ratio; the run row names the 1:1 pair as a candidate while no entry names it and not once it is dismissed, and never the spin-off pair; `master-retract`'s dry run proposes the row once the entry is removed and `--apply` retracts it.
- [ ] Release (T187d, owner): no `held` refusal on the run row (no open book holds a predecessor id); the `before` and `after` records; the entries kept with the evidence table on the PR, the struck ones dismissed; the ingest that writes the rows, `--fill-holes` for the rule-9 sessions, `health --check` green with the block listing every chain, no `chain_price_break` and no candidate; the stale trials listed and rerun per the runbook.

*Open questions:* 36 to 38 in the list below (29 to 31 when written; renumbered after #1550's 29 and #1682's 30 to 35).

**Amendment 2026-10-12 (#1550): a missed ingest day is caught up, and a hole fails `health`.** On 2026-10-08 the 18:30 ET Alpaca run failed (the SIP 15-minute cap, since fixed by #1356 and #1357), and no later run refetched that session: `ingest_session` fetched only its own expected session. Universe rule 6 (a bar on every XNYS session of `universe.min_history_months`) then failed every name, `universe_as_of(close(2026-10-09))` returned 0 members (1,008 at close(2026-10-07)), and `health --check` said nothing, since req 10's staleness reads the reference symbol at S−1 only. The owner repaired the store by hand on 2026-10-11 (backup `tradepartner.pre-backfill-1008.duckdb`, then `ingest --source alpaca --backfill --since 2026-10-08`: 5,499 rows; 1,007 members at close(2026-10-09)). Any one-day outage or a sleeping Mac would recreate the hole and keep it for a year, and the Daily paper book's next plan would have been all cash with no error. The issue's three parts, and where each is written: **(a)** and **(c)** are issue fixes merged before this amendment, recorded here so the spec says what the code does; **(b)**, the paper run's refusal to plan on a collapsed universe, is the [paper spec](paper-trading.md)'s amendment of the same date (before its Requirements) and its plan task T188.

*(a) The daily catch-up (#1572, PR #1578; req 9 as amended).* A daily Alpaca run reads the sessions `prices_daily` holds at or before its expected session (`ingest._stored_sessions`) and the XNYS sessions after the store's first stored bar and before the expected session that it lacks (`_missed_sessions`; the calendar's sessions, never weekdays). It fetches every missed session beside the expected one, in contiguous runs so no request spans a stored session (`_fetch_runs`), each bar stamped `bar_known_at` (its session's close) as every other bar is, so no caught-up bar is a look-ahead; each request still ends under `alpaca.sip_delay_minutes`; a name delisted inside the gap is fetched for the missed sessions, never the expected one; a caught-up session brings its month of corporate actions. The run message counts them (`catch-up: none`, or `catch-up: 2 sessions (…)`), so every run row shows the state of the hole. The run is `stale`, writes only its run row and changes no fact row when the store lacks more than `alpaca.max_catchup_sessions` sessions (default 5; `0` catches nothing up, so any missed session fails), naming the sessions and the `tradepartner ingest --source alpaca --backfill --since <first missed session>` command that clears them (the month chunks of req 9 are the tool for a longer lapse), and when a caught-up session comes back thin (`_check_caught_up`: without the reference symbol's bar, or with more than `ingest.max_missing_share` of the names counted at the expected session, and holding a bar there, missing), since a thin session would count as stored and never be fetched again. Req 10's dark and stale-listing checks stay at the expected session; a caught-up session's remaining per-name holes are the backfill's `--fill-holes`, not this path's. A store with no prices at all, and one complete through the previous session, catch nothing up. The property: **a daily run never reports `ok` with a whole-session hole left behind it** (a per-name hole inside a stored session is the backfill's `--fill-holes`, as above, and the health rule's share threshold is (c)'s). The EDGAR side needs no counterpart: the filing index is scanned over full history on every run (req 3), so a missed day leaves no master hole.

*(c) The health rule (#1575, PR #1579; req 11).* The `session_holes` rule and `health.min_session_rows_share` are the acceptance line under Ingest above (`health --check` fails naming `session_holes` …). Together, (a) keeps the hole from forming on the next run, and (c) names one that formed anyway (a lapse over the bound, a store restored from a backup) in every `health --check`, which the paper runbook runs before a `paper start`. Neither can see a universe that collapsed for another reason; that is (b)'s job.

Acceptance (testable), ticked by #1578's `tests/test_ingest_catchup.py` (each case fails with the catch-up removed, shown on that PR's merge base):
- [x] Catch-up (#1572): a fixture store with sessions D−3 and D−1 and no D−2: the daily run for D ends `ok` with D−2 and D stored, each bar's `known_at` its session's close, the message naming one caught-up session; a gap over `alpaca.max_catchup_sessions` is `stale`, names the sessions and the backfill command, and stores no bar for D; a gap exactly at the bound is caught up; a complete store fetches no extra session; a caught-up session that comes back thin is `stale`; a caught-up session brings its month of corporate actions; a name delisted inside the gap gets its missed bars and none for D.

*Open question:* 29 below.

**Amendment 2026-10-14 (#1682, [ADR 0022](../decisions/0022-no-vendor-security-master.md)): no vendor; the free index answer key as a standing check; the identity defects fixed root cause by root cause.** The owner decided on #1682 on 2026-10-14 (~17:45 ET): **no vendor.** T144 (the USD 69 Sharadar overlap test) is not run, T145 records "no vendor" (ADR 0022, written with this amendment), and the identity source stays SEC filings plus Alpaca. In place of a vendor, the free S&P 500 membership history becomes a standing `health` check, and the causes the answer-key check found are fixed **root cause by root cause, batched and reviewed, largest lever first**, each behind a read-only census and an agreed before/after diff before any store-wide change. No new framework, no reconciliation engine (rejected on #1303), no per-name rule: a defect is a rule with a census, never a one-off patch. **No row's `known_at` moves under this amendment**: nothing here re-stamps a stored row earlier (the two censuses below showed why that would be look-ahead), and the one store repair outside a re-derive is a reviewed delete of rows that were never true. This amendment supersedes the vendor-conditional parts of the 2026-10-08 (#1319) amendment: T146 to T150 are not built.

*The answer key (read-only, 2026-10-14; the full table is in the owner's research library, `2026-10-14-sp500-answer-key-check.md`; the counts on #1682).* The S&P 500 membership history from [fja05680/sp500](https://github.com/fja05680/sp500) at commit `a2430f2a` (2026-09-07) against `universe_as_of` on the owner's store (schema 28) at the 86 month-ends 2019-08 to 2026-09, with OpenFIGI's current security type as a one-off cross-check: 43,325 member-months; 2,496 missed (5.76%, 205 tickers), of which 264 by design (new issues under twelve months, size, price, liquidity, a pending Form 25) and **2,232 (5.2%, about 176 names) for data or config reasons**, about 19 S&P names a month still wrongly out in 2024 to 2026. One cause per miss, so every count is a lower bound. By lever, in the order the plan fixes them (twelve buckets; the twelfth is two months of MRNA on a rule-6 price jump, by design): (F) shares facts 525 months, 18 names (SPG `stale_shares` 86, HRL `ambiguous_shares` 85, BF.B and MKC `no_shares` on the second class, SCHW, WBD, FOXA, IBKR, TAP, ARES, PWR, AMCR, CTVA, LIN, VTRS, SW, PSKY); (C2) listing rows whose `known_at` is 2026-10-03 while their `valid_from` is years earlier, 468 months, 90 names (NI 86, AEP 66, COP 51, ABT 36, PEP 23, and 85 names at six or fewer); (E2) bars missing inside the twelve-month history window, a rename not followed or a hole, 270 months, 20 names (VMC 33, BIIB 24, PEAK, BBWI, EXE, FI, STX, WBD, NLOK, ARNC, J, RTX/UTX, HWM, JEC, IR, SYMC, PARA, RVTY); (C1, C3) no listing row at all or a listing that starts at the first cover-page ticker, 265 months (FITB 86 and MMC 77; PG 38 before its first row of 2022-10-19, BAX, WBA, WRK, CPB, BRK.B, EXPD, HES, HST, NEM, NLSN, WLTW and twelve more whose first row is between 2019-09 and 2020-03); (H) a common class classified otherwise, 183 months, 4 names (IVZ and CRH `depositary` by the F-6 rule, GRMN and BG `unclassifiable` on "Registered Shares"); (E1) a share class's bars starting at its first cover page instead of its first trade, 144 months, 16 class ids (BF.B, BRK.B, DISCK, PG's `:common-stock`, GOOG, MKC, NWS, TAP, UA, VIAC/CBS/VIAB, IR/TT, TFC/BBT); (B) a note on the primary id, 98 months, 2 names (VZ 84: `VZ22A`'s row and the note's Form 25 end the common; PG 22: `PG25` typed `debt`); (config) CBOE on `CBOE_BZX`, 86 months, 1 name; (A) universe rule 2 reading the newest listing's exchange when it is `NYSETX` or `NYSE_CHICAGO`, 75 months, 10 names (DHI, ICE, HAL, GL, IBM, MMM, and four more, from 2025-07); (D) a successor CIK not chained, 46 months, 6 names (BLK, XRX, BG, XOM, APA, APTV); (C6) FDIC filers, 60 months, 2 names (FRC, SBNY: no EDGAR registrant, claim TP-4); (G) a ticker the key names that no listing row carries and OpenFIGI names another company for, 10 months, 6 names. Outside the index the same causes keep about 9 of the top 500 names by dollar volume and about 27 of the top 1,000 out each month, so the key is a lower bound on the universe's holes, not a bound on them.

*The check (plan T189, T189b): the index list as a second input to `health`'s `coverage_reference` block (amendment 2026-10-11, #1541, item 3).* **It is a check only.** S&P membership is itself a selection made by a committee after the fact, with look-ahead and survivorship in it; the list is therefore never a universe, a signal, a filter or a feature: no module under `src/` outside `health.py` imports its loader or opens its file, no SQL or path string names it elsewhere (a source scan, not only an AST test), `universe_as_of` and every backtest, paper and research read are byte-identical with the file present, absent and emptied, and no trial can read it (the plan's look-ahead test pins all of it). Once the owner sets its threshold the block *gates*: `execution/shakedown.py`'s E.7 requires the health report to be ok, so a set `coverage.index_max_missing` can hold the live shakedown gate; that is a gate on the store's quality, not a selection, and it is intended. The list is one committed file, `src/tradepartner/store/index_members.csv` (the `dividend_reviews.csv` shape: a reviewed list with a pure loader), one row per `(ticker, from, to)` membership interval, derived by the loader module's pure `derive_intervals` from the dataset's historical-components file at a pinned commit; its header carries the source, the commit SHA (`a2430f2af0c79ddf0748e91de11bdeb1616ab5a7`, "2026-09-07 update"), the licence (MIT, copyright 2019-2020 Farrell J. Aultman: the notice is the licence's one condition, and it is kept in the file), the upstream provenance the dataset's README states (an original list from Andreas Clenow's *Trading Evolved*, changes since 2019 from Wikipedia's S&P 500 page, checked by the maintainer every few months) and the derivation date; T189's PR quotes that provenance. The file is committed, not gitignored, because an evidence number must be reproducible from the repo at the SHA that produced it. A refresh is a size-S PR that moves the SHA and regenerates the file; `health` prints the SHA it checked against, so an evidence line always names its key. For each sample session S (the block's existing `coverage.sample_months` from `coverage.sample_from`; an evidence run sets all twelve months from 2019-08-01, the key's 86 month-ends, and T189b's PR states the time per session on the fixture store, since the nightly `--check` must stay cheap), each interval covering S is resolved to a security through the store's non-retracted `listings` rows at `t`, by ticker (normalised as the check normalised them: case, dots, slashes, spaces; `BRK.B` and `BRKB` are one ticker), preferring a row covering S, then a row of the ticker live inside the interval, never a row of another span (a reused ticker's old holder is not the key's name); **the resolved `security_id` a member of `universe_as_of(close(S))` is a hit** (by security, not by CIK, so a second class's own miss counts: BRK.B, GOOG, BF.B, MKC); a miss carries its bucket, the universe's exclusion `rule_name:reason` for the resolved security (`exchange:not_listed`, `exchange:delisted`, `exchange:NYSETX`, `history:missing_bars`, `shares:stale_shares`, `security_type:depositary`, …), or `unresolved` when no listing row carries the ticker (the G names, the FDIC filers, and a name the community dataset keys by a later ticker: its known limit, never dropped silently). The finer root-cause buckets of the answer-key table (A to H) were a hand diagnosis over those reasons and the store's rows; the block reports the reasons and the names under each, which is what the next census starts from. **Count rule:** misses are reported with their bucket and their names; the block fails `health --check` only when `coverage.index_max_missing` is set and the miss count on some sample session exceeds it; the key is unset (report only) until the owner sets it (**open question 30**). The block also reports the hit share per sample session, so the two releases below have one before/after number each. Nothing here changes `coverage.reference_names` or its rule.

*The OpenFIGI cross-check (T189c): a second check, on types, never a source; the orchestrator's addition of 2026-10-14, kept small.* In the answer-key check, OpenFIGI's current `securityType` confirmed levers B (VZ and PG are "Common Stock" while our primary id reads a note) and H (IVZ, GRMN, BG and CRH are common), and called 93 of the 351 non-index data failures common; it is the one free outside view on *what kind of instrument* a ticker is, and a dated record of it is what the next census keys on. The owner holds a key (`OPENFIGI_API_KEY`, in `.env`; `.env.example` gains the commented line). One ingest source, `openfigi` (`ingest --source openfigi` only; never under `--source all`): for every security with a listing on a `universe.exchanges` exchange live at the run and a bar in the last `universe.min_history_months` window (the universe-size set, about two thousand names), one mapping job by its current ticker (`idType: TICKER`, `exchCode: US`, the undotted form from `alpaca.class_symbols` where one exists), batched at the service's limits, which the config states and the client enforces: with a key **25 requests per 6 seconds and 100 jobs per request** (about twenty requests for the set); without one 25 requests per minute and 10 jobs per request (the no-key fallback: the same run, about two hundred requests over eight minutes, so it still completes; the numbers are config keys `openfigi.requests_per_window`, `window_seconds`, `jobs_per_request`, with the keyed and keyless defaults pinned from the service's documentation at build time and quoted in T189c's PR). The response is stored in a new additive table `openfigi_types(security_id, ticker, figi, composite_figi, share_class_figi, security_type, security_type2, market_sector, name, exch_code, known_at, ingested_at, provenance, source)`, **one row per security per change** of the mapped fields (the `borrow_status` rule: rows record change, the run row records the observation), `known_at` the clock reading after the response, so a current lookup is never written as past knowledge, and a schema version taken at claim time (T185b's "next free" rule). `health` gains a `figi_types` block: the latest stored row per security against the latest classification and the primary listing's title at `t`: a security our store types `common` that OpenFIGI calls a note, bond, preferred, warrant, unit or depositary receipt, and the converse (ours `depositary`, `unclassifiable` or `debt` on a class OpenFIGI calls "Common Stock" or "REIT"), each named with both types, plus the unmapped count; a report, no `--check` rule until the owner sets `openfigi.max_disagreements` (open question 30's second half), and then a gate like the index block's. **Check only, like the index list:** the string `openfigi_types` appears under `src/` only in `schema.py`, `ingest.py`'s `openfigi` branch, the adapter and `health.py` (a source scan), the trial data-vintage rule leaves the table out as it leaves `borrow_status` and `symbol_changes` out (T171b), and the same byte-identity cases as the index list hold with the table populated and empty. No page card and no `--source all` wiring: the block is read from `health` and the first real run is a step of T198's evidence if T189c is merged by then; T189c gates nothing. OpenFIGI's terms (free API, attribution, no redistribution of bulk data) are read and quoted in the PR before the first request; the stored rows are derived counts and per-name types for our own check, never exported. A FIGI is stored because it is the one stable outside identifier a later census can key on; nothing in this amendment keys a security on it.

*The fixes: the rule for every lever.* Each build task's first step is a **read-only census** on the owner's store (read-only, never a copy; the #1681 census method: the pure rule re-run on exported frames or cached inputs against the stored rows), posted on the task's issue before any code: the names and months the key attributes to the lever, the rows the rule would move, and the **before/after diff** the owner agrees to: membership at the key's 86 month-ends, the bars and rows touched, the registered trials that go stale. The owner's agreement to that diff is the gate before the build PR is opened (not enforced by `team.py`); a census that finds the lever is something else re-files the line by amendment, never by widening the task. No build task writes to the store: a rule lands behind a switch (an existing one, or T190d's new `edgar.class_member_link`) or as a pure read-time rule, and the store changes only in the two named releases below. **Read-time rules and registered trials.** A frozen key is a config value a hypothesis freezes (`FROZEN_SECTIONS`); a rule's logic is code, and every trial row records the code version it ran under (`registry.code_version`: commit and dirty flag), so a trial run before and after T190 or T196 is told apart by its commit. Neither moves a data vintage, so no trial is marked stale by them; the registered sweeps are rerun once after `rederive-1682` (open question 28's shape), and the release's `after` record names the read-time merges in its `reason`. A tolerance for missing bars in rule 6 is different in kind: it is a number, which would be a new frozen key and a new world for every family (open question 34). The levers and their rules, in lever order:

1. **Shares facts (F; T190, T190b, T190d).** Census: **Codex census 2 on #1682** (`codex: census 2 (shares)`; the 525-row evidence file is `~/tradepartner-probes/research-library/2026-10-14-census-2.csv`, owner-side). It found four mechanisms, not three: **per-class absent, 159 months, 3 names** (BF.B, FOX, MKC: the second class's count is stated in the filing under a class member the master never linked to that class; the fix is T190d's ingest-side link at each fact's acceptance: a stored shares fact's `class_member` is linked to the class whose title or slug known at that acceptance names it, never the cover cache; `edgar.class_member_overrides` repairs DKS, TR and VMEO one by one today and becomes a special case of the rule or stays as it is, whichever the census shows honest, the PR saying why); **ambiguous class rows, 165 months, 5 names** (ARES, HRL, PWR, SCHW, TAP: a nonvoting or exchangeable member beside the listed common on one `as_of_date`; the rule: the member linked to the listed class is that class's, equal values collapse, and only two different unlinked values stay `ambiguous_shares`); **a tiny pre-trading seed, 59 months, 7 names** (AMCR, CTVA, FOXA, LIN, PSKY, SW, VTRS: a first fact of 1, 100, 1,000, 13,001 or 25,000 shares dated before the class traded makes #845's ratio rule reject every later true fact; a plain defect that gets its own small line, T190b, with the failing test first: a fact dated before the security's first listing `valid_from`, or before its first bar, each known at or before `t`, is no baseline); and **extraction coverage, 142 months, 3 names** (IBKR, SPG, WBD: the latest stored fact is from 2009 to 2022 while every later 10-Q and 10-K states the count on its cover; the per-period fact is dropped by `ingest.fact_rows` as `unmatched` for these filers, so the fix is the ingest-side per-class link, T190d, behind `edgar.class_member_link`: rule (a) a lettered member goes to the sole unlettered listed common class and rule (b) a class unseen on `edgar.dead_class_filings` consecutive periodic cover pages stops being a candidate; no parser change). Outside the index the same mechanisms hold 41 names and 565 months (per-class mapping 7 names / 100 months, class disambiguation 23 / 195, fresh-fact extraction 11 / 270). Every rule reads only evidence **known at or before `t`**: a member link learned from a later cover page, an explaining corporate action or security row known after `t`, never moves a read at `t` (the look-ahead cases of T190's test).
2. **Listing rows known 2026-10-03 (C2): no re-stamp; T184h is the fix.** Census: **Codex census 1 on #1682** (the `codex: blocked` comment and the C2 finding after it; the draft file `2026-10-14-census-1-draft.csv`, accepted as it stands). 462 of the 468 member-months (86 names) are **`snapshot_static` rows**: `MasterBuild.snapshot` and `_snapshot_listing` write today's ticker and exchange back-dated to the listing start with `known_at` = the companies-snapshot fetch (2026-10-03 22:16:55 ET, inside EDGAR backfill run `1d946422…`); no filing stands behind them by construction. The other six are three same-day `filing` months (ABMD, COO, FBHS: the row's acceptance is the month-end itself, by design) and three ZBH months where the key's ticker resolved to a Zions preferred id (a mapping artefact of the check, fixed by T189's resolution rule). AEP shows why a re-stamp would be look-ahead: its static row is `AEP/NASDAQ` with `valid_from` 1994-05-16; its 2018 10-K says the common traded on **NYSE**, and an 8-K of 2020-09-15 moved it to NASDAQ from 2020-10-01; 14 of its 66 misses precede the move. **The property:** a `snapshot_static` row's exchange and `valid_from` are today's venue back-dated and are evidence for nothing before the row's `known_at`; no command re-stamps a snapshot row, moves its `known_at`, or writes a filing-family row from its values; the dated venue spans come only from each filing's own text, at that filing's acceptance. **The fix is a filing-text listing source, and it is T184h's family, un-deferred:** T184 to T184g (merged, behind `edgar.filename_listings`, default `false`) read a 10-K's instance file prefix for the ticker and its Section 12(b) text for the exchange, one verdict per 10-K since 2015, each written as a `filing_name` row known at that 10-K's acceptance; the 86 names are 10-K filers whose FY2018 10-Ks were accepted early in 2019, before the key's first month-end. T184h (its release) is therefore the C2 fix and runs after this amendment, with the key's 86 names added to its evidence. What it does not cover, stated so no evidence line expects it: a venue move dated by an 8-K between two 10-Ks (AEP from 2020-10-01 reads NYSE until its FY2020 10-K's acceptance in 2021: later-known, honest, and both venues are listed exchanges so membership holds; the 8-K Item 3.01 reading is not built); a second class (a file name has one prefix; item 4's T193); and a name whose 2019-and-later cover pages are still unread (NI: item 4). A `filing_name` row is its own key (`provenance`, its own `valid_from`), so the ingest's "Filed rows" rule never re-stamps it from the snapshot row, and T198's dry run has a stop rule: no `filing_name` row of T184h's release changes `known_at`.
