3. **Bars missing in the history window and renames not followed (E2; T192, T192b).** Census, step 0 of T192: for each of the 20 names and each missed month, the sessions with no bar in the twelve-month window (count, first, last), whether a `symbol_changes` row (schema 28, Alpaca's name changes from 2020-06-01) or a listing row sits within `alpaca.rename_lead_days` of the gap's start (a rename), whether the sessions are already in `--fill-holes`'s hole list, and the overlap with the 26 #1549 resolver-drift securities. **The rule:** the resolver takes a stored `symbol_changes` row known at `t` as the span boundary when the master's listing rows lag it (the old symbol's span ends the session before `process_date`, the new symbol's starts there, under the same security; today the lead runs only from the new ticker's first cover-page row backward by `rename_lead_days`); plain holes need no rule and go to release 2's fill-holes step. A tolerance for a few missing bars in rule 6 is not built (**open question 34**). *Census (on #1696, read-only on the 2026-10-14 copy, which holds `symbol_changes` only from 2026-07): the follow restores 29 of the 270 member-months (RVTY) once T172d stores the changes and the fill runs, and the fill alone 27 (PEAK, BBWI). PARA's 42 were a contested span, not a lag: Paramount's `PARA` span had ended (2025-08-17) before another company renamed into `PARA` (2026-08), so the contested term (#735) is made time-aware in T192 (above), which restores PARA 42, STI 4 and IR 3. A followed change is refused when the rules then deny its security the new ticker on its day, or when it takes a session of either ticker from a security the listings alone give it to (a sibling class already listing the new ticker). RVTY's 29, and EXE's and WBD's months, wait for T172d's backfill of `symbol_changes` from 2020-06 (T198's gate names it). Of the remaining 141, 129 are gaps inside a month with stored bars, which `--fill-holes` does not list (T192b), and 12 are STX's whole months that it calls no hole, their cause found in T192b's census. BIIB's 24 (two all-day halts, 2020-11-06 and 2023-06-09, no bar exists) are a residual under open question 34.*
4. **No listing row, or a listing that starts at the first cover ticker (C1, C3; T193, and T184h).** Census, step 0 of T193: the cover-page context shape of NI, FITB, MMC and the 24 C3 names at their first periodic filing of 2019 and at the filing that first yielded a row (plain, exchange axis, split over contexts, or absent), the 2020 P&G, Abbott and Fifth Third cases #1651 found, and the NiSource shape #1675 broke on. **The rule:** the split-context reading (`edgar.cover_split_contexts`, on `main` behind its switch, default `false`, backed out of the store by #1683) joins the title, symbol and exchange facts of one class only inside one class-axis member, or inside one undimensioned context; a fact in a context with another member, or an equity-unit, note or warrant title, is another class and never joins (#1675's done-when is this line's test). **T184h is the C3 fix for a primary class** before its first cover page (item 2) and T193 the fix from 2019 on for a class whose cover shape is still dropped, and for a second class (NWS, FOX class B). T184h is not superseded; it runs after this amendment with the key's names in its evidence.
5. **A common class classified otherwise (H; T194).** Census, step 0 of T194: every class the rule below moves, with its title, forms, bars and the key's months. **The rule:** a class whose latest cover-page title is plain common (rule 6's `_is_plain_common`) is `title_common` even when its CIK has filed an F-6 (classification rule 3, sticky today: CRH's ordinary shares, listed on NYSE directly since 2023-09, read `depositary` for good; IVZ the same) or files foreign forms (rule 4); the F-6 rule types a class whose title names depositary shares or receipts, or a class with no titled listing, as before; funds (rule 2) and SPACs (rule 5) keep precedence. "Registered Shares" (GRMN, BG) and "Ordinary Shares" are common-equity titles (`_COMMON_WORDS`, whole words under `edgar.title_fallback`). The rows reach past sessions through T182's `--rebuild-classifications` in release 2 (each row at the instant its title became known, never earlier).
6. **A share class's bars from its first trade (E1; T195).** Census, step 0 of T195: the 16 class ids' first bar, the CIK's first session, the months the key loses, and, for every session the lead would add, which class of the same CIK the symbol belonged to then (Under Armour's `UA` was its Class A until 2016-12 and its Class C after; FOX/FOXA and VIAC/CBS have the same shape) and whether another company held it. **The rule:** the first-span lead (#974, #1408) of a class id (`<cik>:<slug>`) reaches back to the CIK's first session (the primary class's), not to the class row's own `known_at` (its first cover page, 2019 for every pre-2019 class), bounded by Alpaca's own first bar for the symbol under `asof`, refused by the existing collision probe when the symbol named another company then, and **refused on every session on which the symbol belonged to another class of the same CIK** (a same-CIK ticker swap, named on the census issue); the bars are fetched in release 2's fill-holes step.
7. **A note, coupon or unit on the primary id (B; T191).** The v6 build already derives only the common on these primaries (VZ, PG, EXPE, EXC: the #1681 census, part B); what remains is stored: `PG25` unretracted on P&G's primary, and `VZ22A`'s row plus the note's Form 25 (a `delistings` row on the primary id) ending Verizon's common since 2020-06, with the retractions #859 stamps at the run, which no as-of read before 2026-10-13 sees. A retraction at the false row's own `known_at` cannot be stored: it would share the listings UNIQUE key and `known_at` with the row it retracts, and `delistings` has no `retracted` column (`RETRACTABLE_TABLES` is `securities` and `listings`). **The rule is the #1613 one:** a stored master row that was never right at any instant (a note's listing or delisting on the common's id) has no `known_at` at which it was true, so it is **deleted under a named release** from a reviewed manifest (`table, security_id, the row's key columns, known_at, accession`), the backup being the history; the manifest names, for each row, the filing that produced it, accepted **no later than the row's `known_at`** (normally the same accession), whose text shows the instrument was never the common, so the delete corrects a misreading of what was public, never applies knowledge learned later; the command refuses a row whose accession is not the security's CIK's or was accepted after the row's `known_at`, reuses `ingest.restamp_from_manifest`'s `_crosses` open-window guard (#1522) with the delete's span running from the row's `known_at` to infinity (a delete has no end date), writes nothing on a dry run, and lands with the run row and the counts. After the delete the common's listing is no longer ended by the note's Form 25; the census shows that Form 25 landing in `unmatched` on the next derive (#1163's title match never reads "2.375% Notes due 2022" as plain common) and T197's step reads it so, and T198's dry run has a stop rule: no deleted key reappears. A delete-only repair may leave the vintage unmoved (the runbook's "delete-only" paragraph), so the sweeps that read the touched sessions are rerun by that rule. The alternatives are **open question 35**. No builder rule changes here: #1680's first-class guard is parked with #1681, whose census moved 22 SPAC-era commons (22,289 bars on slug ids) onto their primaries as a side effect; those CIKs are left as they are, and the parked rule gets its own census if it is ever re-opened.
8. **CBOE on `CBOE_BZX` (config).** One name. `universe.exchanges` is a frozen key, so a change is a new world for every registered family; a config decision for the owner, **open question 32**, no task.
9. **Exchange choice (A; T196).** Census, step 0 of T196: every security at a key month-end whose latest-`valid_from` listing row is on an exchange off `universe.exchanges` while an older row of the same security on a listed exchange is live (not ended by `listing_ends_as_of`), listing every newer-row exchange value it would pass, and the converse (an older listed-exchange row ended by its own Form 25: a true transfer, which stays excluded). **The rule:** rule 2 passes when any listing row of the security live at S is on a listed exchange **and the newer row's exchange is one of the master's exchange values other than `OTC`** (a dual listing on NYSE Texas or NYSE Chicago is an addition, not a transfer, and the cover page names both; a newer `OTC` row with a live older NYSE row and no matched Form 25 is a name that left the exchange, and it stays excluded as today); the ticker read for bars stays the latest row's; #1574's same-day tie rule is unchanged. A pure read-time rule: no release; the evidence line carries the diff and the rerun follows the paragraph above.
10. **Successor CIKs (D).** The design is PR #1631's (the #821 amendment: an owner-reviewed `master.chains` list, `successor_chains` rows at the evidence's acceptance, chained as-of reads, resolver rule 9, health; T187 to T187d, release `chains-821`), parked with `hold` until the owner's answer to open question 33; the key adds XRX (2019), BG (2023-11) and APA/APTV to its evidence names. The owner answered **open question 33** (a): it is un-parked as written and `chains-821` runs as its own release after `rederive-1682`; this amendment adds no line of its own.
11. **FDIC filers (C6)** are disclosed (claim TP-4), not built. **G** is reported as `unresolved` and not built.

*Releases (the owner's T197 and T198; [data-releases.md](../runbooks/data-releases.md)).* Two named releases, in lever order, each one sitting with the jobs stopped, a backup, the dry runs, `health --check` exit 0 and the index block before and after: **`restate-1682`** (T197: item 7's reviewed delete from T191's manifest), then **`rederive-1682`** (T198: item 4's switch flip with the FSN and cover cache versions bumped, the re-derive, T182's classification rebuild for item 5, T190d's switch flip with the facts re-ingest under the same bump, then `ingest --source alpaca --backfill --fill-holes` with `asof` for items 3 and 6; T184h's filename rows share the sitting when both are ready, as spec open question 22 allowed for T181, with one `FSN_VERSION` bump). Items 1, 9 and T190b are read-time rules and need no release; item 10 is `chains-821`. Both releases stale every registered trial store-wide, once each, and the sweeps rerun as open question 28 says. **The open paper windows.** Window 3 (book `main`, first rebalance 2026-10-30) and window 4 (book `daily`, first rebalance 2026-10-15) opened on 2026-10-14 as a machinery shakedown with the universe gaps known; their returns are not strategy evidence. Runbook rule 5 and ADR 0016 point 7 forbid a release that touches a session inside an open window, and **both releases and both read-time merges change what an in-window read returns**: the delete un-ends Verizon's listing at every later session; T194's rebuild changes the current classification CRH and IVZ carry; T193's flip adds rows live now; the fill-holes backfill runs through the in-window sessions; T190 and T196 change `universe_as_of` at every session from the merge on. So the windows cannot stay open through a sitting as the rule stands (and T191's command refuses to run across an open window's read instants). The owner's call, **open question 31**; the recommendation keeps paper dark for hours, not weeks: abandon both windows at the start of each sitting and restart them the same day after the `after` record, merge T190b and T196 on a sitting day while the windows are abandoned (T190 already landed, #1703, on the owner's word while the paper windows were open; the spec open question 31 option (b) exception, recorded on #1682 on 2026-10-16), and batch the two sittings as close as the censuses allow.

*Lean.* Seventeen lines in the plan (three for the checks, twelve build tasks sliced by file, two owner releases) and one deferred release un-deferred (T184h), no new framework, one new additive table (the OpenFIGI rows, so a lookup is dated), no per-name list added (one replaced: `class_member_overrides` by its rule), no row re-stamped, every rule behind a switch (an existing one, or T190d's new `edgar.class_member_link`) or a read-time function, every census a gate. The answer-key scratch scripts are not kept: the loader and `health --coverage` replace them.

*Acceptance (testable; the plan's T189 to T199 carry the files).*
- [ ] The check (T189, T189b): on a fixture store, an interval covering S whose ticker resolves to a security absent from `universe_as_of(close(S))` is a miss with that security's exclusion `rule_name:reason`; a second class's miss counts while its primary is a member; an interval no listing row carries, or whose ticker's only rows lie outside the interval, is `unresolved`; an interval not covering S is not checked; `BRK.B` and `BRKB` resolve to one security; the block reports the pinned SHA and the hit share per session; `health --check` never fails on the block while `coverage.index_max_missing` is unset, and fails `index_members_missing` only when it is set and exceeded on some session; a source scan of `src/` fails on any import of `store.index_members` or any string naming its file outside `health.py` and the loader; `universe_as_of`, a fixture backtest run and a fixture paper run are byte-identical with the file present, absent and emptied.
- [ ] OpenFIGI (T189c): with a recorded fixture response, `ingest --source openfigi` writes one `openfigi_types` row per mapped security with `known_at` after the request's send time and before `ingested_at`, a second run with the same response writes no row, a changed `security_type` writes one; the client never exceeds `jobs_per_request` jobs or `requests_per_window` requests per window (a fake clock counts); without a key the keyless defaults apply and the run completes; `--source all` never runs it; the `health` block names a `common` class OpenFIGI calls a note and a `depositary` class it calls "Common Stock", and reports with no failure while `openfigi.max_disagreements` is unset; `data_vintage` at any cutoff is unchanged by the rows; a source scan of `src/` fails on the string `openfigi_types` outside `schema.py`, `ingest.py`, the adapter and `health.py`; `universe_as_of`, a fixture backtest run and a fixture paper run are byte-identical with the table populated and empty; a recorded response is scrubbed of the key (the fixture-scrub test).
- [ ] C2 (T184h's evidence, with this amendment's done-test; the plan's T199): on a fixture in AEP's shape (a `snapshot_static` NASDAQ row with `valid_from` 1994 known at the fetch; a 2019 10-K whose instance prefix is `aep` and whose 12(b) text names NYSE; a 2021 10-K naming NASDAQ), reads at `t` before the fetch see no listing before the 2019 acceptance, NYSE from it and NASDAQ from the 2021 acceptance, never NASDAQ before 2021; the static row's `known_at`, `valid_from` and exchange are byte-identical after the release; a look-ahead test fails if any writer path uses a `snapshot_static` row's exchange or `valid_from` as evidence for a row known before that snapshot's `known_at`.
- [ ] Shares (T190, T190b, T190d): on a dual-class fixture whose stored facts carry class members and whose classes carry titles, each class reads its own shares and neither is `ambiguous_shares`; a nonvoting member beside the listed common selects the common; two member rows on one class with equal values collapse and with different values stay ambiguous; a link learned from a cover page known after `t` leaves the read at `t` unchanged; (T190b) a seed fact of 100 shares dated before the class's first listing is no baseline and the next true fact is accepted, failing on the merge-base; an explaining action or security row known after `t` leaves the read at `t` as `stale_shares`; (T190d) an IBKR-shaped cover whose shares fact the census says `ingest.fact_rows` drops yields the fact on the class the member links under `edgar.class_member_link`, the DKS, TR and VMEO fixture cases pass with `class_member_overrides` empty or kept, the PR saying why, and every row is byte-identical with the switch off; every case fails on the merge-base.
- [ ] Deletes (T191): the command refuses a manifest row whose accession is not the security's CIK's, was accepted after the row's `known_at`, or names a table other than `listings` and `delistings`; refuses to run while an open paper window's read instants are crossed; a dry run writes nothing; after the run the note's listing and delisting rows are gone at every `t`, the common's listing is not ended at any `t`, the next derive on the fixture lands the note's Form 25 in `unmatched`, and the run row names the release and the counts; the look-ahead suite shows no read at `t` sees a row whose evidence was accepted after `t`.
- [ ] Resolver (T192, T195): a `symbol_changes` row known at `t` dates the span boundary of a rename whose cover-page row lags it and the old symbol's bars stop the session before `process_date`; a change known after `t` bounds nothing at `t`; a class id's first span leads back to the CIK's first session and stops at Alpaca's first bar for the symbol; a symbol another company held before the class existed is refused by the collision probe; a session on which the symbol belonged to another class of the same CIK is refused; `alpaca.first_span_lead=false` leaves every mapping as before.
- [ ] Split contexts (T193): NiSource's 2021-05-05 cover (Common Stock `NI` and Equity Units `NIMC`) parses to two classes with `NIMC` never on the common's member, in both parsers with the switch on; the P&G, Fifth Third and Abbott 2020 split-context cases still yield their common; with the switch off every parse is byte-identical to today's.
- [ ] Classification (T194): a CRH-shaped issuer (an F-6 on file, a later titled "Ordinary Shares" class) reads `depositary` before the titled page's acceptance and `common` from it; "Registered Shares" is `title_common`; an ADS-titled class of the same CIK stays `depositary`; a fund and a SPAC keep precedence; fails on the merge-base.
- [ ] Rule 2 (T196): a security with a live NYSE row and a newer `NYSETX` row passes rule 2 and reads the newer row's ticker; one whose NYSE row ended by its Form 25 before the newer row is `exchange:NYSETX`; one with a live NYSE row, a newer `OTC` row and no Form 25 stays `exchange:OTC`; #1574's tie cases are unchanged.
- [ ] Releases (T197, T198, owner): the evidence lines in the plan, including the index block's miss count by bucket on every month-end of the key before and after each release, the membership diff the census promised against the diff the release made, zero rows written outside the manifests and the named steps, and no `filing_name` or `snapshot_static` row's `known_at` changed.

*Open questions:* 30 to 35 below (numbered after #1550's 29; PR #1631's 29 to 31 are renumbered 36 to 38).

**Amendment 2026-10-18 (#1731, [ADR 0023](../decisions/0023-break-first-fast-books-paper-exam.md)): the freeze after `rederive-1682`, the accept rule, the data-release id, and the universe for forward books.** Four sentences, each the owner's decision of 2026-10-18 recorded where the store's rules live; the code is the [backtest spec's amendment of this date](backtest.md#amendment-2026-10-18-1731-adr-0023-fast-strategies-open-fills-cost-terms-hold-mode-and-the-band-cash-accrual-reported-alpha-spin-offs-the-scale-budget-universerank_by) and the [plan](../plans/data-foundation.md)'s T212 to T214.

1. **The accept rule (D1; closes #969).** A defect in the master, the bars or the actions is fixed when it is a correctness class (a rule bug, a look-ahead risk, a holdout-integrity fault) or touches more than 0.5 % of in-sample universe-months; every other defect is **accepted residue**, listed in this spec's Risks and cited by every backtest report. After `rederive-1682` the residue is: the FDIC filers (TP-4), the 22 SPAC-era CIKs, the unresolved index tickers, and the 1:1 chain names (BLK, XOM) until T187d.
2. **One release, then the freeze (D5, D8).** `rederive-1682` (T198) runs as planned, in one sitting with the #1382 restamp store step and `repair-dividends` (T186c). After it the master's rules, the schema (unless a trading need) and the process scripts are **frozen**: no new health rule, census or identity fix unless a running book or a kill test needs one (the pause list is the plan's). The staled trials are replayed only for H1 and for a kill-ladder rung-4 survivor.
3. **The data-release id (D8; T214, optional).** At that release the frozen `master`, `gap` and `adjust` sections of a hypothesis's canonical set are replaced by one key, `data.release_id`, the named release the store is at (the `data_release` decision kind), as the backtest amendment specifies; a data fix after it stales a trial only by moving that id. If T198 and T214 cannot share the sitting, this item lapses.
4. **The universe for forward books (D4; T212).** `universe.rank_by` (backtest amendment) makes rule 7's shares fact optional: under `dollar_volume` the universe at `t` keeps rules 1 to 6 and the floors, ranks by median dollar volume from the bars alone, and is intersected with Alpaca's tradable assets as of `t` (the T171 snapshot rows) **only from the first snapshot with `known_at ≤ t`**: before it the intersection is skipped and the trial's rebalance row says so (`assets_intersection_skipped`), never today's list read into the past, so a historical kill-filter run and the forward book's tracking trial apply one rule and agree; a forward book is right today without a release; `cap`, the default, is byte-identical to today, and every registered family keeps it. This store's identity work therefore serves history and the historical kill filter; a forward exam's universe does not wait on it.

*Open question:* 39 below.

## Risks & domain checks

- **Look-ahead:** adapters stamp `known_at`; the as-of API filters; truncation invariance with probes around every `known_at` proves agreement; the broken adapter proves each check has teeth; revisions are never back-dated. Two accepted deviations are decided at the run from later knowledge and bounded by counts: the price-to-security mapping (the resolution amendment's "Look-ahead" paragraph) and, from amendment 2026-10-12 (#1597), whether a pre-2019 `filing_name` listing row exists at all (its confirmation against the security's later readings; it only removes names, counted `refused_*`); neither moves a row's `known_at`, and the truncation harness cannot see either. Amendment 2026-10-14 (#1682) adds a third, also bounded by a count: the reviewed delete of master rows that were never true at any instant (a note's listing or delisting on the common's id) under `restate-1682`, each row named in the manifest with the filing accepted no later than its `known_at` that shows it, so a read at any `t` afterwards returns what a correct reading of the filings public at `t` would have; the harness cannot see a deleted row, and the release's backup is the history. The same amendment re-stamps nothing: a `snapshot_static` row is never evidence for a date before its `known_at`.
- **Survivorship:** securities exist from their first filing, so delisted names are in the denominator; the gap is bounded and shown; it gates the holdout in Phase 3.
- **Adjusted-price rewrites:** raw storage plus read-time adjustment; revised actions get `ingested_at`.
- **Level-rule timing:** raw close at T and ex-date ≤ T, so a known-but-future split cannot distort cap or the price floor.
- **Ticker reuse and class confusion:** master keyed by `security_id`; delistings matched by class title and exchange; price rows mapped to securities by the #735 resolver rules (equity classes only, same-day pairs and same-start ties unassigned, a later class of one company never takes a ticker its sibling holds) and the #793 registrant check (a co-registrant or unproven claim never takes a live holder's ticker), every unassigned row counted on the run row.
- **Secrets:** pydantic settings; scrub test; fixtures pattern-checked.
- **Terms and opens:** the owner's T3 research resolves Alpaca terms, auction vs IEX open, fractional-at-open behavior, actions depth, history depth, assets-endpoint reuse, EDGAR rate limit and User-Agent, backfill runtime; it sets `liquidity_rule_enabled`, `execution.fill_price`, `max_missing_share`.
- **Single writer:** chunked commits release the lock; readers are short-lived; page has a busy state.
- **Statement facts (#660):** the look-ahead a fundamentals signal usually carries is a *restated* number standing in for the as-filed one; the first-vintage UNIQUE makes that impossible at the schema, and the hold and late counts make the one order-of-arrival hole visible instead of silent. The data-quality risk is heterogeneity: a tag list that is too short loses coverage (counted on `statement_none`), one that is too long mixes concepts, so the lists are config and every row names its `xbrl_tag`. Volume: about 10^7 records across the universe, so the ingest streams and bulk-inserts per CIK; T78 records the run time. Rollout: the switch is off until T78, so no merged task changes the backfill.
- **Rates (#1498):** the look-ahead a rate series carries is a value stamped on the day it describes rather than the day it was published (the one-day H.15 posting lag), which the vintage rule and the `--check` recomputation of every `known_at` make impossible to store; a row the rule would stamp after the run's clock is skipped and counted, never clamped. The terms risk (FRED's storage clause against its personal-use licence) is the ADR's; the key travels only as a query parameter the raw client never logs and the scrub covers. Volume: about 2,700 rows and one request a backfill, one a day after.
- **Costs:** none (the FRED API is free with a registered key; ADR 0009's USD 0 holds). **Order path:** fake broker only. **LLM inputs:** none.

## Open questions

1. **Pre-2019 ticker ranges** remain `snapshot_static`. Because such rows count only from their fetch time (#35), a name listed before 2019 is absent from `universe_as_of` until a filing supplies its ticker and exchange, so coverage early in the backfill window is thin and skewed by when each company first filed a tagged cover page. A research brief on 8-K Item 5.03/3.03 parsing is the candidate if Phase 3 needs better; not here. Amendment #974 (the first-span lead, in the resolution amendment) supplies such a name's bars before that filing, so rule 6 passes once the filing is known; membership before the filing is unchanged. *Answered by amendment 2026-10-12 (#1597): the name's own 10-K file names and a Nasdaq Form 25's exhibit name are filing-time evidence, known at acceptance, and supply the rows; the 8-K Item 5.03/3.03 brief is not needed.*
2. **Backfill window**: if T3's estimate exceeds one overnight run, `--since` defaults to the last 3 years and the plan says so. `--since` limits bars, actions and facts only; the filing index and Forms 25/25-NSE are always full history.
3. **`edgartools` cover-page parsing**: if it proves unstable, fall back to parsing the iXBRL `dei` tags with the standard library and pin the approach in T11.

Open owner questions from the 2026-10-03 amendment (#660); none blocks T76 to T77c, each is a config change or a follow-up issue once answered:

4. **Restated values as revisions.** The table keeps first vintages only and counts restatements. If a hypothesis ever needs "the latest value known at T" rather than "the value as first filed", should restatements go into a separate `statement_facts_revisions` table (new rows at their own acceptance, never touching the vintage), or stay counted only? Default: counted only.
5. **IFRS filers.** 20-F and 40-F filers tag `ifrs-full:Revenue` and relatives, which no list carries, so they count on `statement_none`. Should an IFRS fallback list be added (a config change plus a re-ingest) once T78 reports how many universe names it costs? Default: no, until the count says otherwise.
6. **Non-periodic carriers.** `edgar.statement_forms` excludes S-1, S-4 and 8-K, so an IPO's pre-listing years are first seen in its first 10-Q or 10-K (flagged `comparative`), and an 8-K recast never becomes a vintage. Should S-1 and S-4 be admitted so the pre-IPO years are the registration statement's? Default: no; a registration statement's XBRL is optional and its periods are re-reported in the first 10-K.
7. **Reporting currency.** A 10-K filer whose statements are in another currency is skipped and counted (`statement_non_usd`). Store those rows with their `unit` instead, leaving conversion to the consumer? Default: skip, until T78's count says it matters.
8. **`InventoryNet` and other names.** Decision (b) leaves inventory out. The owner may add it, or any further concept, to `edgar.statement_tags` when a registered hypothesis needs it; the cost is one re-ingest with `--bulk-from-cache`. Is there a name the first profitability hypothesis will need that is not on the list (e.g. `us-gaap:OperatingIncomeLoss`, `us-gaap:NetIncomeLoss` for a cash-based variant)?
9. **Bulk reuse as the default.** `--bulk-from-cache` is a flag; the nightly re-download stays the default. Should a maximum zip age (`edgar.bulk_max_age_days`) replace the flag so a daily run never re-downloads 1 GB for one day's filings? Default: the flag, until the daily runtime is measured.

Open owner question from the 2026-10-04 amendment (#859):

10. **`master_underived` as a table.** Health reads only the store, so each master check records its underived set in this new non-fact table (`run_id`, tz-aware UTC `recorded_at`, the row's key and `known_at`), outside every as-of and fact read path. Keep it, or report only the count in the EDGAR run message? Built as a table; the owner decides whether it stays.

Open owner questions from the 2026-10-09 amendment (#1358, filing events); none blocks T164b to T164d, and 11 is its own issue:

11. **The stamps clock (#1382).** T164's count found that the SEC's submissions data serves, in October 2026, `acceptanceDateTime` values moved by New York's UTC offset (4 h on EDT dates, 5 h on EST dates) in both directions for a changing set of CIK members: late in the 2026-10-08 bulk zip (against the SGML header for 2 accessions and the 2026-09-25 API fixtures for 2,560) and in the per-CIK API by 2026-10-09; early in at least 34 CIKs' stamps written from the 2026-10-01 bulk zip (103 2.02 8-Ks provably, by the filing-date rule), with the early share of the bulk-stamped events estimated at an eighth to a sixth from the hour signature (the amendment's "The clock" paragraph; the evidence is on the T164 PR). `filing_events` takes the stamp as it is, like every filing-derived row, so a moved stamp is a moved `known_at` here too, and an early one is a look-ahead at `close(T)`. The rule is fixed by the amendment: `edgar.filing_events_enabled` stays `false` and no trial reads the table until this issue is closed. What the owner decides on #1382: how to find the moved stamps (the filing-date detector first; the SGML header, one request per filing, as the only per-filing truth; the per-CIK hour signature in between) and whether to re-stamp them (a repair of every filing-derived `known_at` of those CIKs, with the trial-reproducibility consequence of a `statement_facts` rebuild, and a registry note on which logged H1/B3 trials to re-run), how to guard stamps written now (both stamping paths would write moved values today), and whether to wait for the SEC to settle. Recommendation: decide before T164e's real-store run; T164b to T164d do not depend on it.
12. **8-K/A in `edgar.event_forms`.** The default stores both forms and a hypothesis filters at read time (B9 reads `8-K` only, its B9-6). Should the store keep `8-K` alone? Default: both, so a later signal on amendments costs no re-ingest. Note the `--check` rule `form in edgar.event_forms`: narrowing the list later fails health on the stored `8-K/A` rows until a `--rebuild-filing-events`, so the change and the rebuild go together.
13. **Item codes before 2004-08-23.** EDGAR numbered 8-K items differently before then (results of operations were Item 12), and `items` is stored verbatim, so a consumer reading `2.02` sees no event before that date. Should `filing_events_as_of` translate old codes? Default: no; every registered hypothesis reads from 2016 or later.

Open owner questions from the 2026-10-11 amendment (#1498, rates); neither blocks T169 to T170b:

14. **A second series.** `rates.series` takes a list so a financing or hedging model can add one later (`DGS3MO`, the constant-maturity yield, or `DGS1MO`). Should the backfill fetch any beside `DTB3` now, while the request is written? Default: `DTB3` only; a series is a config change plus one `ingest --source fred --backfill`, no code.
15. **The engine's cash rate.** `backtest.cash_rate` is a frozen key the engine refuses when non-zero. Should a later task let it read the same table (interest on the strategy's cash, as a bill would pay), or does cash stay at zero by convention (ADR 0005 measures against benchmarks)? Default: zero, until a hypothesis file asks; it is a backtest spec amendment of its own, not this one.

Open owner questions from the 2026-10-11 amendment (#1506, borrow status and the Alpaca spin-off and name-change actions; questions 14 and 15, and requirement 18, are the #1498 amendment's, written beside this one in PR #1499); each is recommended one way below and the plan builds the recommendation, so none blocks T171 or T172; a different answer is a config change or one line in the task, except 19, which is the confirmation the paper path needs before T172b is claimed:

16. **Rows per change or rows per day.** The amendment stores a `borrow_status` row only when an asset's fields change (about 33.6k rows once, then the daily churn), with the `ingestion_runs` row as the record that a day was observed and `observed_at` on every read. The alternative, one row per asset per day, is simpler to read and about 8.4 million rows a year. Recommendation: per change.
17. **Keep the raw assets payload.** The daily run stores eleven fields; the payload carries more (`maintenance_margin_requirement`, `margin_requirement_short`, `attributes`). Keeping each day's gzipped payload (about 1.7 MB a day) under `borrow.raw_dir` would let a later task add a field with no loss of history. Recommendation: yes, default `data/alpaca_assets/` beside the store (gitignored), and T171c writes it; the owner may set the key empty to turn it off.
18. **The spin-off and name-change stamp.** #1506 says "process date after the close"; the amendment uses the spec's existing date-only rule (req 5, `announcement_date_known_at`): the close of the first session **strictly after** `process_date`, one session later than the issue's phrase, because a date carries no time to show the record was public by that day's close, and it stays checkable (`--check` recomputes it on `symbol_changes` and bounds it on `spinoff` rows). A spin-off is applied to no adjusted series, so the stamp changes no price; it delays only when a paper residue can be explained by it: until the session after Alpaca's `process_date`, which can itself be weeks after the ex-date, and `window.start` keeps refusing such a residue until then, which is safe. Recommendation: the date-only rule, as written.
19. **The T64 convention, and the confirmation the paper path needs.** A `spinoff` row carries the parent's `security_id` in `source_action_id`, so Alpaca's own action id is not kept for spin-offs and the row's identity is (child, parent). The alternative is a `related_security_id` column (a DDL change and a `window.py` change). With real rows, `window.start` will accept a spin-off child position it refuses today and journal a `spinoff_receipt` that `exits.py` sells whole, with Alpaca's ratio and the resolver's parent; the `window.py` docstring asks the owner to confirm the convention before that happens. Recommendation: confirm the convention now (no DDL; #473 and `window.py` already read it); T172b and T172c carry safety-reviewer for it.
20. **The actions backfill's request.** The backfill asks Alpaca for `spin_offs` and `name_changes` with no `symbols` filter, one request per month (the corpact probe: 11 requests for 9 band scans), and resolves every record through the store resolver. The alternative is the daily path's per-symbol batches, about thirty requests a month over the listed set. The two methods have not been compared on the same month, which is why coverage before mid-2020 is recorded as unknown and T172d compares one month. Recommendation: symbol-less.
21. **Which key fetches the assets list.** The daily run uses the data key pair through the existing `TradingClient(paper=True)` that the fixture recorder already uses for `GET /v2/assets/{symbol}`; the scratch recorder used the `main` paper book's key. The list is assumed the same and T171d checks it once. Recommendation: the data pair, so no new secret and no paper book's key leaves the execution path.

Open owner questions from the 2026-10-07 amendment (#1199, stale listings), listed in the [ADR 0003 amendment](../decisions/0003-data-adapters-local-first.md#amendment-2026-10-07-1199-the-stale-listing-rule) and answered on #1199 before its step 2 is claimed: how the frozen key `gap.stale_listing_sessions` treats H1's registration (`FROZEN_KEY_DEFAULTS` entry off, 63, or the key unfrozen; the ADR recommends 63); whether a listing with no bar at all stays `no_bar_at_t` (proposed: yes); whether `trial_rebalances` stores the stale count (proposed: not now).

Open owner questions from the 2026-10-12 amendment (#1597, pre-2019 listings from filing-time evidence); each is recommended one way and the plan's T184 to T184h build the recommendation, so none blocks a build task; 22 is the gate T184h waits on:

22. **Order against the `cover-listings-1541` release and the open paper windows.** This release writes rows known from 2015 on and bars from 2016 on, so `universe_as_of` changes on every session since 2017, inside the open paper windows as #1541's question 2 describes, and every registered trial goes stale once. Recommendation: run it after T181, in its own sitting, under whatever the owner answered on #1541's questions 2 and 3; if T181 has not run when T184h is ready, the two land in one sitting with one `FSN_VERSION` bump and one FSN re-extract, so the trials go stale once and the windows restart once.
23. **Single-reading rows.** An NYSE-family delister with no `dei:TradingSymbol` and no cover page has one reading, its prefix. (a) Write and count, and let the backfill's collision count and the deny-list judge it by its `asof` payload (recommended: the probe's NYSE delisters were 10 of 10 right, and refusing them leaves the 2016-2018 universe biased toward Nasdaq exits); (b) refuse (`single_reading` rows are never written).
24. **Renames read from the prefix.** A prefix that differs from the cover page but is followed by a prefix that equals it is kept as a rename (Herc's `HTZ`). (a) Keep and count `renamed`, with the release dry run listing every such name for the owner's eye (recommended: the count is small, and the alternative loses the renamed name's months before the rename); (b) refuse unless the rename is also dated by a `symbol_changes` row or the filing's own `dei:TradingSymbol`.

Open owner questions from the 2026-10-12 amendment (#1613, one cash dividend stored twice); each is recommended one way and T186 to T186c build the recommendation, so none blocks a build task; 27 and 28 are answered on #1613 before T186c's sitting:

25. **The three unresolved groups.** Seagate 2021-05-11 $0.67 touches one member-month; Alpaca no longer returns either id, so the raw-record comparison is closed, and only the issuer's record (one quarterly dividend declared that month, or two) settles it. Recommendation: leave it `unresolved` and reported until a PR with the issuer's release moves it to `duplicate`, applied by a release of its own. The two `0001372807` groups have no listing at their ex-dates (a listing gap of #1597's kind), so they wait for the listing.
26. **Ingest after the repair.** A retracted id is not remembered, so a later Alpaca payload that carries it again (a `--fill-holes` backfill that re-asks a month, as T184h's step 5 does for the names it newly spans; a daily run does not reach 2019 to 2026-09) recreates the duplicate, which the health rule fails the next morning as a third identity, or, after the release, reports as pending (two identities on a listed key again): the morning read compares the pending count with the deferred count the `after` record names, and a rise is a returned id. Paper reconciliation reads the same live rows (`reconcile_run._dividends` sums every live dividend row a book held through the ex-date), so a duplicate inside a window doubles the cash it expects before any check runs: a follow-up issue, not this amendment. The alternative is an ingest-side deny-list of retracted ids (the `edgar.filename_listings_refused` shape, one more config key). Recommendation: not now; the check is the measure, and the list is added only if a group returns.
27. **The open paper windows' lookbacks.** Eight listed rows (SGHC 2026-02-02 to NCDL 2026-09-30) lie inside the formation lookback of decisions in the windows open since 2026-10-09, so deleting them can move a recomputed in-window rank (the amendment's "open paper windows" paragraph). (a) `--to`: delete everything before the earliest formation start of any in-window decision now, defer the eight (pending in health) to a release of their own once those windows close (recommended: no new tooling beyond one option, the eight are small names on a paper book, and the exam stays a test of the book); (b) a read-only pre-check on a scratch copy of the repaired store that recomputes every in-window paper run's targets and shows them unchanged, as rule 5's #1497 exception does (`precheck.<release>.json`), deleting all if so: more work and a store copy on a tight disk; (c) delete all now and annotate each open window's tracking check with the release: the residue is then the release's, not the book's, and the exam loses its meaning for those months.
28. **When the sweeps rerun.** The touched sessions span nearly every registered sweep, so the runbook's delete-only paragraph means `sweep run` plus `--rerun` on each, every rerun counting in N, and T184h (`pre2019-listings-1597`) stales every trial store-wide too; run back to back, the reruns double. Recommendation: run this release as soon as T186 and T186b are merged (the store first), rerun no sweep until T184h's sitting if that sitting is within the same week, and rerun once after it, as question 22 does for T181 and T184h; otherwise rerun now. H1's holdout trial 4 is never rerun: `pre-sweep-20261008` stays the record of what it read, and the holdout is not spent again.
29. **The catch-up bound and a long lapse** (amendment 2026-10-12, #1550). With `alpaca.max_catchup_sessions=5`, a lapse of more than a week (a Mac asleep over travel; a vendor outage) fails every daily run until the owner runs the named backfill, and every `paper run` in between is `stale` at its step 2 with its rebalance pending until the catch-up window lapses it to `missed`. Options: (a) keep 5 and the owner's backfill (recommended: a lapse that long is an owner event, the backfill's month chunks and `--fill-holes` are the right tool, and the `health --check` failure plus the daily `stale` alert say exactly what to run); (b) raise the bound to the shakedown's N = 10 sessions, so a two-week lapse heals by itself; (c) have the scheduler's wrapper run the backfill on a `stale` run that names one. The bound is a live key, so the owner can move it without a window stop.

Open owner questions from the 2026-10-14 amendment (#1682, no vendor; the answer key and the root-cause fixes); each is recommended one way and the plan's T189 to T198 build the recommendation unless the owner records another choice on #1682 before the task is claimed; 31 and 35 are answered before T197's sitting:

30. **The thresholds for the two checks.** `coverage.index_max_missing` (the index block) and `openfigi.max_disagreements` (the types block) are unset, so both report and neither fails `health --check`; once set, each is a gate the shakedown's E.7 reads. Recommendation: leave both unset through `restate-1682` and `rederive-1682`, then set each to the residual the second release leaves (the by-design misses, G, the FDIC filers, the unmapped) plus a margin of five, so a regression of six names on one session fails the next morning and a one-name flicker does not.
31. **The open paper windows (3 and 4) and the releases.** Both releases and the read-time merges (T190 landed, #1703; T190b, T196) change what an in-window read returns (the releases paragraph), runbook rule 5 forbids a release across an open window, and T191's command refuses to run across one; both windows are a machinery shakedown whose returns are not evidence. (a) Abandon both windows at the start of each sitting and restart them the same day after the `after` record (`paper start` for each book; the daily book's window is cheap, and window 3's first rebalance, 2026-10-30, falls after both sittings if they land by then); merge the read-time rules on a sitting day while the windows are abandoned; batch the two sittings as close as the censuses allow (recommended: paper is dark for hours, the shakedown span restarts on the corrected store, and nothing in ADR 0016 changes); (b) for the read-time merges only, keep the windows open and record on each window and on #1682 that its recomputed half differs from its book, which is a rule-5 exception the owner records (an ADR 0016 point 7 note, as #1497 did for stamps), acceptable only because these windows are a shakedown, never for an exam window; (c) abandon both windows now and restart after `rederive-1682` (weeks of no paper: against the fast-paper direction of 2026-10-09).
32. **CBOE on `CBOE_BZX`.** One index name, 86 months, excluded by `universe.exchanges`, a frozen key: a change makes every registered family a child registration in a new world. (a) Leave the list and count the name as by design in the check (recommended: one name is not worth a world); (b) add `CBOE_BZX` to the list at the next frozen-key change the lab makes anyway, with an ADR 0006 note.
33. **PR #1631 (successor chains, #821).** The key's D lever is its design (BLK, XRX, BG, XOM, APA, APTV, 46 months; its own count 17 pairs, 55 months). (a) Un-park #1631 as written and let `chains-821` run as its own release after `rederive-1682` (recommended: the design is reviewed, the list is owner-kept, and this amendment adds only the key's names to its evidence); (b) keep it parked until the two releases above have landed and re-count.
34. **A tolerance for missing bars in rule 6.** E2's census (on #1696; the answer key's note: "tolerate few missing bars") shows that after T192, T192b and the fill the only residual is BIIB, 24 months from two all-day halts (2020-11-06, 2023-06-09); option (a) leaves BIIB out by design. A tolerance is a number, a new frozen key and a new world for every family. (a) No tolerance: holes are filled under `rederive-1682` and renames are followed by rule (recommended: a hole is a defect in the store, not in the rule); (b) a tolerance of up to N sessions, registered as a new frozen key with every family re-registered as a child.
35. **The never-true master rows (item 7).** #859 stamps every retraction at the run and never earlier; a retraction at the false row's own `known_at` cannot be stored (the UNIQUE key; no `retracted` on `delistings`). The B lever is two names and 98 months, and every month of it is a session a trial reads. (a) Delete the rows under `restate-1682` from T191's reviewed manifest, each with the filing accepted no later than the row's `known_at` that shows the instrument was never the common, the backup being the history and the delete-only rerun rule applying (recommended: the #1613 precedent, a row with no `known_at` at which it was true; the rule is narrow and the manifest is reviewed); (b) a schema change: `retracted` on `delistings` and an as-of tie-break that lets a retraction win at equal `known_at`, which adds `schema.py`, `store/delistings.py` and a migration to T191 for the same two names; (c) accept the residual, as #1541's question 4 (a) did, and let the check report VZ and PG until the windows close.

Open owner questions from the 2026-10-12 amendment (#821, successor chains; 29 to 31 when written on PR #1631, renumbered here after #1682's), each recommended one way:

36. **Statement facts across a chain.** `statement_facts_as_of` is keyed by CIK, so a B-family read of the successor starts at its first 10-K and the predecessor's revenue history is not read as its own. (a) Chain `statement_facts` the same way (CIK to CIK, no date bound) in T187b (recommended if a B-family hypothesis is live when T187b starts: one more read under one rule); (b) leave it, since no registered B-family trial reads a chained name today, and file it when one does.
37. **The open paper windows.** The release writes rows whose `known_at` (BlackRock 2024-11-06, ExxonMobil 2026-10-03) precedes the windows opened on 2026-10-09 and touches no stored row on an in-window session, so no past paper run read differently; but `universe_as_of` recomputed at any in-window session admits BlackRock and ExxonMobil where the books did not, so a backtest recomputed from the store over the window differs from the books: an in-window change to the recomputation, the kind runbook rule 5 guards and #1613's question 27 weighs. (a) Run the release now, under rule 5's exception wording (#1497), and annotate each open window's tracking check with the release and the two names, so the residue is the release's and not the book's (recommended: #1615 is the owner's ask that the books plan with the two names, and the annotation keeps the exam readable); (b) wait for the windows to close, as #1613's option (a) defers its eight rows.
38. **Mergers closed with a reorganisation.** Marvell (Inphi), nCino (SimpleNexus) and Aspen (Emerson) reorganised under a new CIK and issued shares the same day, so the old holder's series continues 1:1 but the share count does not; the three lose 52 universe-months today and no entry names them. (a) Keep them out (recommended: the definition is one share for one share, and an acquirer's series across a large merger is a judgement an index committee makes, not this store); (b) admit a pair on the owner's word with the share-count change recorded as evidence, under a second list.
39. **The accept rule's denominator** (added 2026-10-18, #1731). "0.5 % of in-sample universe-months" needs one definition the census scripts share. Options: (a) member-months of `universe_as_of` over H1's in-sample month-ends (2020-08-31 to 2023-12-29, about 41 × 1,000), the number the index answer key already reports against; (b) member-sessions over every registered in-sample window, which grows with every cadence. Recommendation: (a), one number every census can print; a defect is measured against the same 41 month-ends whatever the cadence of the strategy that found it.
